A dialog that carries fixed IDs (the message screenshot form: `#message_screenshot_start_id`, `#message_screenshot_end_id`) therefore existed twice when it was reopened inside that window, and `locator.fill` failed with a strict mode violation in "recovers after a failed capture" (`tests/message-screenshot.e2e.js`): a failed capture toasts after about 110ms. The test had passed before `e14b96c66` only by accident: `.popup[closing]` used `animation: pop-out var(--popup-animation-speed) ...`, and `--sb-transition-fast` is a duration plus an easing, so the shorthand was invalid at computed-value time and the dialog was removed in about 30ms.
  - **Guard:** `promptForMessageScreenshotRange` (`public/script.js`) awaits the previous screenshot dialog's `show()` before it builds the next one. A dialog with fixed IDs does the same, or waits on `.popup[closing]` leaving the document. Tests that reopen such a dialog wait for the old one to leave first.
  - **Tests:** `tests/message-screenshot.e2e.js` ("never holds two screenshot forms when reopened while the first dialog is closing", "recovers after a failed capture ...").
  - **`animation: 'none'` popups:** `runAfterAnimation()` also resolves on an `animationend` that bubbles up from the dialog's `::backdrop`. A popup with `popup--animation-none` has a 0ms backdrop fade (`--popup-animation-speed: 0ms` in `public/css/popup.css`), so Chromium and Firefox removed it within a frame, while the theme's `.popup[closing]` exit (200ms) ignores the class and WebKit does not report the end of the 0ms backdrop animation: there the dialog stayed 205 to 238ms. `Popup.#hide()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`) now removes such a popup at once, with no wait. The callers are the action loader (`public/scripts/action-loader.js`) and the Quick Image Gen prompt review. Test: `tests/popup-animation-none.e2e.js` (red in the WebKit config before the change, 226 and 238ms; Chromium passed before and after), which `tests/playwright.webkit.config.js` runs.
- **Adopted search inputs:** restore a caller's input to its original parent and sibling on page teardown, remove only frame-added classes and attributes, and unbind frame listeners. Reuse must retain one input callback and the shared Ctrl/Cmd+F and type-to-search behavior; Escape clears before closing only when `escapeClearsFirst` is opted in. Destroying active header-slot selection must restore the real title and controls without stealing focus from a closing dialog. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-dialog.test.js`; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`.
- **Opening layout:** a dialog showed its upstream layout for the length of the opening animation and then changed: a framed Popup showed labelled inputs and a leftover heading before the adapted rows, a destructive confirmation showed a blue OK that turned red, and a Window opened at one size and jumped to another. `Popup.show()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`) runs `onOpen` through `runAfterAnimation()` (`public/scripts/utils.js`), which waits for `animationend` or 500 ms, and the adapters changed the DOM there. With reduced motion `onOpen` runs in the same task as `show()`, so the jump does not show.
  - **Guard:** DOM, class, focus and layout changes of a Popup go in `onOpening(popup)`. `opened()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/popup-frame.js`) ends with it, in the same task as `showModal()`, after the frame is built and the default focus is set, through `runOpening()`. That function logs a throw, so the dialog still opens; calls `contentChanged()`, which measures the frame again; and finishes the CSS transitions the handler started: a class added after the style flush in `opened()` otherwise transitions, and OK faded from blue to red. Leave `onOpen` where it is, because `docs/dialogs.md` guarantees its timing to extensions. Load the list of a content-sized Window before it opens.
  - **Opening scale:** during the animation `getBoundingClientRect()` returns the size at scale 0.8. Measure with `offsetWidth`. A Popup Alert that measured rectangles kept long labels in a row with motion on and stacked them with reduced motion on. Code that has to read rectangles either stays in `onOpen`, as the extension row actions do, or divides the distance it takes from them by the scale, as the swipe picker does when it scrolls to the current swipe in `onOpening`; scrolling in `onOpen` showed the top of the list and then jumped.
  - **Measuring in `onOpening`:** the footer height reservation (`--sb-popup-response-height`) updates from a `ResizeObserver`, after `onOpening` ends. Code that measures the body after it changes the footer calls `popup.sbFrame.contentChanged()` first; otherwise the swipe picker's scroll position is off by the change in footer height.
  - **Height lock:** `bindWindowHeightLock()` records its first height in `opened()`, before `onOpening` runs, and only grows. `contentChanged()` calls `remeasure()`, or a content-sized sheet keeps the empty space that the adapter removed (60px on the phone Admin and Snapshots Windows). A page binds no height lock (see Short Windows and their pages), so there `contentChanged()` only lays the frame out again.
  - **Theme transitions:** Moonlit Echoes sets `transition: all 0.5s` on `.menu_button`, so `flex-grow` and `width` animate between the layouts of an Alert, and the buttons start narrow. A transition starts from the style before the change, so `layoutWithoutTransitions()` keeps transitions off until a forced layout has applied the final value. Both Popup Alerts and `openAlert()` use it.
  - **Tests:** `tests/dialog-opening-layout.e2e.js` compares the first frame with the settled size at 1440×900 and 390×844; `tests/swipe-picker.e2e.js` (`with motion on`) also checks the scroll position. `tests/utility-dialog-first-paint.e2e.js` and `tests/destructive-first-paint.e2e.js` compare every frame from the first with the settled dialog, also in the WebKit config; `tests/unit/popup-contract.test.js`; `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js`. The dialog test helpers default to reduced motion, so pass `MOTION` from `tests/helpers/dialog-opening.js` at boot; `page.emulateMedia()` after boot does not clear `body.reduced-motion`.
  - **Pitfall:** a spec with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` runs `onOpen` at once, so it cannot see this class of bug. The first frame can come after the animation when the main thread stalls, as WebKit does on the first layout of the Extensions list, so the helper does not require a frame inside the animation. In WebKit the first Popup of a page can open with no animation, so the first-paint specs open a throwaway Popup first.
  - **Open:** The character lorebook dialog in `public/script.js` sets up Select2 in `onOpen`; the shared select row hides the Select2 container, so nothing visible changes, but its change handlers bind after the animation. The new chat confirmation in `public/script.js` (`newChatConfirm.html`) also binds its checkbox listener in `onOpen`, so a toggle during the animation is missed.
- **Preset Menu:** the Presets page hides each backend's original preset select row and action buttons, the Chat Completions title card and Master Import/Export, and the row's Preset Menu clicks those hidden originals, because upstream and PresetManager bind every action to them (`mountPresetRows()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`).
  - The menu is built on every open, so PresetManager's late restore-defaults button and the read-only Update of a bundled preset are current.
  - **Pitfall:** the menu's clicks are untrusted, and preset autosave saves a pending edit only on a trusted press of a control in `FLUSH_CONTROL_SELECTOR` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-preset-autosave.js`). That list names `[data-sb-preset-row]`, so the press on the row's ⋮ or picker saves first. Without it, a menu action such as Delete… or Rename… starts while the edit is still pending.
  - **Pitfall:** Edit Prompts is an activatable `createRow()` row, but the activatable-row and chevron rules live in the deferred `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css`, which the Presets page does not load. On desktop the Characters list usually loads it first, which hides the gap. The page keeps its own copy of those rules in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`.
  - **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js` (menu sections, Import… opens a file chooser, Edit Prompts styled with the Editor stylesheet off), `tests/sillybunny-preset-autosave.e2e.js` (read-only Update, the press saves at once, Edit Prompts and autosave placement).
- **Preset API/Sampler Linking:** `mountPresetLinkingGroup()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` moves the three Chat Completions linking blocks out of their drawer into a group after Presets. It removes the emptied drawer, because the flatten pass would turn it into an empty headed section inside Presets.
  - **Pitfall:** `preparePromptingPanel()` runs while the Prompting content is still detached from the document, so `document.getElementById()` returns `null` there. Look nodes up from the container instead.
  - **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js` (the Preset API/Sampler Linking group test).
- **Character editor action menu:** the More menu (`_buildActionSections()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-character-editor.js`) offers Global Styles, External Media and Character Stats by clicking hidden transports. Their handlers act on the selected group or on `this_chid`, which is not always the card in the editor. Upstream hides `#character_open_media_overrides` with an inline `display: none` where its dialog would act on another entity: in the create form (`select_rm_create()` in `public/script.js`) and on a group member's card (`select_selected_character()` toggles it with `!selected_group`).
  - **Breaks:** the menu listed External Media in both states anyway, so in the create form it opened the dialog for the character that was selected when the form opened, and in group peek for the group.
  - **Guard:** the menu leaves out a recovered action whose transport has an inline `display: none`. `.sb-editor-transport` hides every transport with `display: none !important`, so the resolved style is `none` for all of them and only the inline style carries upstream's signal. Global Styles and Character Stats never get an inline display, so the menu offers them in the create form, where they act on `this_chid`; upstream shows both buttons in the create form too.
  - **Tests:** `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` (`the action menu offers External Media while editing and leaves it out of the create form`, 1440 and 390 px). Group peek has no test.
- **Adopted selects and their descriptions:** `createSelectRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/select-row.js`) hides the adopted `<select>` from assistive technology (`aria-hidden="true"`, `tabindex="-1"`), and its trigger button is the control that takes focus, so a description on the select reaches no one.
  - **Breaks:** the trigger was described only by the row subtitle. The NanoGPT Allowed and Ignored providers rows lost the PAYG billing warning (`aria-describedby="nanogpt_billing_warning"` in `public/index.html`), and seven other Connections select rows, `#model_openai_select` among them, lost the help that `applyConnectionsFormPass()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-connections-form.js`) attaches to the select after the row is built.
  - **Guard:** `sync()` gives the trigger the select's own description ids followed by the subtitle's id, and the row's existing `MutationObserver` on the select runs `sync()` again when a caller changes the select's `aria-describedby` later. The row never writes the select's attribute, so `destroy()` has nothing to restore.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-select-row.test.js` (`the trigger carries the select's own descriptions, then the subtitle`; `the trigger follows a description added to the select after the row is built`).
- **Values written with `change.select2`:** upstream writes a loaded model with `$(select).val(x).trigger('change.select2')` (`openai.js`, `textgen-models.js`) to refresh select2 without running the page's change handlers.
  - **Breaks:** jQuery runs only the handlers in the `select2` namespace for that event, so the row's `change.sbSelectRow` listener never ran, and setting `.value` queues no mutation. The row kept the old model name until something else changed the select. select2's `destroy()` also calls `off('.select2')`, which removes any listener bound in that namespace.
  - **Guard:** `onSelectChanges()` in `select-row.js` also binds `sync` as `change.select2.sbSelectRow` and binds it again from the row's `MutationObserver` (select2 destroy and init change the select's class and attributes). The select2-namespaced listener only syncs; it never calls `onChange`, which stays for plain changes.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-select-row.test.js` (`with a value written through select2 or a plain change`); `tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js` (`the model row describes the chosen model and follows a value written through select2`).
- **Delete Tag merge target:** `onTagDeleteClick()` in `public/scripts/tags.js` looked the select up with `popupContent.find('#merge_tag_select')`. The select is a top-level node of the `deleteTag` template, and jQuery's `find()` only matches descendants.
  - **Breaks:** select2 was never initialised on desktop, and the Delete confirmation always read an empty value, so a chosen merge target was dropped and the tag was deleted without merging.
  - **Guard:** the handler holds `mergeSelect`, built from `filter()` plus `find()`, for the select2 setup, the select row and the final read.
  - **Tests:** `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js` (`Delete Tag offers its merge target as a select row and merges into the tag picked there`, 1440 and 390 px).
- **The Tabby downloader has an adapter, and its caller reads the inputs by name:** `downloadTabbyModel()` (`public/scripts/textgen-models.js`) reads `hf_repo_id`, `revision`, `folder_name`, `hf_token` and the two `tabby_download_*` textareas from the rendered template after the popup closes. `tabbyDownloaderOptions()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/adapters/utility-dialogs.js`) moves those live inputs into entry rows (floating for the inputs, stacked for the textareas) and keeps names, values and ids, so the call is unchanged apart from spreading the options. The Repo ID caption is a bare text node in the template, so the adapter reads it from there. Tests: `tests/unit/utility-dialogs.test.js`; `tests/utility-dialogs.e2e.js` (it fills the fields and reads the request).

### Cache, boot and reload

- **Breaks:** boot continues before the cache is invalidated; a module is evaluated twice.
- **Files:** `public/index.html`, `public/sw.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-restart-cache-clear.js`, `src/middleware/frontend-assets.js`, `public/scripts/extensions.js`, `public/scripts/extension-boot-lifecycle/index.js`.
- **Guards:** unhashed assets are served with `Cache-Control: no-cache` (`src/middleware/frontend-assets.js`). Fingerprinted assets are served as `immutable` (`src/frontend-assets.js`). `scripts/check-frontend-budgets.js` checks asset budgets.
- **Guards:** the browser keeps one module instance for each full URL, query string included, so a JavaScript module that one importer loads with `?v=` and another loads bare runs twice. The extension loader loads an extension's entry module by its bare URL (`buildExtensionModuleUrl()` in `public/scripts/extension-boot-lifecycle/index.js`, used by `addExtensionScript()` and `callExtensionHook()`). `getExtensionAssetUrl()` keeps `?v=<CLIENT_VERSION>` for templates, stylesheets, manifests and locale files, which no module imports. `prefetchExtensionAsset()` hints a script by its bare URL. The query adds no freshness for a module: the server answers every public JavaScript file with `Cache-Control: no-cache` and an ETag (`setPublicAssetHeaders()`) and a user's third-party extension with `public, max-age=0` and an ETag (`createExtensionsRouteHandler()` in `src/users.js`), and a conditional request gets 304 (checked with curl). The query also stayed the same between extension updates, so it never busted the cache for one.
- **Breaks:** from `e50d2d344` (2026-04-12) until this fix, the loader added `?v=` to the entry module, and 28 TTS provider files import `./index.js` bare. The browser ran `tts/index.js` twice. In the copy that the providers call, `ttsProvider` is undefined, so typing in a provider's settings field threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'settings')` in `saveTtsProviderSettings()` and the setting was not saved. A third-party extension that imports its own entry module ran twice the same way (a scratch extension counted 2 evaluations before and 1 after). A probe of the 23 bundled extension scripts with every extension on found only `tts/index.js` under two URLs. Quick Reply had worked around it in `quick-reply/src/shared.js`.
- **Tests:** `tests/frontend-asset-middleware.test.js`, `tests/restart-cache-clear.test.js`, `tests/script-module-identity.test.js` (`script.js` in `index.html`; #427), `tests/extension-boot-lifecycle.test.js` and `tests/extension-boot-lifecycle-wiring.test.js` (the loader's URL choice), `tests/extension-module-identity.e2e.js` (a TTS provider setting is saved and `tts/index.js` loads once; no bundled extension module loads under two URLs).
- **Limit:** `tests/extension-module-identity.e2e.js` turns the bundled extensions on by editing the settings that its page receives, and it names `sillybunny-debugger`, the only bundled opt-in extension, in them. It does not load a user-installed third-party extension; the evaluation counts above came from a scratch one.
- **No test:** `b6d657e08` (#380, stale iOS service-worker cache), `fe3e25446` (#429, versioned module URLs evaluate `script.js` twice).
- **Fixes:** #416 (restore JavaScript revalidation), #906 (boot races the restart cache-clear reload), `fe3e25446` (#429, `script.js`), F-291 (extension entry modules).
- **Service-worker cache keys for imports and images:** `public/sw.js` serves `/css/` and `/img/` stale-while-revalidate (`:7–12`) and keys the cache by the full request URL. A changed `@import`ed stylesheet or image under an unchanged URL is answered from the old cache on the first load after an update; a new `style.css?v=` does not help, because the imported URL is unchanged. The `loader.css` import in `public/style.css:6` and the splash picture URLs in `public/scripts/sillybunny-splash.js` (`ART_VERSION`) carry `?v=` keys for this reason; change the key with any picture change. For the art of `assets-src/art/art-manifest.json`, `node scripts/art/cli.js bump --write` changes the Home `ART_VERSION`, the splash `ART_VERSION` and the splash script's key in `index.html` when the files they cache changed, and `node scripts/art/cli.js check` fails with `key-stale` until it has run. A key in the files that is ahead of the manifest's record passes `check` (`keyProblems` in `scripts/art/lib/keys.js`), because the splash script's key in `index.html` also moves when that script's code changes: `82a80f09c` raised it to `20261110a` on the overhaul branch, and the derive branch's `check` then failed with `key-mismatch` until the rule changed. A key behind the record still fails. Test: `scripts/art/test/keys.test.js`. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` (each picture of the `ART` table is the file of the table, at the size of the table). The icon set replaces `public/favicon.ico`, `public/img/icon-*`, `public/img/apple-icon-*` and `public/img/logo.png` under the same names; the favicon links in `index.html` and `login.html` point at new URLs (`public/img/sillybunny/logo-*.png`), which are fresh at once. No page code requests a same-named file under `/img/`: `img/logo.png` appears only in `scripts/templates/welcomePanel.html`, which nothing renders (Home renders `sillybunnyHome.html`). `/favicon.ico` (a CSS background in `public/style.css`) and `/manifest.json` are outside the worker's prefixes. The server sends the icons with `Cache-Control: public, max-age=3600` and the manifest with `no-cache` (checked with `curl -I`). Whether a browser's own fetches of the favicon, touch icons and manifest icons pass through the worker is not tested here. Rename the file or key its URL if a same-named image must change at once; changing `SB_SW_CACHE_VERSION` in `public/sw.js` makes the worker delete every older `sillybunny-cache-` cache when it activates, and no rule in this file or in `CONTRIBUTING.md` says when to do that.
- **Account storage before init:** `accountStorage.init(state)` merges the saved settings over the current state (`public/scripts/util/AccountStorage.js:79–81`), so a value written with `setItem()` before `getSettings()` runs is replaced by the saved one. `public/scripts/sillybunny-chat-mode-memory.js` keeps such an early write in memory, writes it again on `SETTINGS_LOADED`, and then leaves `accountStorage` authoritative. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-chat-mode-memory.test.js`.
- **Home re-render:** `openWelcomeScreen({ force: true })` (pin, rename or delete in Recent Chats) used to empty `#chat` before appending the new panel, so the Home state turned off and on within one task. It now removes only the non-panel children and `sendWelcomePanel()` replaces the panel in place (`public/scripts/welcome-screen.js`), so the state stays on. `sillybunny-chat-mode-memory.js` still judges a Home exit one task later (`setTimeout(0)`) as a guard. `sendWelcomePanel()` also re-checks after its template `await` that no chat opened meanwhile, because the first render fetches the template uncached. Replacing the panel destroys the focused control, so `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/focus.js` restores focus to the same control in the same row, or the nearest one. Tests: `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (a pin re-renders Home with no `sb:home-state-changed` event; focus after pin, Recent Chats Settings and Delete; a held template request with a chat opened meanwhile adds no panel), `tests/home-mode-memory.e2e.js`.
- **A Home panel's observers end with the panel, not with the Home state:** after a chat opened from Home, the footer fit, the header fit and the Getting Started tile kept the detached panel alive until Home was opened again.
  - **Cause:** `fitCardFooters`, `fitHomeHeader` and `mountTiles` each kept a module-level stop function and ran it only when the next Home panel mounted. Until then their two `ResizeObserver`s (on 5 elements of the panel), the header `MutationObserver`, two `document.fonts` `loadingdone` listeners and the state subscription held the panel.
  - **Guard:** the three functions return their stop and keep no state. `mountHome` hands each stop to `beginPanelLifetime()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/panel-lifetime.js`), which runs them when the panel is removed from `#chat` (a `MutationObserver` on the children of `#chat` that reads the removed nodes) or when the next panel begins. A panel that is not in `#chat` yet is left alone, because `mountHome` runs before the panel is attached.
  - **Pitfall:** the Home state is the wrong signal. `deriveHomeActive()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home-state.js:21–25`) is off while `#sheld[data-sb-conversation-mode='on']` is set, and Conversation sets that attribute without removing the panel (`public/scripts/sillybunny-conversation/chrome.js:1378`). Stopping on `active: false` froze the footer labels and the version placement after Conversation closed. With Conversation open the state is already off, so a chat that then empties `#chat` sends no event at all.
  - **Hero art:** the Home hero (`createHeroArt()`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js`) is a plain `span`, so unlike `<sb-art-panel>` it has no `disconnectedCallback`. The layout dropped it only in `run()`, when a resize found it disconnected. A panel removed while Conversation hid it saw no resize, so the hero and its band stayed observed (found by counting every observation inside the panel). `createHeroArt()` now returns `{ panel, stop }`, and `mountHome()` keeps `stop` in the lifetime. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout-motion.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout.test.js` and `tests/home-observers.e2e.js` (the hero and its band are counted).
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-panel-lifetime.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-mount-lifetime.test.js`, the stop tests in `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-footer-fit.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-header-fit.test.js` and `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-tiles.test.js`, and `tests/home-observers.e2e.js`. The e2e spec wraps `ResizeObserver`, `MutationObserver` and `document.fonts` in an init script to record what each watches. A lifetime that stopped on the Home state failed both Conversation tests.
- **Web fonts must be real WOFF2:** the eight `public/webfonts/AdwaitaMono/*.woff2` files added in `6f579835b` were raw TrueType (first bytes `00 01 00 00`) under a `.woff2` name. Chromium's font sanitiser (OTS) and Firefox rejected them ("OTS parsing error: glyf: Bad glyph flag …, bit 6 must be set to zero for flag 1", because upstream's TrueType sets the overlap flag on later points), so monospace text silently fell back to the next font; WebKit accepted them. Any real WOFF2 conversion fixes it, because WOFF2's glyph encoding keeps only the first point's overlap flag. The files were rebuilt from GNOME `adwaita-fonts-50.0` (source, checksum and the `pyftsubset` command are in the `docs/upstream-touch-ledger.md` "Frontend identity, localization, and static boot assets" entry). `public/sw.js` caches `/webfonts/` by full URL, so new font bytes need a new `?v=` key on every URL in that family's `stylesheet.css` and on its `index.html` and `login.html` links. Tests: `tests/unit/webfonts-format.test.js` (container signature, URLs resolve, keys present), `tests/webfonts-load.e2e.js` (every Adwaita face loads through `FontFace` with no OTS or decode message; it fails with the old Regular Latin file).
- **Stylesheet keys:** a stylesheet referenced under two different `?v=` keys loads twice, which is the same cause as `fe3e25446` for modules. When you change a stylesheet, give it one new key at every call site in the same commit (`index.html` and every `loadStylesheetAsync` literal), then grep for one key per file. `820589f4d` moved the eight stylesheets changed for PR #870 to one new key each. Changing an `@import` key inside `public/style.css` is a change to `style.css` too: `0f3a0d109` and `11df3a94c` bumped the `sillybunny-home.css` import key without moving `style.css?v=` in `public/index.html`, so a browser holding the old `style.css` kept importing the old Home sheet; the next commit moved both `index.html` keys. `tests/unit/stylesheet-cache-keys.test.js` fails on a sheet referenced under two keys anywhere in `public/` (vendored and third-party code aside); it was written after the Backgrounds gallery shipped with the editor shell and editor chrome sheets under keys a day old, so whichever page loaded them first decided which copy the session used.
- **The editors' shared sheet is deferred, and an editor that shows before it applies is held back:** `public/css/sillybunny-character-editor.css` (44.6 KB: the editor header, actions, fields, sections, tag chips and the group editor's rules) was a blocking sheet, and the blocking total was 2,766 bytes over the base of the UI polish work. It is now a `rel="preload"` with `data-sb-deferred-style` in `public/index.html` at its old place in the head, so the cascade order against the other sheets is unchanged and idle activation applies it. The blocking total fell from 1,041,697 to 997,611 bytes. Every surface that uses its rules loads it through `loadListEditorStyles()` (the shell sheet and this one) or `EDITOR_CHROME_STYLESHEET` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/list-shell.js`) and waits before it shows: the World Info, Prompt, agent and persona editors, the lists (selection mode uses `.sb-editor-action`), the group editor and the Chats Archive. The character and group editors are static markup shown from many paths by `selectRightMenuWithAnimation()`, so `holdEditorsUntilStyled()` (called from `initCharacterEditor()`) watches `#rm_ch_create_block` and `#rm_group_chats_block` and keeps one that turns active before its sheet applies at `visibility: hidden !important` inline. An important inline value is needed because the mobile shell sets `.right_menu.sb-active-right-menu { visibility: visible !important }` (`sillybunny-mobile-shell.css`), which a plain inline `hidden` lost at 390 px. When the sheet applies, the gate finishes the `CSSTransition`s that it started (the Save button's `background-color` otherwise faded from the unstyled value in the first visible frame) and shows the menu in the same microtask, before the frame. One rule group that surfaces outside the editors use stayed blocking and moved to the end of `sillybunny-tabs.css`: `.sb-editor-section` / `.sb-editor-section__body` (the Connections page, dialogs and windows build sections). A second group, `.sb-draft-toast__actions` (the draft recovery message can show while the sheet is loading), was there until `581fe195f`: the notification's button row lays out the buttons now, so no sheet styles them. A computed-style census of the surface registry's non-editor surfaces that opened on a test server (269 open states at 1440 px), with the sheet switched off, found differences only in the editors and the selection headers, which wait for the sheet. Tests: `tests/editor-styles-first-frame.e2e.js` (holds the sheet's request, opens the character editor and a World Info entry at 1440 and 390 px, and records every frame: no frame that shows the header lacks the sheet, and the first one has the settled header height and Save fill), `tests/unit/sillybunny-editor-styles-gate.test.js`. A new module would have been a startup request (`frontend-performance.e2e.js` counts 861 at its limit), so the gate lives in `list-shell.js`, which the start loads already.
- **Boot timing marks:** a start makes four `performance` marks: `sb-boot-inline` (`sillybunny-splash.js`), `sb-boot-init-start` (`firstLoadInit()` in `public/script.js`), `sb-boot-dismiss` (the first dismissal in `sillybunny-boot-guard.js`; `dismissPreloader()` runs more than once) and `sb-app-ready` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-boot-marks.js`). About → Troubleshooting lists them and `scripts/measure-frontend-performance.js` reads them through `BOOT_MARK_NAMES`. The splash reads only localStorage, because it runs before modules and settings, so `sillybunny-splash-preferences.js` mirrors the Splash Character choice and Reduced Motion there after settings load and on each save. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-boot-marks.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash-preferences.test.js`, `tests/splash.e2e.js`.
- **Home renders never wait on a status request:** the first version of the assistant cards' Restore and Update offers awaited the managed-content status (capped at 5 s) before every Home render, so a slow or hung server froze Home on each pin, rename, pointer change or Restore, not only at boot (review measured 9.0 s first paint and 5.0 s re-renders with a hung route). Home now waits for a first answer only briefly, reuses the last known status on later renders, refreshes it in the background, and re-mounts the cards only when the offers they would show change and no menu or dialog is open (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/managed-status.js`, `assistants-refresh.js`, `welcome-screen.js` `sendWelcomePanel`). Comparing the raw status re-mounted identical cards when a late empty answer arrived, so the comparison is on the offers (`describeOffers`). Tests: `tests/managed-updates.e2e.js` (slow, hung and failing status; re-mounts held by an open menu or alert), `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-managed-status.test.js` (`d3ec31955`).
- **The splash colours come from the last session:** `rememberBootSurface()` (`public/script.js`) stores `sb-boot-surface` after `APP_READY` and on `pagehide`, and the inline boot script in `public/index.html` reads it before any stylesheet, so a test of the seeded splash colours boots the app once and then holds the splash on a reload (`tests/splash.e2e.js`).
- **The Home cards load the small thumbnail, and a cache refresh keeps the preset:** the desktop avatar preset is 864×1280 PNG (`thumbnails.dimensions.avatar` in `default/config.yaml`), larger than the 768×1152 card files, so the server scaled Reisen up to 1,391,637 B and Tewi to 1,209,554 B for a 128px box; the mobile preset (`&preset=mobile`, 320×480 JPEG) is 30,563 B and 27,517 B. Home's cards and the Choose Assistant picker now use `getMobileThumbnailUrl` at every viewport (`public/scripts/welcome-screen.js`), and chat avatars elsewhere are unchanged. `refreshCharacterAvatarCache()` matches pictures with `img[src^="/thumbnail?type=avatar&file=…"]`, a prefix that also matches the mobile URL, and it rebuilt every match as the desktop URL with `&t=`, so an Update or a Restore put the full-size file back on the card. It now refreshes the cached mobile file too and gives a picture that had the preset a new source on the same preset. Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("are the small mobile preset at every viewport"), `tests/managed-updates.e2e.js` ("Update refreshes the cached file of the mobile preset", "Update gives the picture of the card a new source on the mobile preset"), which fail 4 of 4 with the wiring alone.
- **The Recent Chats rows load the small thumbnail too:** a row shows its picture in a 44px box (`.welcomeRecent .recentChat .avatar` in `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`), but `getRecentChats()` in `public/scripts/welcome-screen.js` built `char_thumbnail` with `getThumbnailUrl`, so on desktop each row fetched the full-size file: Reisen 1,391,637 B and Seraphina 2,093,971 B (PNG) against 30,563 B and 40,067 B (JPEG) on the mobile preset. It now uses `getMobileThumbnailUrl` at every viewport, and the `system_avatar` fallback is unchanged. The row's picture is matched by the `img[src^=…]` prefix in `refreshCharacterAvatarCache()`, so it keeps the preset on an Update or a Restore through the 4.25 fix. The collage of a group row comes from the shared `getGroupAvatar()` in `public/scripts/group-chats.js`, which uses `getThumbnailUrlForViewport` and is also used by the group panel and editor, so it was left as it is. Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("of the Recent Chats rows are the small mobile preset too"), `tests/managed-updates.e2e.js` ("Update gives the picture of a Recent Chats row of that card a new source on the mobile preset"), both red at 1440 and 390 before the change.
- **A spinner is the shared spinner, and the helpers for it live in a module the start already loads:** `spinnerMarkup()`, `createSpinner()`, `setIconBusy()` and `ensureSpinnerStyles()` are exports of `public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/status-page.js`, which `sillybunny-tabs.js` imports at startup, so a spinner site adds no module request; the sheet that draws it (`sillybunny-editor-shell.css`) loads on first use, as it does for a status page. A control that waited with a spinning glyph (`fa-spin`) swaps its icon for the spinner with `setIconBusy()`, which hides the icon with an inline `display: none` (the `hidden` attribute loses to the `display` that Font Awesome sets on a glyph) and restores the previous inline `display`. A spinner under `.hidden` must not animate, because `.hidden` is `visibility: hidden` and keeps the layout and the animation: `sillybunny-theme.css` stops `.sb-spinner.hidden`, and `header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js` (`waitForAnimations`) fails on one that runs. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-status-page.test.js`, `tests/header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js`.
- **The Caps Lock icon follows events, not the system:** a page cannot read the Caps Lock state without an event, so the icon of `createEntryRow` (`capsLockIndicator()` in `rows.js`) follows `getModifierState('CapsLock')` of key and pointer events of a masked field and hides when the field loses focus or its text is revealed. Playwright's keyboard never sets the lock state (`keyboard.down('CapsLock')` reports `false` in the events that follow), so `tests/sillybunny-editor-rows-layout.e2e.js` sets `modifierCapsLock` on a dispatched event. `rows.js` takes the translated label as `capsLockLabel`, because the unit tests evaluate the module with its one import removed.

### Route errors and crashes

- **Breaks:** one request that fails outside its route's error handling ends the server, and the server stays down.
- **Cause:** Express 4 ignores the promise that a route handler or middleware returns, so a rejected async route got no answer and reached the `unhandledRejection` handler in `src/server-main.js`. That handler and the `uncaughtException` handler exited with code 0, which the supervisor passes on as a stop. A throw in a stream, readline or timer callback still reaches `uncaughtException`, because no route is on the stack there.
- **Breaks:** the client's CSRF retry (#475) never runs.
- **Cause:** `fetchWithCsrfRetry()` in `public/scripts/csrf-token-refresh.js` refreshes the token and repeats the request only for a 403 whose body contains `Invalid CSRF token`. Express's default error handler sends only the status text in production, and `server.js` defaults `NODE_ENV` to production, as `Start.bat`, `Start-Node.bat`, `start.sh`, the `Dockerfile` and `docker/docker-compose.yml` set it, so the 403 said only `Forbidden`.
- **Files:** `src/route-errors.js`, `src/server-main.js`, `src/server-supervisor.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `forwardAsyncRouteErrors()` in `src/route-errors.js` replaces `handle_request()` and `handle_error()` on the Express 4 `Layer` prototype, so a rejected promise from a handler or an error handler goes to `next()`, as the Express 5 router does. It covers every app, router and route made from the server's copy of Express, including the routers that server plugins get. It does not cover a router made from another copy of Express or code that runs outside a route. A falsy rejection reason becomes `Error('Rejected promise')`, because `next()` with a falsy value goes on to the next route. Express 5 does this itself, so the module goes with an upgrade to Express 5.
  - `handleRouteError()` is registered after the not-found handler, before the server listens. Express calls a four-parameter middleware only for errors and skips the not-found handler for them, so both only have to come after every route, plugin routes included. It logs the error as Express's default handler did, and a client disconnect as the same warning as the process handlers. It answers with the error's own 4xx or 5xx status (400 or 413 from the body parser, 403 for a CSRF token) or 500, with only the status text as the body in every `NODE_ENV`; Express's default handler sent the stack trace outside production. It does not answer a response that has started: it destroys the socket of an unfinished one and leaves a finished one alone.
  - The CSRF refusal is the one error answered with its own message. csrf-sync 4.2.1 makes one `invalidCsrfTokenError` per protection object and passes that object to `next()` for every refused request. `src/server-main.js` sets its message and passes it to `answerWithErrorMessage()`, so `handleRouteError()` answers it with 403 and that message as `text/plain`, without the chalk color codes, which `stripVTControlCharacters()` from `node:util` removes under Bun and Node.js. The match is the object itself, not the `EBADCSRFTOKEN` code that other CSRF libraries also use, so another error with that code keeps the status text.
  - The `uncaughtException` and `unhandledRejection` handlers exit with `SERVER_CRASH_EXIT_CODE` (1) after the usual cleanup, or after `SUPERVISOR_FORCE_KILL_TIMEOUT_MS` when a cleanup step fails or does not finish.
  - `runSupervisor()` relaunches a child that reported startup (`SERVER_STARTUP_READY_MESSAGE`) and then exits with a non-zero code or a signal while no stop was requested. It waits `SUPERVISOR_CRASH_RESTART_DELAY_MS` (1 s), doubled for each crash restart within `SUPERVISOR_CRASH_WINDOW_MS` (10 minutes), and a crash after `SUPERVISOR_CRASH_RESTART_LIMIT` (5) such restarts ends the supervisor with the child's exit code. A stop request ends the wait at once. A failure before startup, exit code 0, the restart, plugin update and supervisor reload codes and a requested stop behave as before. The launchers loop only on exit code 75, and Docker's `restart: unless-stopped` restarts on any exit, so they need no change.
- **Limit:** a crash restart loses what the server process holds in memory, such as session-auth sessions and server-side generations that were running.
- **Tests:** `tests/route-errors.test.js`, `tests/server-supervisor-crash-restart.test.js`, `tests/csrf-refusal-retry.test.js` (the csrf-sync setup of `src/server-main.js` with a colored message, and `fetchWithCsrfRetry()` against its real answers).
- **Fixes:** `ae27417cc` (async route errors), `120fa5fbf` (crash restarts), `a41adfe62` (crash exit code), `b0735ddab` (CSRF refusal text).

### Runtimes, Termux, launchers and updates

- **Breaks:** the wrong runtime or shell behaviour on one platform.
- **Files:** `Start-Node.bat`, `scripts/`, `scripts/lib/npm-launcher.js`, `src/npm-launcher.js`, `src/endpoints/server-admin.js`.
- **Guards:** `server.js` runs under `src/server-supervisor.js`. It loads `src/server-log-buffer.js` first and imports `src/server-main.js` last. Do not change this import order.
- **npm from a script:** Node.js on Windows cannot spawn `npm.cmd` without a shell, so a script under `scripts/` that runs npm calls `runNpm()` from `scripts/lib/npm-launcher.js`. It takes the command from `getNpmCommand()` in `src/npm-launcher.js`, which runs `npm-cli.js` with `node` on Windows and plain `npm` elsewhere, and passes the arguments without a shell. `scripts/check-frontend-foundation.js` is the one script that runs npm (test: `tests/frontend-foundation-launcher.test.js`; no test runs the Windows branch of this script).
- **Bun and disconnects:** under Bun 1.3.12 and 1.3.14 an Express response emits no `close` when the client disconnects; the request and its socket do. Node emits all three, and emits the response's `close` from inside the socket's. The streamed routes in `src/resumable-generations.js` use `whenClientGone()`, which watches both and runs once (test: `tests/server-generation-commit.test.js`). Still on the response's `close`: `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js`, `src/endpoints/server-admin.js`, `src/endpoints/chat-archive.js`, `src/server-listen.js`.
- **Bun and transformers.js images:** sillytavern-transformers takes its browser code path when `self` is defined, and Bun defines it. On that path `RawImage.fromBlob()` needs `createImageBitmap()` and `RawImage.resize()` needs `OffscreenCanvas`. Bun has neither, so local captioning failed for every image. `decodeRawImage()` in `src/transformers-image.js` decodes with the server's jimp and returns a `RawImage` subclass whose `resize()` also uses jimp (test: `tests/transformers-image.test.js`, which defines `self` so that Node.js takes the Bun code path). `crop()` and `center_crop()` still need a canvas under Bun; the default captioning model does not call them.
- **Bun and the listen port:** before Bun 1.4.0, sockets on Windows are inheritable (oven-sh/bun#36936, fixed by oven-sh/bun#36938). A process that the server starts, such as git during an update, can keep the port bound after the server exits. `engines.bun` still allows 1.3.14. The listen retry window in `src/server-listen.js` and `GIT_OPTIONS` in `src/endpoints/server-admin.js` (no background git maintenance during updates) guard against it. `hasBunSocketInheritBug()` limits the port-conflict hint and the dead-holder diagnostic to Bun before 1.4.0 on Windows (test: `tests/server-listen.test.js`). Before Bun 1.4.3, `closeAllConnections()` does nothing once `close()` has run (oven-sh/bun#43557), so `closeListeningServers()` also destroys the tracked sockets.
- **Bun and fetch idle timeouts:** under Bun 1.4.4, `fetch` ends a request with `TimeoutError: The operation timed out.` after 360 seconds with no bytes, including a gap in the middle of a stream. Node.js 24.22.1 does not. `timeout: 0` turns this off under Bun, and `node-fetch` 3 ignores the option. In `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js` only the Mistral, Cohere, generateContent, Interactions and KoboldCpp generation requests pass it (tests: `tests/google-model-request.test.js`, `tests/gemini-interactions.test.js`, `tests/koboldcpp-chat.test.js`).
- **Git child processes:** simple-git 4 removes every `GIT_*` variable and `EDITOR`, `VISUAL`, `PAGER`, `PREFIX` and `SSH_ASKPASS` from the environment of the git processes it starts, unless the `allowEnvironment` option names them. SillyBunny sets no `allowEnvironment`, so a user's `GIT_SSH_COMMAND`, `GIT_ASKPASS` or `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` does not reach git during server updates, server plugin updates, or extension installs and updates. simple-git 4 also sets `GIT_TEST_DISALLOW_ABBREVIATED_OPTIONS`, so git stops on an abbreviated long option such as `--dep` for `--depth`; write options in full. It refuses a `-c` key that it treats as unsafe, such as `core.sshCommand`; `GIT_OPTIONS` sets only `gc.auto` and `maintenance.auto`.
- **Tests:** `tests/server-supervisor.test.js`, `tests/base64-atomic-writes.test.js`, `tests/server-admin-update-install.test.js`, `tests/server-plugin-manager.test.js`, `tests/server-plugin-update-helper.test.js`, `tests/frontend-foundation-launcher.test.js`.
- **No test:** `c1a87366c` (#522, Bun bootstrap on Termux), `cd7429421` (issue #672, unescaped parentheses in `Start-Node.bat`).
- **Fixes:** #806 (explicit Base64 decode on Bun), #912 (run npm through Node.js on Windows), #916 (the frontend foundation gate runs npm through the shared launcher).
- **A fetch that must stop must abort:** under Bun, `node-fetch` is a shim over the native fetch, and cancelling the body reader or leaving a `for await` loop does not close the connection; only `AbortController.abort()` does. A hostile endless body stayed open upstream until the Bundled Extras theme route aborted its request whenever its deadline or size cap ended the task (`withDeadline` in `src/endpoints/sillybunny-bundled-themes.js`). The deadline also leaves `AbortError` rejections under Bun, which `server-main.js` logs as benign. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-bundled-themes.test.js` ("aborts its request once it is turned away", against a real local server).
- **Not every data folder has hard links:** exFAT and some Android storage refuse `linkSync`. `publishNewFile()` (`src/sillybunny-publish-file.js`) publishes a new file through an exclusive hard link and falls back to an exclusive copy on `EPERM`, `ENOTSUP`, `EXDEV` or `ENOSYS`, removing a half-written copy; `/api/themes/create` and the Bundled Extras theme route use it. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-publish-file.test.js`.

### Character import and upload files

- **Breaks:** data in an imported card or archive, in an uploaded sprite or sprite pack, or in a create request decides which folder the server writes to, which file type it stores, or how long the card parser runs.
- **Cause:** the import and upload code takes names, extensions and chunk lengths from the data it receives. A name that sanitizes to nothing makes the parent folder the target folder, an archive or uploaded file keeps its own extension, `sanitize-filename` keeps only the first 255 bytes of a name and so can cut off an extension at its end, and `png-chunks-extract` allocates, copies and checksums the length that a chunk declares before it finds that the file is shorter.
- **Files:** `src/endpoints/characters.js`, `src/endpoints/worldinfo.js`, `src/endpoints/sprites.js`, `src/endpoints/data-maid.js`, `src/users.js`, `src/external-media-import.js`, `src/external-media-manifest.js`, `src/stored-media-headers.js`, `src/image-file-type.js`, `src/character-card-parser.js`, `src/png/extract.js`, `src/charx.js`, `src/byaf.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `getCardAssetFolder()` in `src/endpoints/characters.js` names the asset folder of a CHARX or BYAF card after the sanitized card name, or after the card's file name when the name sanitizes to nothing. A CHARX import skips its assets when that name is not a folder directly inside the characters and user images folders. A preserved name such as `...png` gives that on POSIX systems, where `getPreservedName()` does not sanitize.
  - `isImageFile()` in `src/image-file-type.js` accepts a file only when its extension is png, apng, jpg, jpeg, jfif, gif, webp, avif or bmp and its leading bytes are an image of that type. A BYAF import stores a background or an alternate icon, and a CHARX import an asset, only when it passes; `isArchiveImage()` in `characters.js` logs the others. The stored bytes are the archive's bytes. A BYAF chat names only the backgrounds that were stored.
  - `localizeCharacterExternalMedia()` and `localizeWorldInfoExternalMedia()` in `src/external-media-import.js` run only when the request field `import_external_media` is a true form (`true` or `1`; any other value counts as false). A saved setting or a config key cannot turn them on, and `allowExternalMediaImport: false` in `config.yaml` forbids them for every user. Each external URL is checked by `isValidExternalMediaUrl()` (HTTP/HTTPS only, default port, no credentials, no `localhost` / trailing-dot `localhost.` or IP literals, no unresolved `{{...}}` macros) on the initial URL and on every redirect hop, and a redirect from `https:` to `http:` is refused. It is fetched through `getUntrustedRequestAgent()` (using `requestWithNodeHttp()` via `node:http`/`node:https` under Bun because Bun aliases `node-fetch` to its built-in `fetch()` which ignores `options.agent`), with `Accept-Encoding: identity` and no decoding on both paths, capped at 25 MB, verified by `isImageFile()` or `getImageExtension()`, and stored under `user/images/<folder>/<stem>-<sha256-16>.<ext>`. Any failed or non-image URL is left unchanged so the import still succeeds.
  - One import may download 200 MiB and take 120 seconds in all (`createImportBudget()`); a download reserves its byte limit before it starts, so downloads that run together cannot pass the total, and the URLs that are left stay remote. A file is stored through a temporary file and a hard link that fails when the name exists (`storeWithoutReplacing()`), asynchronously, so a 25 MiB write does not stop the server's thread and a file that the user keeps under the same name is never replaced. When that file has other bytes the URL stays remote.
  - The author's URL is kept: `recordExternalMediaOrigins()` in `src/external-media-manifest.js` writes `user/images/<folder>/.external-media.json` (stored file name to `{ url }`, the URL as the author wrote it, written one write at a time per folder) before the card or lorebook is saved, and the import leaves every URL remote when that write fails. `POST /api/characters/export` (`png` and `json`) and `POST /api/worldinfo/export` (called by the lorebook export button) call `restoreExternalMediaUrls()`, which replaces a local URL that its folder's manifest lists in the exported copy only. A damaged manifest lists nothing. Two URLs with the same file stem and the same bytes give one stored file, so the manifest keeps the first of them in text order and an export gives that URL back for both. Data Maid keeps a listed file while a card or a lorebook names it (`getKeptExternalMediaPaths()`), and keeps every listed file when it cannot read them all; a chat that copied the greeting is not checked.
  - `/user/images/*` sets `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` itself and gives `.avif` and `.jfif` an image type (`setStoredMediaHeaders()` in `src/stored-media-headers.js`). It sets no `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`: with that header Chromium does not load audio or video that the user opens directly or in a frame, and WebKit also fails with `default-src 'none'`.
  - `extractExternalMediaSpans()` runs on the server's thread for every text field of an imported file, so each open-ended class of its patterns also excludes the character that starts the next candidate (`[`, `<`, `(`), and a span is checked against the sorted spans of the earlier passes. Without that, 0.2 MB of repeated `url(`, `<img ` or `![` took 7 to 16 seconds and the time grew with the square of the length. An unclosed image or tag therefore does not take the link after it, and an alt text with `[` or a tag with `<` in an attribute is found only by the bare-URL pass.
  - The stored URL encodes the folder with `encodeURIComponent()` and then `!'()*~`, because a quote or a parenthesis ends the URL early in a single-quoted attribute, a Markdown image or a CSS `url()`. A folder name with `%` is not localized: `createRouteHandler()` in `src/users.js` decodes the path once more than the router did, so the static route answers 500 or 404 for it.
  - Log lines quote the URL and the host's error text with `quoteForLog()`, which escapes C0 and C1 controls, DEL and direction marks.
  - `POST /api/sprites/upload-zip` stores a pack entry only when its sanitized name passes `isImageFile()`. `POST /api/sprites/upload` keeps the uploaded extension when that passes, takes the extension of the image type that the bytes are (`getImageExtension()`) when it does not, and answers 400 when the sanitized name still fails, before it replaces an existing sprite. A sprite uploaded from a URL arrives as `image.png` whatever its type, so a wrong image extension is replaced and not refused. `readImageSignature()` reads only the leading bytes of the upload.
  - `POST /api/characters/import` takes the import function only when `Object.hasOwn(formatImportFunctions, format)`, so every other file type gets the unsupported format answer.
  - `POST /api/characters/create` answers 400 to a `file_name` that sanitizes to nothing, such as `.` or `..`, because the same name also names the card's chats folder.
  - `src/png/extract.js` walks the chunk headers up to IEND before `png-chunks-extract` runs, and throws `PNG chunk at byte <n> runs past the end of the file` when a chunk header or the data length it declares does not fit in the file. `read()` and `write()` in `src/character-card-parser.js` use it. A file that ends inside the CRC of a chunk still gets the CRC error, and a file that ends inside the CRC of IEND still imports.
- **Tests:** `tests/character-archive-assets.test.js`, `tests/character-route-names.test.js`, `tests/external-media-import.test.js`, `tests/world-info-export-media.test.js`, `tests/data-maid-external-media.test.js`, `tests/user-images-route.test.js`, `tests/png-chunk-bounds.test.js`, `tests/png-chunk-fuzz.test.js` (chunk lengths past the end of the file and files cut inside a chunk), `tests/card-import-corpus.test.js` (import, export and import again for each card and chat format), `tests/sprite-upload-images.test.js`.
- **Fixes:** `803c96bf0` (import formats), `45683ee8b` (create file names), `1d06397ae` (archive asset folders), `42c2a54d6` (archive images), `cf407575c` (PNG chunk lengths), `afac16c92` (shared image check), `2049ad29e` (sprite uploads), #1002 (external media import).

### Character route input

- **Breaks:** a request field of an unexpected type, or a file that a route cannot recover or remove, makes a character route throw outside its `try` block, so the request gets the generic 500 from `handleRouteError()` in `src/route-errors.js` instead of a 400 that names the problem.
- **Cause:** a rejection from an async route reaches `handleRouteError()` in `src/route-errors.js`, which answers 500 without saying what was wrong. `sanitize()`, `path.parse()` and `path.join()` throw on a value that is not a string, and `getFileNameValidationFunction()` in `src/middleware/validateFileName.js` checks a field only for separators and NUL, so a number, a list or an object reaches the route. A JSON body can send an object whose `toString` is not a function, and loose equality, string concatenation and `Number()` throw for it. `charaFormatData()` keeps a `json_data` that parses to a primitive (`tryParse(...) || {}`), and setting a field on a primitive throws in an ES module.
- **Files:** `src/endpoints/characters.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `POST /api/characters/rename` answers 400 when `avatar_url` or `new_name` is not a string.
  - `POST /api/characters/edit` answers 400 when `avatar_url` is missing, empty or not a string. It formats the body inside a `try` block that answers 400 when `charaFormatData()` throws or returns a primitive.
  - `POST /api/characters/delete` answers 400 when `delete_chats` is an object. It logs a thumbnail that `invalidateThumbnail()` cannot remove and still answers 200, because the card is already deleted.
  - `POST /api/characters/import` answers 500 when `recoverFileWriteSync()` throws for the card that `preserved_name` names, for example on a damaged write-recovery record.
- **Tests:** `tests/character-route-inputs.test.js`. Its harness option `requestTimeoutMs` makes a request that a route never answers fail at a deadline, and Jest fails the running test with the unhandled rejection. `tests/hostile-file-names-characters.test.js` requires 400 from `/api/characters/edit` for an empty `avatar_url`.
- **Fixes:** `5e6923c81` (request fields), `62068ea31` (file steps).

### Character index

- **Breaks:** the SQLite character index returns text that differs from what `parse()` returned for the card file, or keeps an entry for a file that no longer exists.
- **Cause:** in the source of Node.js 22.13.0, 22.22.0 and 22.23.4 (`src/node_sqlite.cc`), `node:sqlite` builds a TEXT value without its byte length, so the value ends at the first NUL character. Node.js 24.22.1 and Bun 1.4.4 return the whole text (checked by running them). `readCharacterData()` stores an entry for every file that it reads, including the uploaded PNG that an import deletes right after.
- **Files:** `src/character-index.js`, `src/endpoints/characters.js`, `src/server-main.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `CharacterIndex.set()` does not store a card whose text has a NUL character, so every runtime reads that card from its file.
  - `importFromPng()` calls `characterIndex.delete()` for the upload right after `readCharacterData()` reads it. `POST /all` prunes only the user's characters folder, so only the startup `prune()` would remove that entry.
  - `runSql()` calls `run` on Bun and `exec` on Node.js. The Bun 1.4.4 type file (`packages/bun-types/sqlite.d.ts`) marks `Database.exec` as a deprecated alias of `Database.run`, and the two are the same function in 1.4.4. If a release removed the alias, every call would throw and `#handleError()` would turn the index off with one warning.
  - `#handleError()` logs one line, without the error or its stack, when the error code is `ERR_UNKNOWN_BUILTIN_MODULE`, which `require('node:sqlite')` throws on Node.js older than 22.13. The index stays off, and every other error keeps the generic warning with its stack.
  - The Bun job (`run-bun-checks` in `.github/workflows/pr-checks.yml`) runs `bun test ./tests/character-index.test.js` after its `bun install`.
- **Tests:** `tests/character-index.test.js` runs under Jest and under `bun test`. Its `under Bun` block runs only under Bun, where it removes `Database.exec` and checks that the index still works. Its `on Node.js` block runs only on Node.js, where it makes `require('node:sqlite')` throw `ERR_UNKNOWN_BUILTIN_MODULE` and checks the one-line warning. `tests/pr-checks-workflow.test.js` checks the Bun job step.
- **Fixes:** #938.

### Font Settings: imported fonts, presets and the legacy Google name

- **Breaks:** a font name went into `--mainFontFamily` as `'<name>'` with no escaping, so a name with a quote gave an invalid value, and a name written into an `@font-face` rule would have let a user's text close the rule. A minimal font file that a test builds is refused by the browser's sanitiser unless every table is valid: an empty `glyf` fails with `OTS parsing error: glyf: zero-length table`, and a short `maxp` fails with `Failed to read version 1 table data`.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-fonts.js`, `applyGoogleFont()` and `normalizeFontSettings()` in `public/scripts/power-user.js`, `presentFont()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-appearance.js`, `src/endpoints/data-maid.js`.
- **State:** `power_user.sb_fonts` is the list (one entry per file: `id`, `family`, `file`, `weight`, `style`, `format`) and `power_user.google_font` stays the choice and holds a family name. `resolveFont()` decides where a family comes from: an imported family, then a bundled preset by its exact name, then the Google stylesheet of old. `canonicalFontName()` rewrites a name that differs from a preset or an imported family only by case or spacing, once, at load. The stack is `'<name>', 'Adwaita Sans', system-ui, sans-serif`.
- **Guards:**
  - Every name that reaches CSS goes through `cssString()`, which writes any character outside letters, digits and a few marks as a code point escape. `normalizeFonts()` drops an entry whose file path is not `/user/files/sb-font-<12 hex>.<ext>`, so a tampered setting cannot reach a stylesheet or a delete request.
  - The server checks only the file name of an upload, so `validateFontFile()` checks the extension, the size (5 MB), the first four bytes against the extension and refuses collections before anything is sent, and `FontFace().load()` must resolve before the file is uploaded. The upload name is built by the client (`sb-font-<12 hex>`), never from the user's file name.
  - Data Maid skips files that start with `sb-font-`; without that it offers every imported font as a loose file.
  - The module loads on demand: `applyGoogleFont()` returns without it while no font is saved, none is imported and it has never loaded. `tests/font-import.e2e.js` asserts that default data requests no module, no preset stylesheet and nothing from Google.
  - A preset adds one `#sb-preset-font-style` link to `webfonts/<Dir>/stylesheet.css` and removes the Google link; switching presets replaces the link, and Default removes it. `tests/font-import.e2e.js` chooses each preset and asserts that its stylesheet and upright file are requested only then and only from the app, that italic text requests the italic file, that `--mainFontFamily` and the body's computed `font-family` carry the stack, and that a reload keeps it. A browser serialises a one-word family without quotes, so the test compares the stack without quotes. A legacy Google name still requests Google's stylesheet; the test answers that request itself and refuses `fonts.gstatic.com`, so no test run reaches the network.
  - A WOFF table declares its own size, so `readWoffTables()` refuses more than 1 MB before inflating. WOFF2 metadata is read only where `DecompressionStream('brotli')` exists (`canReadWoff2()`); otherwise the naming dialog opens.
- **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-fonts.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-fonts-page.test.js`, `tests/font-routes.test.js` (the real routes on Node.js and on Bun, through `tests/helpers/font-routes-harness.js`), `tests/data-maid-fonts.test.js`, `tests/font-import.e2e.js`, `tests/dialog-header-metrics.e2e.js`; the font builder is `tests/helpers/font-fixtures.js`.
- **Open:** the list is saved with `saveSettingsDebounced()`, which reports no result, so a failed save leaves the uploaded files on the server; the next Data Maid run does not list them either. jsdom has no `TextDecoder`, so a jsdom test of the reader sets it from `node:util`.

### Appearance: the Custom CSS Window and the theme reset

- **Breaks:** Custom CSS sat in a group at the end of the page, away from the theme settings, with a 4-row editor; and "Reset Colors to Libadwaita" wrote the ten colors from the saved Libadwaita theme (a user's edited copy included) and left Custom CSS, the effect sliders, Blur Strength, Shadow Width and the font as they were.
- **Files:** `presentCustomCss()` and `presentTheme()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-appearance.js`; `.sb-custom-css-window` in `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css`; `resetThemeToDefault()` and the `.sb-theme-preset-reset` handler in `public/scripts/power-user.js`; `THEME_RESET_DEFAULTS` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-theme-css.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `#customCSS` is the one store. Its id, value and handlers stay, and it stays in the document in a hidden home (`[data-sb-custom-css-home]` in the UI Theme section) while the Window is closed, because `power-user.js`, saved themes and extensions read it at any time. The Window moves the live field row, the save state (`#custom_css_save_state`) and the AI wand (`#custom_css_ai_wand`) in on open and back on close; recreating any of them would drop the handlers `power-user.js` bound. The old group (`[data-sb-category="custom-css"]`) and its entry in `SB_SETTINGS_SECTION_ORDER.appearance` are gone, so settings search finds the "Custom CSS" row of the theme group, which sits between Accent Colors and the reset row.
  - The Window opens through `openDialog({ tier: 'window' })`, which returns focus to the element that was active when it opened. Safari does not focus a button on a click, so `presentCustomCss()` focuses the row itself when focus is on the body after close.
  - `THEME_RESET_DEFAULTS` is one frozen table. Its theme keys (the ten colors, Custom CSS, the selected theme) equal `default/content/themes/Libadwaita Dark.json`; its effect keys equal the `defaultValue` of `THEME_EFFECT_PROPERTIES`; Blur Strength (13) and Shadow Width (2) equal `default/content/settings.json`. The bundled theme file's own `shadow_width: 0` is a legacy key that no code reads, so it is not the source. The reset must not read the saved theme: the server never overwrites an edited copy of a bundled theme.
  - The reset leaves layout, Chat Display, the visual switches, backgrounds and imported fonts alone; changing that scope changes what "Reset" means to the user.
  - `resetThemeToDefault()` sets the `#themes` select with `trigger('change.select2')`, which refreshes the select row without running the upstream `change` handler (that handler starts a theme switch and its Custom CSS prompt).
  - The reset asks through `openAlert` (Cancel and a destructive Reset) before it writes anything; Escape and the scrim count as Cancel.
- **Tests:** `tests/custom-css-window.e2e.js` (both widths); `tests/unit/sillybunny-theme-reset-defaults.test.js`; "Reset asks first" in `tests/theme-custom-css-import.test.js`; the `settings.customize.appearance` section of `tests/fixtures/settings-inventory.json` (the editor and its two actions are recorded as hidden, because the audit does not open the Window).

### Profile select rows: the title column takes the room the control leaves

- **Breaks:** every row that `createProfileRow()` builds (UI Theme, Font Preset, Connection Profile, the preset rows of the Prompting page) gave its title column `max-width: 180px`, so a subtitle wrapped into a column about 190px wide at 1440px and into four lines at 390px while most of the row was empty. The plain select rows beside them let the subtitle run the row.
- **Files:** the `.ds-row.ds-select-row:has(> .ds-row-suffix > .ds-select-row__menu)` rules in `public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`.
- **Guards:**
  - The title column is `flex: 1 1 var(--sb-row-title-min)` with `max-width: 100%`. The suffix is `flex: 0 1 auto`, between `--sb-row-control-min` plus the 44px menu button and the larger of that and 55% of the row, so the value is cut before the control wraps.
  - The suffix has `margin-inline-start: auto`. Without it a suffix that wrapped onto its own line sat at the start of the line, because it no longer grew; the audit's `content-inset` check on the Presets page caught it at 390px.
- **Tests:** `tests/font-import.e2e.js` (the Preset row's subtitle is one line at 1440px and the title column is more than half the row), and the `content-inset` parity check of `tests/audit/surface-audit.e2e.js` for the wrapped state.

### Security and credentials

- **Breaks:** untrusted card scripts reach remote resources; secrets cross scopes.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/card-script-sandbox/`, `public/scripts/card-script-runtime.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js`, `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - The card-script sandbox CSP blocks network access (`connect-src 'none'`).
  - Sandbox frames have an opaque origin, so `event.origin` cannot be checked. `handleSandboxMessage()` checks `event.source`, the message schema, the nonce, the message ID and the rate limit, then the command allowlist. It rejects unknown commands.
  - Card scripts need user confirmation before they run (`confirmCardScriptExecution()`). This gate applies only to card scripts; other imported behaviour has no general gate.
  - `decodeRawImage()` in `src/transformers-image.js` passes only bytes with a PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP, TIFF, WebP or AVIF signature to jimp. file-type before 21.3.1 loops forever on a 55-byte ASF header with a zero-size sub-header (GHSA-5v7r-6r5c-r473) and blocks the event loop, so one `POST /api/extra/caption` stopped the whole server under Node.js. Test: `tests/transformers-image.test.js`.
  - The other `Jimp.read()` and `Jimp.fromBuffer()` callers (`src/endpoints/avatars.js`, `characters.js`, `content-manager.js`, `image-metadata.js` and `thumbnails.js`) have no signature check, so they depend on the file-type release that `@jimp/core` uses. `@jimp/core` 1.6.0 uses file-type 16.5.4, and its `Jimp.fromBuffer()` did not return within 8 s on that ASF header under Node.js or Bun. `@jimp/core` 1.6.1 uses file-type 21.3.4, and `Jimp.fromBuffer()` and `Jimp.read()` from `src/jimp.js` reject the header with `Could not find MIME for Buffer` on both runtimes.
  - `getPngName()` in `src/endpoints/characters.js` sanitises the name on every platform and drops a trailing `.png`, because `getPngFileName()` keeps that extension and the import route returns the name without it. `writeCharacterData()` writes only a PNG directly inside the user's characters folder (`resolveCharacterAvatarPath()`). The V2/V3 JSON import sanitises the card name as SillyTavern's `importFromJson()` does (`1bb2a5ea1`).
  - Model catalogue fields are untrusted (a provider's model list, a Horde worker list). The select2 model templates in `public/scripts/openai.js`, `textgen-models.js` and `horde.js` build a string that jQuery parses, so each interpolated field goes through `escapeHtml()` from `public/scripts/utils.js`, numbers included: a field typed as a number can arrive as a string. `DOMPurify.sanitize()` is not an escape for this: it keeps markup it allows (`<img src=x onerror=alert(1)>` becomes `<img src="x">`, which still makes a request), and in a `title="..."` attribute it leaves a `"` as it is (`a" onmouseover="alert(1)` comes back unchanged), so a model ID could break out of the attribute. Horde's old `strip()` set `innerHTML` on a detached element, which still loads an `<img>` and runs its `onerror`, and then put the decoded text back into HTML; the template now reduces a field with `plainText()` (an inert `DOMParser` document, `public/scripts/sillybunny-model-details.js`), escapes it where it is interpolated, and links only an `http(s)` address. A new template, or a field added to one, escapes it the same way.
  - The private request filter (`src/private-request-filter.js`, upstream) compares an address with its ranges as written, so it let through `0.0.0.0`, `::` and IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses such as `::ffff:127.0.0.1`, which all reach a service on the server's own host or a private IPv4 host. A hostname from untrusted input (a URL for `/api/search/visit`) can resolve to one of them. `unmapIpv4Address()` in `src/private-address-forms.js` gives the IPv4 host of a mapped address before the range and whitelist checks, and the ranges hold `0.0.0.0/8` and `::/128`. The ranges do not hold carrier-grade NAT (`100.64.0.0/10`, which Tailscale uses), because the global filter would then block a backend that a user reaches there.
- **Tests:** `tests/card-script-runtime.test.js`, `tests/card-script-sandbox-wrapper.test.js`, `tests/openai-prompt-logging-wiring.test.js`, `tests/openai-preset-utils.test.js`, `tests/hostile-file-names-characters.test.js`, `tests/private-request-filter.test.js`, `tests/unit/select2-model-templates.test.js` (every select2 model template: the markup for a normal model equals the markup from before the change, and hostile names, descriptions, numbers and addresses come out as text).
- **Fixes:** #110 (sandbox helpers), #144 (block remote loads from the sandbox), #348 (gate verbose prompt logging), #560 (bind custom endpoint profiles to their saved secrets), #934 (`@jimp/core` 1.6.1 for the other jimp callers).
- **Order matters:** at startup, `readSecretState()` (`public/scripts/secrets.js`) runs before `getSettings()` calls `accountStorage.init()`, and `accountStorage.getItem()` returns `null` until then. Call `checkOpenRouterAuth()` only after `getSettings()` (`public/script.js`). An earlier call does not find the PKCE code verifier and removes the OAuth query from the URL, so the OpenRouter key exchange never happens. SillyTavern moved the call out of `readSecretState()` in `97dba399e`; the SillyTavern 1.18 port (`9fe08ef34`) kept it. Test: `tests/openrouter-oauth-return.test.js`.

### Accounts and session login

- **Breaks:** an account or session route reads a request field, a handle or a server path that it has not checked, or answers before the checks that guard the other routes.
- **Cause:** Express 4 does not catch a rejection from an async route or middleware, so that code must answer its own errors. `toKey()` reads an array as the handle in it, and `getUserDirectories()` joins a handle into a path as it is.
- **Files:** `src/middleware/sessionAuth.js`, `src/server-main.js`, `src/users.js`, `src/endpoints/users-public.js`, `src/endpoints/users-private.js`, `src/endpoints/users-admin.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - `registerSessionAuthRoutes()` in `src/middleware/sessionAuth.js` registers `POST /api/auth/login` and `/api/auth/logout` above basic auth, behind the IP whitelist (when `whitelistMode` is on) and the host whitelist. Login counts failed attempts per client address with `rateLimiting.basicAuthMaxAttempts` over 60 s, answers 400 to a username or password that is not a string, and answers 500 when it fails (`5e0281e7d`). `validateCredentials()` refuses every login while `basicAuthUser.username` or `basicAuthUser.password` is empty, and registering the routes logs one warning for it (`193fba8c2`).
  - `matchesConfiguredUser()` in `src/middleware/sessionAuth.js` compares a single-user login with `basicAuthUser` through `crypto.timingSafeEqual()` over SHA-256 digests, and matches nothing when a configured value is not a string (`abb168c1a`).
  - Every account route answers 400 to a handle that is not a string, and to a password, new password or avatar that is not a string. A route that compares the account's password answers 400 when that password is missing; a login counts a missing password as an empty one (`2f2e50820`).
  - `/change-password`, `/reset-settings` and `/reset-step2` count each password comparison per account with `rateLimiting.accountsLoginMaxAttempts` over 60 s, and check that limit before the password. `/reset-step2` also checks the reset code limit before the password (`e95c02fae`).
  - `/api/users/delete` and `/api/users/backup` answer 404 for a handle that is not an account. A purge or a backup also needs `isUserRootInsideDataRoot()` in `src/users.js`: the account folder must be a folder directly inside the data root (`c9fc49cbd`).
  - Only an admin may use `/import-sillytavern/folder` and `/import-sillytavern/extensions`, which read a folder that the request names on the server (`requireAdminMiddleware`, `8cd9ab2be`). Other users import with the backup ZIP; with accounts off, the default user is an admin. The read check from `29c3a4e18` (`getImportReadCheck()`) stays behind it and passes every path for an admin.
  - The import card shows its Import From Folder Path pane only while `isAdmin()` from `public/scripts/user.js` is true (`updateFolderImportVisibility()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`, `4abda4f55`), so a member does not see the folder import and the extension sync that the server refuses. The card checks when it is built and again on `SETTINGS_LOADED`, because with accounts on, `isAdmin()` is true until the client knows that accounts are on and then false until `/api/users/me` answers, and `getSettings()` does both before it emits that event. `.ds-row` sets `display: flex`, so the check sets `style.display`; the `hidden` attribute does not hide the pane. A change to who may use the folder routes must also change this check and the Welcome check below.
  - The Welcome dialog offers the same folder route (`fillHello()` and `fillImport()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-welcome.js`), so it reads `isAdmin()` as well. An account that is not an admin gets an Import From SillyTavern row with no subtitle and a page with only Import Backup ZIP, because the subtitle and the page text both speak of a folder. Without the check, a member filled in a path, confirmed the replace prompt and read `Forbidden`. The value is final when the dialog opens: a first run opens it inside `getSettings()`, after `setUserControls()` has awaited `/api/users/me`, and every other opening comes later. A failed `/api/users/me` leaves `isAdmin()` false, so the folder import stays hidden. Test: `tests/welcome-startup.e2e.js` (`an account that is not an admin is offered only the backup ZIP, which still imports`, `an admin account keeps the folder import`).
  - An extension sync moves the existing extension aside, and moves it back when the synced copy cannot be moved into place (`77c6012bd`).
  - Before it writes anything, the backup ZIP import answers 400 when sanitize-filename would change a folder or file name of an entry that it imports: a NUL or control character, a character that Windows refuses such as `:`, a reserved device name such as `CON`, or a trailing dot or space (`hasPortableNames()`, `4109a0812`). Under Node, a NUL byte made `createWriteStream()` throw in a yauzl callback, which no route handler reaches, and ended the server.
  - The import also catches a throw from `createWriteStream()`. yauzl's stream for a stored entry emits an error when it is destroyed, so the import listens for that error before `destroy()` (`6b41a15ff`). A failed entry closes the archive, because yauzl closes it only after its last entry or its own error, and removes the file that the entry had opened (`8a4d834ae`). An error with a syscall answers 500 with the generic message; an archive error answers 400 with its message (`2d093e8af`).
  - Keep `yauzl` at `3.3.1` or later (`package.json` pins `^3.4.0`). `yauzl@3.2.1` sets `self.destroyed = true` before `self.push(null)` in `fd-slicer.js`; on Node `>= 26.1.0` (`nodejs/node#62557`, where `Readable.prototype.resume()` is a no-op once destroyed), `pipeline(readStream, writeStream)` in `importZipContents()` (`src/endpoints/users-private.js`) stalls indefinitely on the first entry large enough to trigger backpressure, leaving that file truncated and the uploaded archive in `data/_uploads/`. Test: `tests/users-import-sillytavern.test.js` (`imports deflated and stored binary entries larger than the stream buffer without stalling`).
  - `setUserDataMiddleware()` answers 500 when it cannot read the user record of a session (`132d2a531`).
