- **Tests:** `tests/session-auth-routes.test.js`, `tests/users-request-fields.test.js`, `tests/users-password-attempts.test.js`, `tests/users-folder-guards.test.js`, `tests/users-import-guards.test.js`, `tests/users-import-accounts-off.test.js`, `tests/users-backup-zip-failures.test.js`, `tests/users-extension-sync-swap.test.js`, `tests/users-session-read-failure.test.js`, `tests/folder-import-visibility.e2e.js`, `tests/middleware-session-auth.test.js`, `tests/users-public-routes.test.js`, `tests/users-rate-limits.test.js`, `tests/users-discreet-login.test.js`, `tests/users-admin-routes.test.js`, `tests/users-private-routes.test.js`, `tests/users-import-sillytavern.test.js`, `tests/recover-password.test.js`. Every test in `tests/session-auth-routes.test.js` reloads the server modules, so each one passes `MODULE_RELOAD_TIMEOUT_MS` from `tests/util/module-reload-timeout.js` instead of Jest's 5 s default (`06af52715`).
- **Limit:** `registerSessionAuthRoutes()` calls `getWhitelistMiddleware()` once more, so in Docker `addDockerHostsToWhitelist()` resolves the Docker host names twice at startup (code reading).
- **Limit:** a backup ZIP import that fails part of the way keeps the files that it wrote before the failed entry, and the folders that it made for that entry.
- **Limit:** `tests/folder-import-visibility.e2e.js` runs its cases with accounts on only when `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_ACCOUNTS_URL` names a server with accounts on and `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_ADMIN_HANDLE` and `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_MEMBER_HANDLE` name its accounts, with `_PASSWORD` variables for accounts that have a password. A run against one server skips those cases, and skips the single-user case when that server has accounts on.
- **Backup ZIP trust:** the backup ZIP import (`/import-sillytavern/zip`) restores every file in the account folders that it imports, extensions included, and the account's `settings.json` and `secrets.json`. The client loads the restored third-party extensions after the import reloads the page. A backup ZIP is therefore trusted input, like an extension install, and the import does not filter file types.
- **Text checks:** many unit suites read `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` as text and look for exact strings. `tests/conversation-mode-character-selection.test.js` looks for the file's `'../script.js'` import line as it is, so the folder pane check imports `event_types` and `eventSource` from `./events.js` on a line of its own (`4abda4f55`). After you change a file, run the suites that `git grep -l <file name> -- tests/` lists.

### CI and workflows

- **Breaks:** a workflow change merged to `staging` has no effect on pull requests, or the `staging` and `release` copies of a workflow drift apart.
- **Files:** `.github/workflows/pr-auto-manager.yml`, `.github/pr-auto-comments.yml`, `.github/pr-auto-labels-by-branch.yml`, `.github/workflows/pr-metadata.yml`, `.github/workflows/pr-checks.yml`, `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, `.github/workflows/design-reference.yml`.
- **Guards:**
  - `pull_request_target` workflows (`pr-auto-manager.yml`, which reads `pr-auto-comments.yml` and `pr-auto-labels-by-branch.yml`) and scheduled workflows (`nightly.yml`) run from `release`, the default branch. Their changes also need an `actions/*` PR into `release`; `pr-metadata.yml` limits those branches to `.github/`, `tests/`, `scripts/bump-version.js` and `scripts/update-changelog-merged-prs.js`.
  - `pull_request` workflows (`pr-checks.yml`, `pr-metadata.yml`) run from the PR's merge ref, so a PR into `release` uses the `release` copy unless the PR changes it.
  - Required check names come from rulesets; renaming a job such as `✅ Check Bun Runtime` blocks every PR.
  - `push` workflows run from the pushed commit, so `design-reference.yml`, which runs on a push to `staging`, needs no `actions/*` PR. Its `workflow_dispatch` Run button appears only once the file is on `release`, the default branch.
  - A step in the Bun job (`run-bun-checks` in `pr-checks.yml`) has only what the root `bun install` provides, because the job installs nothing in `tests/`. `bun test` maps `@jest/globals` to its own test API, so a test file that also runs under Jest works there (`tests/character-index.test.js`).
- **Tests:** `tests/pr-auto-manager-workflow.test.js`, `tests/pr-checks-workflow.test.js`, `tests/nightly-workflow.test.js`, `tests/release-workflows.test.js`, `tests/design-reference-workflow.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-launcher-smol.test.js`.
- **Fixes:** #888, #892, #895 and #903 carried workflow and test changes to `release` through `actions/*` branches. `label-by-branches` in `pr-auto-manager.yml` waited for a `synchronize` event with `changes.base`, which never arrives; a base-branch change is an `edited` event. On 2026-10-23 the `release` copy of `pr-metadata.yml` still rejected `hotfix:` titles.
- **Runner images:** GitHub moves `ubuntu-latest` to Ubuntu 26.04 between 2026-10-19 and 2026-11-19 (actions/runner-images#14748), so a job can land on either image. `@playwright/test` 1.60.0 refuses to install Chromium on Ubuntu 26.04, which broke `Install Chromium` in nightly (issue #920) and in `Run Chromium E2E suite` on #925. Playwright 1.61.0 added Ubuntu 26.04 support; `tests/package.json` pins 1.64.1. A Playwright bump needs `npx playwright install chromium` in `tests/` locally, because each version expects its own browser build.

### Playwright tests

- **Breaks:** a wait passes at once, so the test checks state too early or proves nothing.
- **Cause:** `page.waitForFunction()` takes a returned Promise as a truthy result (checked with `@playwright/test` 1.64.1), so an `async` predicate never waits. Read module values into `window` first, then wait on a synchronous predicate, as `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` does.
- **Open:** `tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js` and `tests/pathfinder-settings.e2e.js` still wait on `async` predicates.
- **A timed window that includes work of the server:** `round 1 character opening measurement 390` in `tests/mobile-character-toggle-and-panels.e2e.js` samples 1700 ms from the tap and wants more than ten frames from 700 ms after the list first shows. On a data root that has never served the three bundled avatars, the first request for each thumbnail makes it (about 700 ms), and the list mounts after them, so run alone it saw the list at about 1000 ms and sampled five frames; later tests on the same server found the thumbnails made and passed, so it passed in a shared run and failed alone. Requesting the thumbnails before the tap (in the test, not the product) takes the server's work out of the measurement. Why the list's modules are requested only after the thumbnails finish was not found (no long task and no pending resource in between), so it is open as a possible product delay on a first run.
- **Breaks:** a startup check passes with 4 workers and times out with 8 or more. All workers share one server, so app start takes longer as the worker count goes up. The `#preloader` check in `exerciseUpgrade()` (`tests/topbar-upgrade.e2e.js`) used the 5 s `expect` default. On a 16-thread host, 17 of its 18 test runs with 8 or 12 workers (6 tests, three suite runs) failed there. All 6 tests passed with 1 and 4 workers. The check now has a 20 s timeout. `tests/mobile-shell-smoke.e2e.js` sets a 60 s test timeout for the same cause. Give startup waits an explicit timeout instead of the 5 s `expect` default.
- **Breaks:** a spec that you run alone against a fresh data root times out at startup, but passes in the full suite.
- **Cause:** a fresh data root stops startup at the first-run Welcome dialog (it replaced the upstream onboarding popup) until a persona is named. `waitForAppReady()` in `tests/frontend/frontent-test-utils.js` answers it through `answerFirstRunWelcome()` (`tests/helpers/welcome-dialog.js`): it names the persona, unticks Show at Startup and presses Start, and it throws if the upstream popup appears instead, because that means Welcome failed to load. Every other boot keeps Welcome closed: `keepWelcomeClosed()` (a context route from `protectMaintainerServer()`, `awaitST()` and `goST()`) and `applyWelcomeSettings()` / `applyHomeTestSettings()` (for specs that serve `/api/settings/get` themselves) give the account a seen version and Show at Startup `'false'`. An account that never saw Welcome opens it once, and a stubbed settings save never stores the seen version, so without them it opened on every boot. `tests/unit/welcome-helper-coverage.test.js` fails when a spec or helper sets `firstRun` to false without one of these helpers. Before Welcome, the `app_ready` waits in `tests/character-list.e2e.js`, `tests/character-folders.e2e.js` and `tests/character-create-flow.e2e.js` did not answer onboarding, so they passed only after an earlier spec had answered it on the same server.
- **Open:** `tests/character-folders.e2e.js` passed on a fresh data root on 2026-11-01; `character-list` and `character-create-flow` were not run on one.
- **Breaks:** work that the app starts when a chat loads runs on its own timers and can land between a test step and its assertion. The failure is rare locally and can still fail a single nightly run (issue #920, see § In-chat agents and companions). To reproduce such a race, hold the request that starts the timer chain with `page.route()` and release it after the test step.
- **Breaks:** a test step can land between an app timer and the frame that the timer queued. `tests/chat-scroll-regressions.e2e.js` "chat-load bottom lock yields when the view moves above the last pin" failed in one nightly run (issue #993) with the end state that this race gives. On a Windows PC a step that Node sends never arrived inside that gap: in 32 runs with the scroll moved onto a settle timer, the scroll came 2.7 ms plus a multiple of about 16 ms after the timer, so the queued frame had already run. To reproduce such a race on any system, start a page timer with the delay of the app timer right after the app starts its timers: it runs right after the app timer and before the next frame (`scrollMessageNearTopBeforeQueuedChatLoadPin()` in `tests/chat-scroll-regression-helpers.js`).
- **Backend-dependent fixtures:** tests that open Chat Completion prompt rows (`.sb-prompt-row`) fail when the server's saved `main_api` is another backend, because those rows do not render. `tests/in-chat-agents-list.e2e.js` and `tests/sillybunny-mobile-overlays.e2e.js` force `main_api: "openai"` through settings interception (`028a5c62e`). `tests/world-info-editor.e2e.js` (q56) still depends on the saved backend.
- **Active-character assertions:** Chat History's subtitle names the character selected in the server's saved settings, which differs between data roots. Read the selected character's name from the page context instead of hard-coding it; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js` does this.
- **Source-text tests:** a unit test that reads module source and matches it with regular expressions fails when the module splits or the markup it names is removed, and then it says nothing about behaviour. The Chat Archive presentation tests did this and failed on the `ui.js` split into `ui/*.js`; they were replaced by model and DOM tests (`tests/unit/sillybunny-chats-archive-model.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-chats-archive-ui.test.js`). A test that must read source reads every module in the directory, as `tests/unit/native-dialog-replacements.test.js` does.
- **Route handlers after a test ends:** a handler that calls `route.fetch()` and then reads the body throws `Response has been disposed` when the test ends while the request is in flight. It showed as a load flake in `tests/preset-lorebook-dialogs.e2e.js`. Fetch through `tests/helpers/route-fetch.js` (`fetchJsonOrSkip`, `fetchBodyOrSkip`). It returns `null` only for teardown errors and rethrows any other error (`3754089e5`, `09b66af39`).
- **Reduced-motion overrides hidden by the theme:** under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, the universal rule in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` (`@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { *, *::before, *::after { … } }`) sets `animation: none !important` on every element. A component's own reduced-motion rule that loses on specificity therefore still looks right in a test. The splash dots' rule did (`.sb-boot-dots > span` against `.sb-boot-dots > span:nth-child(n)`). Test such a rule with the theme's global rule removed from the CSSOM, as `tests/splash.e2e.js` does, and assert that a rule was actually removed.
  - **A hidden-until-animated style fails closed:** that global rule, and `body.reduced-motion *` in the same sheet, remove a `forwards` fill, so an element that starts hidden and is shown only by its animation's end state stays hidden. The splash letters and sparkles did when the app's Reduced Motion class arrived while the splash was up. Make the finished state the base style and let the animation only hold the element hidden until its turn (a `backwards` fill with `steps(1, end)`), as `public/css/loader.css` does. Test: `tests/splash.e2e.js` (the whole line and every sparkle after `body.reduced-motion` is added mid-reveal; the last letter and the sparkles hidden before their turn).
- **Specs that save the server's settings change later specs:** `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` connected a fake Custom model and saved it into the test server's settings, so later boots reported "Status check bypassed" and Getting Started counted Connect a Model as done (see "Live signals undo stubbed state"). It now uses `protectMaintainerServer` and lets only `/api/chats/save` through, because its first test is about the real save's integrity check; a full run leaves `settings.json` byte-identical. `tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js` and `tests/card-script-sandbox.e2e.js` saved settings the same way (a NanoGPT source, model and preset file, fake keys in `secrets.json`, `allow_card_scripts: true`); they now use `protectMaintainerServer`, and `keepSavesInPage()` (`tests/helpers/saves-in-page.js`) replays a spec's own settings, preset and secret saves on its next read, so a save-reload-check spec still works and a full run leaves `settings.json` and `secrets.json` byte-identical.
- **Saved reduced motion:** the shared data root keeps `power_user.reduced_motion`. One run saved `true`, and then 8 motion assertions in `tests/character-create-flow.e2e.js`, `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` and `tests/characters-navigation.e2e.js` failed at every commit until the value was reset. A spec that asserts motion sets `reduced_motion: false` through settings interception, as `tests/sheet-swipe.e2e.js` does. A run under system reduced motion is enough to cause it: `switchReducedMotion()` (`public/scripts/power-user.js`) sets `power_user.reduced_motion` to `true` when `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` matches, and the next settings save keeps it in the data root, so a Playwright context with `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` turns motion off for later runs on that root. Run those cases on a fresh root or last.
- **Open:** `tests/characters-navigation.e2e.js` still reads the saved value. `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` and `tests/character-create-flow.e2e.js` set `reduced_motion: false` through settings interception; the create flow's two `flash` class tests failed on any data root that had saved `true`, because `flashHighlight` is skipped under reduced motion and `emulateMedia` does not override the setting.
- **Closing popups:** with motion on, a closing `dialog.popup` keeps `[open]` with `[closing]` until it is removed. A locator such as `dialog.popup[open]` then matches the closing popup and the next one, and fails in strict mode. Match `dialog.popup[open]:not([closing])`, use `.last()`, or wait for the old popup to be removed (`7abe38b07`; `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js`, `tests/persona-editor.e2e.js`).
- **Tests must not depend on saved chats:** the Home specs once picked characters by `character.chat`, so they passed only on a server where those characters already had a saved chat (they failed on a fresh data root and passed after a probe saved one by accident). `tests/helpers/fixture-chats.js` (`installFixtureChats`, `pickFixtureCharacters`, `registerFixtureChat`) picks any characters with an avatar, gives each a synthetic chat, and answers `/api/chats/get`, `/api/characters/chats` and `/api/characters/last-chat` for them; `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` and `tests/home-mode-memory.e2e.js` use it and passed against an emulated server with no saved chats. `protectMaintainerServer` (`tests/helpers/maintainer-server.js`) also answers `/api/characters/last-chat` as the server does (200, `OK`), so specs that use it no longer write `entity-last-chat.json` on the data root, and the request event still fires (`tests/maintainer-server.e2e.js`). Specs that do not use it, such as `tests/swipe-picker.e2e.js` and `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive.e2e.js`, still write there.
- **The open action sheet:** `openActionMenu` builds one phone sheet per opening, and a closing sheet stays in the DOM (with `hidden` set at once) until its exit ends. When a menu item opens a second menu, a bare `.sb-action-sheet` locator matches both and fails in strict mode, and `:visible` also matches the leaving one. Use `OPEN_ACTION_SHEET` (`.sb-action-sheet:not([hidden])`) from `tests/helpers/action-sheet.js`; `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` (export at 375px) failed this way after `d0174252b`, fixed in `1902aa447`.
  - **Desktop popovers:** `closeActionMenu()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js` also sets `hidden` on a closing `.sb-action-popover` at once and keeps the node, inert, until its fade ends (150 to 280 ms in local runs), so the next menu can open while it is still in the DOM. Playwright's role matching does not skip inert nodes, so `getByRole('menuitem')` matches the leaving menu's items as well. The export test at 1440px in `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` failed this way in most runs; it now finds the menu and its items through `.sb-action-popover:not([hidden])`.
- **Bundled characters ask to import their lorebook:** opening a bundled character with an embedded World/Lorebook shows the import prompt (`checkEmbeddedWorld`, `public/scripts/world-info.js:≈6466`), which covers the editor. With `/api/settings/save` stubbed, its "seen" flag never persists, so it returns in every test on a fresh data root. Answer `/api/settings/get` with `power_user.world_import_dialog = false`, as `tests/character-editor-rendering.e2e.js` does. The character list also fills about a second after the preloader goes; wait for `characters.length > 0` before selecting an index. `tests/swipe-picker.e2e.js` (whose fixture selects the first character with an avatar) does the same, and `tests/action-menu-viewport.e2e.js` declines the prompt through its button with `selectSampleCharacter()`: closing it with `dialog.close()` left it half closed, and its late focus move to `#send_textarea` closed the message menu a few milliseconds after it opened. `tests/characters-navigation.e2e.js` (11 of its 12 tests failed on the prompt) now answers `/api/settings/get` with `world_import_dialog = false`. A fresh data root holds Seraphina and the Guide (`default/content/index.json`), neither with an embedded lorebook; a data root that already has a card with a book, such as a Nahida card from an earlier version, still shows the prompt.
- **Request checks need the request event:** the browser's resource-timing buffer holds a limited number of entries, so a check that reads `performance.getEntriesByType('resource')` after a full app boot can miss a request and pass by mistake. Record requests with `page.on('request')` instead, as `tests/backgrounds-tabs-query.e2e.js` does for stray requests for the app document.
- **Opening a recent chat passes through the character's default chat:** `openRecentCharacterChat()` (`public/scripts/welcome-screen.js`) calls `selectCharacterById()`, which loads the character's default chat, and only then opens the requested file. With chat saves stubbed, that default chat gets a freshly minted name (`src/endpoints/characters.js` names a chat on read when none is saved). A test that reads `getCurrentChatId()` once, right after Home closes, can see the in-between chat under load; `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` ("a Recent Chats entry opens its chat") failed this way at 1024×600 with 3 workers. Poll until the requested chat is current.
  - **Same race in the Home mode switcher:** `resumeLatestRoleplayChat()` (`public/scripts/welcome-screen.js:1099`) ends in `openRecentCharacterChat()`, so Home turns off while the minted default chat is current, and `openCharacterChat()` (`public/script.js:12568`) swaps in the requested file 10 to 75 ms later, after its flush and `clearChat()` awaits. A frame sampler on a run of `tests/home-mode-memory.e2e.js` ("choosing Roleplay on Home opens the chat with the newest last message and ignores pins") saw `Assistant Tewi - <time>` first and `qa-fixture-2` next in all 24 traces that were parsed. The test read the chat once after `expectChat()` and failed in 5 of 11 runs of it alone at 1440×900 plus 390×844, on `78138c6f6` and `d84385344` alike, always at 390×844; two whole-file runs passed. It now polls with `expect.poll()`; 18 of 18 runs of it alone passed.
- **Settings save stubs need a version token:** a stub that answers `/api/settings/save` with `{}` makes `saveSettings()` throw "Settings save returned an invalid version token" (`public/script.js:12949`). The app then shows the error toast "Settings could not be saved" (`:12960`), and under load that toast can land on top of the element a test touches or clicks.
  - **Breaks:** `tests/foundation/message-actions.e2e.js` (press-and-hold on the phone project) failed in 12 to 15 of 18 runs at 3 workers, on PR #870 and on its base alike, because the CDP touch hit the toast instead of the Edit button.
  - **Guard:** answer with `{ version: ++settingsVersion }`, as `tests/foundation/conversation-mode.e2e.js` and `tests/foundation/message-actions.e2e.js` do. `protectMaintainerServer()` already returns a valid token.
  - **Open:** `tests/foundation/shared-ui.e2e.js:63` and `tests/foundation/shell-navigation.e2e.js:93` and `:120` still answer `{}`.
- **The Expressions worker owns `#visual-novel-wrapper`:** `moduleWorker` runs every 2 seconds (`UPDATE_INTERVAL`, `public/scripts/extensions/expressions/index.js:55`, `:3510`) and, while a character chat is open outside visual-novel mode, sets an inline `display: none` on the wrapper (`:544–558`). It returns early when no character is selected, so a test on Home never sees it.
  - **Breaks:** `tests/side-panel-engine.e2e.js` forced the wrapper visible with an inline `display`, and with a chat open the worker hid it again within a few hundred milliseconds, so the visual-novel geometry assertions read a 0×0 rect.
  - **Guard:** hold it visible with an injected stylesheet rule (`#visual-novel-wrapper { display: flex !important; }`) and remove the rule in cleanup.
- **Service workers hide requests from `page.route()`:** when the service worker answers a request, Playwright does not run the route, so a test that fails a module import is not failed. Use `serviceWorkers: 'block'` in that test. Test: `tests/sillybunny-chats-archive-layout.e2e.js` (a failed archive import shows `Could not open Chat Archive.` and the next open retries).
- **Overlays take focus a frame after they open:** action-menu sheets and popovers move focus in a `requestAnimationFrame` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-sheet.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`) and handle Escape on the overlay itself, so an Escape sent right after the click that opens one can be lost, and the open backdrop then catches the next click. Wait for focus inside the overlay before sending keys, and for the overlay's removal before the next click (`escapeOpenMenu()` in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js`; the 360×640 "empty footer space" test went from 2 failures in 12 repeats to 12 of 12). `page.clock.setFixedTime` installs Playwright's whole injected clock, which by default also fakes `requestAnimationFrame`, timers and `performance` (`tests/node_modules/playwright-core/lib/coreBundle.js`, 1.64.1), so it delays those frames and widens the race: pin the clock only inside the test that needs it, as the Home day and night hero test does. The mobile action sheet's Close test in `tests/conversation-message-actions.e2e.js` failed about one run in five until it waited for focus inside the sheet before pressing Escape.
- **Live signals undo stubbed state:** Getting Started marks steps done from live events (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/getting-started-signals.js`), so a spec that stubs the stored checklist still sees Connect a Model done when the data root's saved connection reports a status at boot, and Get a Character Card done when the library holds a user card. After `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` saved a Custom source into the test server's settings (Custom reports "Status check bypassed" before any request), 20 of 30 tests in `tests/getting-started.e2e.js` and the Getting Started counts in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` and `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` failed.
  - **Guard:** `stubGettingStarted()` in `tests/helpers/getting-started-state.js` forces `main_api: "openai"`, the OpenAI source and `bypass_status_check: false`, and answers the status check with `{ error: true }`. With `bundledCardsOnly` it also keeps only the bundled cards, for the specs that assert a count. A spec with its own `/api/settings/get` route applies the same through `applyGettingStartedSettings()`.
  - **Default:** an account with no stored checklist counts as an existing one and starts hidden, so `installFixtureChats()` stubs a shown checklist.
- **`page.evaluate()` runs with a user gesture:** `navigator.userActivation.isActive` stays true for a few seconds afterwards, so a check gated on a gesture (Getting Started's preset step) passes from any `page.evaluate()`. To emit without one, wait until `isActive` is false and call CDP `Runtime.evaluate` with `userGesture: false` (`emitWithoutGesture` in `tests/getting-started.e2e.js`).
- **A grep run is not a regression run:** after changing shared behaviour, run the whole spec files that exercise it. Welcome stopped closing on Escape on its first page, and `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` "every window leaves no unmarked copy placeholder behind" (which closed every window with Escape) failed only in a full run, because the `--grep "Welcome|Help|About|…"` runs during development did not match its title. It now closes Welcome with its Close button. Again after the shortcut registry lost its `planned` entries: `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` ("leaves out the shortcuts that have no handler yet") still expected 6 General rows and failed at both viewports, and only a review grep for the window's name found it.
- **Waiting for CSS transitions can hang:** a transition on an element that is not painted (a tab button or avatar behind a modal) can end without its `finished` promise resolving, so `settleTransitions` in `tests/helpers/dialog-accessibility.js` waited the full 30 s test timeout and broke the with-motion runs of first-run Welcome. It now waits only for transitions whose `playState` is `running`, each for at most 1000 ms.
- **A programmatic focus that lands later closes a popover the test opened too early:** the Conversation workspace focuses its input in a `requestAnimationFrame` once its stylesheet is in (`revealConversationWorkspaceAfterStylesheet`) and again 100 ms after `openConversationWorkspaceForAvatar` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-conversation/chrome.js`), and a Home card's Assistant Menu is a popover at desktop widths that closes when focus moves outside it (`handlePopoverFocusOut`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`). Run alone, "a new Roleplay chat closes the Conversation workspace that is open" (`tests/home-assistants.e2e.js`) opened the menu between the two: the item took focus at 5935 ms, the delayed focus moved it to the input 1 ms later, and the menu was removed (a focus and menu-mutation log; with a 400 ms wait before the menu opened, 4 of 4 passed). It timed out at 90 s at 1440×900 in 5 of 5 alone runs and passed in parallel runs, where the page is slower. Phones use an action sheet, which focus-out does not close, so 390×844 passed. The input has focus after the first call, so waiting for focus is not enough, and the page shows nothing when the second one is done; the test now opens the menu again until the item can be clicked (`expect(...).toPass()`), as a person would. Passed 10 of 10 alone at both sizes; with the workspace no longer closed by the new Roleplay chat, it fails at both.
- **Known product defects in the accessibility walk:** a surface lists them as `knownBugs` (`tests/helpers/dialog-accessibility-surfaces.js`), and the walk marks that check `test.fail`, so it turns red when the defect is fixed; delete the entry then. The four found when the walk was added (row edges and the switch ring in forced colours, and words breaking at 200% text in Help and in phone rows) are fixed. None is listed now: the last one, the `home-page` surface's clipped labels at 390×844 and 200% text, was fixed by stacking the Home tiles (`4d5a9d246`).
- **Quick matrix:** the Home specs run their tests at several window sizes (`tests/home-layout.e2e.js` at six), so a full run is long. `SB_QUICK_MATRIX=1 npm run test:e2e -- <spec>` (from `tests/`) trims the sizes through `tests/helpers/matrix.js`. Tests that measure layout or follow a desktop or phone branch run at 1440×900 and 390×844 (`tests/home-layout.e2e.js` also at 360×640, and drops 1920, 1024 and 768). Tests that do not depend on the size run at 1440×900 only. Without the variable every spec registers the same tests in every run. The full matrix also runs the plain-`test` geometry tests of `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` at 450×800 (the sheet boundary) and 844×390, listed as `GEOMETRY_ONLY_SIZES` in `tests/helpers/matrix.js` and passed as the third argument of `sizeFreeTestFor`; a quick run leaves them out.
  - **Breaks:** quick mode skips sizes, so a layout change can pass a quick run and fail at a skipped size.
  - **Guard:** CI (`nightly.yml`, `release-e2e.yml`) runs the full matrix, and no workflow sets the variable. Run the full matrix of a spec once before you hand a change over.
  - **Choosing `test` or `sizeFreeTest`:** `sizeFreeTest` (from `sizeFreeTestFor(test, options.viewport)`) is for a test that asserts state, text, focus, roles or requests. Use plain `test` for a test that reads a rect or a computed layout, a sheet or floating presentation, or an art scale, because those answers change with the window. When unsure, use `test`: it only adds passes to a quick run, while `sizeFreeTest` in the wrong place hides a failure from every run that sets the variable. `tests/.eslintrc.cjs` lists `sizeFreeTest` as an alias of `test`, so the Playwright rules still apply to its body. At a size a quick run skips, `sizeFreeTest` and its `.skip`, `.only`, `.fixme`, `.fail` and `.describe` forms register nothing (`tests/unit/e2e-matrix-helper.test.js`).
  - **Trimmed to the desktop whole:** `tests/home-mode-memory.e2e.js`, `tests/managed-updates.e2e.js` and `tests/temporary-chat.e2e.js` run at 1440×900 only. `tests/request-errors.e2e.js` has no sizes; a quick run keeps the first row of each API path.
  - **Always run in full:** the "Home shell width" describe (1440 and 1920) and the describes with a fixed size in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js`, `tests/home-windows.e2e.js`, `tests/splash.e2e.js` and `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js`. The `Splash at <size>` loop in `tests/splash.e2e.js` is not trimmed either.
- **Frame timings in WebKit start too early:** WebKit keeps the main thread busy for several seconds after `APP_READY` (5 to 9 s in the Playwright 1.64.1 WebKit container, at `f6c1b217b` as well), so its first frames come seconds apart. A test that counts the frames or frame gaps of a motion it starts then measures that boot work as a stall. In the WebKit container the first one to three frames of a page push can still stall, depending on the host's state, so `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js` used to fail its painted-frames floor in about one run in ten (today's height motion painted 5 to 8 frames of a long page in WebKit). With the size motion (see "Size motion of a content-sized window") it paints 14 to 18, passed 156 of 156 runs in 6 repeats alone and is in `DIALOG_SPECS` (so also in the Firefox config); its one CDP test skips itself.
  - **Guard:** `waitForQuietFrames(page)` (`tests/helpers/quiet-frames.js`) waits for 10 frames in a row under 50 ms, and throws with the latest gaps when they do not come. `tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js` calls it before it installs its transition probe.
  - **CDP is Chromium only:** `page.context().newCDPSession()` works only in Chromium-based browsers (Playwright documents it so), so a test that throttles the CPU through it skips there (`tests/welcome-transitions.e2e.js`, "Troubleshooting still ends at its rest height when the page is slow to render"). Getting Started ("that the app applies without a gesture of the user…") and the two pixel-ratio cases of `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js` skip in WebKit for the same reason; the reload step of Getting Started's save test stays, and only its last step needs a script without a gesture.
- **Home specs in WebKit:** the 17 Home, Welcome and splash specs that are not in `DIALOG_SPECS` showed these differences from Chromium. Each is a test assumption, not a product defect, except the last.
  - **Focus scrolls later:** `focus()` on a button inside a clipped, sideways-scrolling row (the Recent Chats filters) scrolls the row in the next rendering update in WebKit, and inside the call in Chromium. Tab reaches the buttons in Playwright WebKit (real Safari skips buttons unless its Tab setting is on, which this test does not cover). Poll `scrollLeft` (`tests/home-layout.e2e.js`).
  - **Observers attach later:** the hero art starts watching its band when its layout runs, a frame after the panel appears, so a count of observed elements read at once is one short in WebKit. Poll it (`waitUntilFollowed` in `tests/home-observers.e2e.js`).
  - **Programmatic focus shows a ring:** a dialog's first button, focused by script after a mouse click, matches `:focus-visible` in WebKit and not in Chromium, so its inset ring changes the pixels next to it. A contrast test that samples pixels beside a chip blurs the active element first (`tests/getting-started.e2e.js`).
  - **Clipboard:** Playwright WebKit has no `clipboard-write` permission, so use `trackClipboard` and `copiedText` (`tests/helpers/browser-neutral.js`).
  - **Splash screenshots need one line:** the splash picks its line at random, and the line's width moves the picture by up to a device pixel, so two pages that share a screenshot clip pass `sameLine` to `openSplash` (`tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js`).
  - **Splash loop edge, a product difference:** at a fractional device scale WebKit dropped the first device row or column of the first loop frame (Reisen's top row and Tewi's left column at 1.5, Tewi at 1.25 and 1.75), because `.sb-boot-loop { overflow: hidden }` clips at an edge that is rounded to a device pixel, and the still has no clip. `.sb-boot-loop` is now clipped with `clip-path: inset(-1px)` and no overflow clip (`public/css/loader.css`). Clip a box that must show every edge pixel with an outset `clip-path`, never `overflow: hidden`. The outset is one CSS pixel because rounding moves an edge by less than one device pixel, which is at most one CSS pixel at a scale of 1 and above: `-0.5px` fixed 1.5 and 1.75 but still lost Tewi's column at 1.25. Nothing can show in the outset: all 21 frame pictures are the scene's size at 0,0, and each effect is an `svg` of the scene's size that clips itself. Below a scale of about 0.75 the first-frame check still fails with the outset (also at `-2px`), so the check covers 1 to 3. Tests: `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` ("looks like its still at scale …" for 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2 and 3 in Chromium and WebKit).
- **Fitted text boxes settle late in WebKit:** About ▸ Troubleshooting's info box is fitted to its text by a ResizeObserver (`fitToText`). Chromium has the fitted height at the first sample; WebKit runs the callback about 100 ms after the page shows, so a one-shot read sees the `rows` height and reports an overflow that is not there. Poll the measurement (`tests/splash.e2e.js`, the boot marks in Troubleshooting).
- **Forced colours in tests:** forced colours keep a background colour's alpha and drop every `box-shadow`, so a computed colour can mislead and an edge or state drawn only with a shadow disappears. Measure contrast from the rendered pixels (`readTextContrast` in `tests/helpers/page-contrast.js`), and give a shadow-only edge or state an outline or border under `forced-colors: active`. Found that way and fixed: the greeting picture's edge and focus ring (borders of the same box in `CanvasText` and `Highlight`, `public/css/sillybunny-home.css`; `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js`), toasts (an outline, because a toast rule sets `border: none !important`; `tests/request-errors.e2e.js`) and an `aria-invalid` field (a double border, since forced colours have no error colour, with `!important` because entry rows set their border with an id selector; `tests/connection-notices.e2e.js`). Open: the persona entry and tag-manager name fields have their own `aria-invalid` rules (`sillybunny-persona-detail.css`, `sillybunny-tag-manager.css`) and likely the same problem. In Playwright WebKit, `forcedColors: 'active'` makes the media query match but applies no palette (authored colours stay, `Canvas` stays white), and real Safari never matches it (WebKit bug 225281): a test of the palette replacing authored colours skips in WebKit, while a test of the app's own `@media (forced-colors: active)` rules still runs there (`tests/splash.e2e.js`, `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js`).
- **Measuring toasts:** `protectMaintainerServer()` hides toasts until `showToasts(page)`, and a message that times out can vanish mid-measure, so a test that reads a message's geometry keeps it on screen. A global `toastr.options.timeOut = 0` does that for a toast, which keeps the timing of its call (`tests/request-errors.e2e.js`). An error or a warning notification stays by itself, but an info or a success notification takes its 4 seconds unless the call sets `timeOut: 0` itself, because a global write is not the call's own value (`timingOf()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-toast-motion.js`; `tests/toast-notification-layout.e2e.js` passes the options in every call).
- **A message with an action is held by a press on touch:** touch sends no hover while the finger is down (the compatibility mouse events come after the release), so `holdWhilePressed` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-toast-motion.js` runs toastr's own `mouseenter` and `mouseleave` handlers on `pointerdown`, `pointerup` and `pointercancel` of a non-mouse pointer inside a message of either container (`#toast-container > *`, `#sb-toast-overlay > *`). Chromium's touch emulation holds the message without it, so `tests/toast-queue.e2e.js` ("with a finger") cannot show the fault; `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-queue.test.js` ("pressed on touch") fails without the listener. The Prompt Editor's Remove From List message (with Undo) is the shared toastr layer, not an own element: a message whose unescaped markup holds a button is high priority and shows at once (`isHighPriority()`), and with one button and one line it is a toast.
- **Wait for a lazily built page before tabbing through it:** `tests/keyboard-shell.e2e.js` "Customize: no Tab stop without a box" pressed Tab while `#sb-appearance-content` was still `aria-busy`; with 49 of its 158 controls mounted, Tab ran past the last one onto the body. It failed 6 of 6 at a 6x CPU throttle and passed alone at low load. A test that walks the Tab order of a settings page first waits for the page's content to lose `aria-busy`.
- **Groups in dialogs have no border:** `.sb-dialog .ds-pref-group` drew a `1px` border and its own fill over the base group, so every dialog group (Quick Reply editor, regex debugger, extension dialogs) differed from a settings group. The rule is gone and the base `.ds-pref-group` (card fill, Card Shadow, border `0`) applies. Specs that pinned the box by its border (`tests/window-dialogs.e2e.js` "Regex debugger has one outer scroller", the agent list's `descriptionRight` in `tests/in-chat-agents-list.e2e.js`, which moves by the two pixels of border) now measure the shadow and the new widths.
- **Firefox runs:** `npm run test:e2e:firefox` (from `tests/`, against a running server, in the Playwright container) runs `tests/playwright.firefox.config.js`: the WebKit config's `DIALOG_SPECS` plus `welcome-transitions`.
  - **Breaks:** after every server start, the first load of each browser context reloads twice to clear old caches (`sillybunny-restart-cache-clear.js`). Playwright Firefox's `goto({ waitUntil: 'domcontentloaded' })` resolves on the first document, so the next call into the page fails with "Execution context was destroyed" in `waitForAppReady`. Chromium resolves after the last reload.
  - **Guard:** the global setup `tests/helpers/restart-cache-clear-seed.js` seeds `sillybunny_cache_boot` and `sillybunny_cache_cleared` in the storage state, reading the names from the page script the server serves; `tests/restart-cache-clear-seed.test.js` ties them to the middleware.
  - **Not runnable in Playwright Firefox,** listed with reasons in the config: drags past the bottom edge of the viewport (Firefox delivers no mouse events there), `page.setViewportSize()` before the first navigation in an `isMobile` context (it never settles), a computed font size compared as a string (Gecko serialises 1/64 px), and `:focus-visible` after a lone Shift press. Tab at the last control of a Popup modal does not wrap without browser controls to take the focus; that one is an open question for a headed Firefox.
  - **Limits:** headless Firefox and Xvfb draw in software, so a problem in the compositor does not show, and Firefox's Playwright video capture tears even on a plain test page.
- **Rows taller than their estimate shift a click in WebKit:** `contain-intrinsic-size` is a content-box size, so a `.ds-entity-row` estimated at `auto 52px` took up 65px (padding and border come on top) until it was drawn, while real rows are 59px to 94px. A row that was never drawn grew or shrank when first drawn, which moved every row below it after a click was aimed, and WebKit has no scroll anchoring. In `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js` the ⋮ press then landed on the list, so "Delete chat file" never appeared (WebKit only, 1440 and 390). Almost every entity list had it: the height of 40 rows moved by 200 to 360px when scrolled to the end (tag and folder members, persona list, Data Bank, Chats Archive, group member candidates). `.ds-entity-row` now draws every row; only `.sb-character-list__rows > .ds-entity-row` keeps `content-visibility: auto`, because a relayout of about 970 rows took about 340ms with every row drawn against about 40ms with it (Chromium, `list.characters` with its rows cloned), and it carries the estimate of each row form (`public/css/sillybunny-character-list.css`: 56px, and 242px or 203px for a grid card). `tests/tag-manager.e2e.js` ("a long member list keeps its height while it is scrolled to the end") compares the scroll height of a 40-row member list before and after a scroll to the end. A new long list that wants `content-visibility` needs an estimate per row form, measured as content-box height.
- **A restored Settings page on a phone shows content, so it focuses Close:** the pane that is off screen is inert, and `openSettingsPage()` focused the first sidebar tab, which an inert pane cannot take, so the focus stayed on the top-bar toggle behind the modal page. It focuses `#sb-settings-close` when the page opens in content view (`public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`; `tests/foundation/shell-navigation.e2e.js`). Close lives in the content header, so the sidebar view carries a second Close, `#sb-settings-sidebar-close` after the search toggle (`display: none` above the phone width, same action and name; `tests/sillybunny-overlays.e2e.js`, "Settings Close").
- **Engine differences that tests must absorb, not the product:** WebKit serialises `--mainFontFamily` with double quotes, so `tests/font-import.e2e.js` compares families without quotes, and it requests the italic file of a font family as soon as the family is chosen. WebKit reports the curve of `ease` as `ease`. Headless Firefox delivers a frame only every 50 to 100 ms, so `tests/background-folders.e2e.js` pauses the animations and seeks them (`seekStep`) instead of counting frames. Playwright's Firefox does not restyle for an emulated `prefers-contrast: more`, so the toggle-row test in `tests/misc-form-dialogs.e2e.js` skips that part there.
- **The Playwright clock keeps running until it is paused:** after `page.clock.install()` time flows naturally as the timers fire, so a timed picture checked at an exact time drifts by the page's start-up time. Pause it (`page.clock.pauseAt()`) for exact-time checks, as `tests/art-motion.e2e.js` does. `page.clock` replaces `setTimeout`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `performance` and the other time functions, and `install` must come before any other clock-related call, so a test's own patch of those functions goes after the clock call or is lost.
- **Breaks:** with many workers against one server, tests time out. A server handles its requests on one main thread, and `workers: '50%'` started 64 workers on a 128-thread machine. **Guard:** `getDefaultWorkers()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` allows at most 16 workers for one server (test: `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`).
- **Breaks:** the settings conflict prompt ("Settings changed on another device") opened in a page about one second after APP_READY when another page on the same server had saved since this page loaded its settings. It covered the page and blocked every later settings save. Tests on one server met it often (37 of 57 first `openQuietChatForSmoke()` calls with 4 servers; 7 of 8 pages that booted at once), and so did a user with a second tab or device, without any settings change. **Cause:** every boot saves the settings without a change, through `initCustomSelectedSamplers()` and `validateDisabledSamplers()` in `public/scripts/samplerSelect.js` (SillyTavern #4782 moved what they saved to localforage), and the version check refused a save from a page that had loaded before another page saved (`prepareSettingsSave()` in `src/settings-version.js`). **Guards:** a page sends no save when its settings equal the text that it last loaded or saved (`createSettingsSync()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-sync.js`, which keeps a key-sorted copy of that text, because the page keeps changing the objects that it parsed). This also covers a page whose settings another tab changed in the second after its boot. `POST /api/settings/save` answers a save whose content equals the stored settings with `unchanged: true` and the stored version (`isUnchangedSettingsSave()` in `src/settings-version.js`), so it raises no version, and `saveSettingsInner()` in `public/script.js` accepts that answer. A real change from an older page still gets 409 and the prompt. **Tests:** `tests/settings-version-guard.test.js`, `tests/settings-save-unchanged.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-settings-sync.test.js`, `tests/openai-custom-endpoint-credentials.test.js`. **Test guards:** tests that save real changes at the same time on one server still meet the prompt, as two users would. `protectMaintainerServer()` (`tests/helpers/maintainer-server.js`) answers `/api/settings/save` for the specs that call it, and `keepSavesInPage()` (`tests/helpers/saves-in-page.js`) also serves the saved settings back to `/api/settings/get` (`tests/nanogpt-controls.e2e.js`, for its reload test). `tests/frontend/shared-page.js`, `tests/chat-scroll-regressions.e2e.js` and `tests/frontend/CoreBrowserParity.e2e.js` (its preset test awaits a save inside `selectPreset()`) answer it themselves. `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` also opens a new chat on each page, because pages that write one chat file at the same time fail each other's chat integrity check.
- **Breaks:** a test helper that removes a popup's `<dialog>` leaves the popup's promise waiting forever. `saveSettings()` chains every save on `settingsSaveQueue` in `public/script.js`, so with the settings conflict prompt removed, every later save of that page waits too: `nanogpt-controls.e2e.js` "provider controls preserve restrictions" hung until its 90 s timeout. A popup settles only through `complete()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`), whose `runAfterAnimation()` callback has a 500 ms fallback. **Guard:** `dismissOpenDialogIfPresent()` in `tests/chat-scroll-regression-helpers.js` completes each popup in `Popup.util.popups` with `completeCancelled()`, and removes only the other dialogs.
- **Breaks:** a test on a page that other spec files share fails on state that one of them left behind. **Guards:** `tests/frontend/shared-page.js` restores the chat metadata, which holds the local variables, and the global variables of its boot before each test, and it fails a test that runs on a page that has navigated since the boot. Only files that leave no other state behind import it: MessageFormatter adds permanent formatter hooks, MacroEnvBuilder registers an env provider that throws, and StscriptParserFixes replaces the parser settings getter, so they boot for each test. **Tests:** the seven files that import it.
- **Breaks:** a byte budget fails when the run asks for uncompressed responses. `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js` sums `transferSize`, which is about 20.7 MB of JavaScript without compression against its 12 MiB budget. **Guard:** that spec asks for compressed responses with `test.use({ extraHTTPHeaders })`, whatever the config or runner sends.
- **Breaks:** with `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_SERVERS`, a run started no server and used the default base URL when its shell passed `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX` with an empty value. `getServerConfig()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` took every defined value for the index of a worker. **Guard:** an empty value counts as unset (test: `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`).
- **Breaks:** a server that the config started still showed the first-run prompt to the tests, so a spec file that does not answer it failed all its tests. **Cause:** `tests/e2e-servers-prime.js` closed its page at APP_READY, and the app saves the end of the first run only after its save delay (`saveSettingsDebounced()` in `public/script.js`). The save came first only while the rest of the boot took longer than that delay. **Guard:** the prime step waits until the stored settings say that the first run is over (`waitUntilFirstRunIsOver()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js`). **Tests:** `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** a server that the config started used the `config.yaml` of the repository root, so it ran with the settings of the developer's own server, and it wrote that file with the default values (`port: 4444`) when the file was absent. **Guard:** `getServerCommand()` in `tests/e2e-servers.js` gives each server a `--configPath` inside its data root. **Tests:** `tests/e2e-servers.test.js`; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** with `SILLYBUNNY_TEST_SERVERS`, every worker would use one server if Playwright stopped importing the config in each worker after it sets `TEST_PARALLEL_INDEX` (it does in 1.64.1, `lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js`). **Guards:** the main process config has no `baseURL`, so such a worker fails at its first navigation; `tests/e2e-servers.e2e.js` checks the server of each worker that runs it.
- **Breaks:** a fake clock moved past a debounce does not prove that no write was sent. `page.clock.runFor()` fires the page's timers at once, but a request that a timer starts reaches a Node route handler in real time, after `runFor()` returns. **Guard:** after the move, a negative check waits a short real tail (`WRITE_TAIL_MS` in `tests/sillybunny-preset-autosave.e2e.js` and `tests/connection-profile-change-notice.e2e.js`); a positive check polls for the write. A window that ends only when the page's own requests settle (the profile settle wait in `profile-change-model.js`) stays in real time, because those requests land late on the moved timeline.
- **Breaks:** a locator on a shared component class matches every surface that builds that component. `.ds-profile-row` comes from `createProfileRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/select-row.js`), which the Connections panel, the preset rows and the in-chat agents use, so `.ds-profile-row .ds-select-row__menu` matched five menus and four tests of `tests/connection-profile-change-notice.e2e.js` failed in strict mode. **Guard:** scope such a locator to its section (`.sb-connections-profile`).
- **Breaks:** a button row inside a settings page showed no focus ring. `#sb-settings-page .sb-settings-mounted-shell :is(.ds-pref-group, .sb-settings-flat-section) > .ds-row { box-shadow: none }` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`) outranks the shared `.ds-row.ds-button-row:focus-visible` of `public/css/sillybunny-editor-shell.css`, so the ring came only from page rules; the Connections rules that gave it were deleted with the page's old row styling, and `tests/sillybunny-connections-layout.e2e.js` ("CC and TC Authorize match the General button row") failed. **Guard:** `#sb-settings-page .sb-connections-panel .ds-row.ds-button-row:focus-visible` restores it for Connections. Other settings pages with button rows rely on their own rules.
- **Breaks:** `presentButtonRow()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-connections-form.js`) removes `menu_button` from an upstream `div` button, so the settings inventory no longer saw `#customize_additional_parameters` as a button (it matches `.menu_button` and `[role="button"]`). **Guard:** the helper sets `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"` and an Enter/Space handler itself, and its teardown goes through the panel's controllers.
- **Breaks:** `test.describe.configure({ mode: 'serial' })` skips the rest of its group after a failure ("did not run"), so removing it can expose a test that never ran. In `tests/server-generation-commit.e2e.js` the desktop "Stop during streamed reasoning" test failed on a close button that the Settings page had replaced, and the mobile twin first ran, and failed the same way, once the mode was gone. **Guard:** before calling a failure after such a change a regression, run the test alone on the old tree with only that test selected.
- **Breaks:** a test that writes to a fixed folder outside the repo fails on every machine without it: a recursive `mkdir` under a home folder that does not exist throws `EACCES`, because `/home` belongs to root. About 25 tests wrote review screenshots to `/home/geechan/.opencode/plan/...` or `/tmp/opencode/...`. **Guard:** write artefacts to `testInfo.outputPath()` (one folder per test, under the run's output), as `tests/helpers/q56-review.js` does.
- **Breaks:** a fixed wait for a dialog after each boot costs a second per test even where no dialog can open. **Guard:** `protectMaintainerServer()` records its pages (`isMaintainerServerProtected()` in `tests/helpers/maintainer-server.js`); `openQuietChatForSmoke()` and `openReadyChat()` skip the first dialog wait for those pages, because neither the settings conflict prompt nor Welcome opens there, and keep it for other pages.
- **Breaks:** in WebKit, a control inside a Settings section can keep a computed `visibility: hidden` after the section becomes active. Inactive hosted roots have `visibility: hidden !important; content-visibility: hidden` (`public/css/sillybunny-tabs.css`, `.sb-settings-mounted-shell:not(.sb-settings-active-root)`), and WebKit did not recompute the inherited value of `#tts_provider`'s group after activation: `getComputedStyle()` and `checkVisibility()` said hidden, while `elementFromPoint()` returned the select and screenshots showed it; any style write on the group fixed it. Playwright's actionability check then waited until the timeout (`tests/native-dialog-replacements.e2e.js`, SpeechT5, about half the WebKit runs). **Guard:** that spec selects with `force: true`. The app keeps the `content-visibility` rule; app code that reads computed `visibility` there can see the same stale value.
- **A wait that an endless animation holds:** `element.getAnimations({ subtree: true }).length === 0` is never true while a spinner or an endless hover animation runs, and `animation.finished` never resolves for one, so a step that waits on either holds until the test timeout. The swipe picker did this: the click that opens it leaves the pointer on a row's delete icon, which spins while it is hovered. Wait with `settle()` (`tests/helpers/surfaces/steps.js`), which counts only animations that have an end and fails after a bounded time with the list of those still running (`513f155a2`, `d2f7dceda`).
- **A wait for every image:** an `<img loading="lazy">` that has no box, is hidden or sits off screen never starts to load, so `[...images].every(image => image.complete)` never becomes true. Home's chat rows behind "show more" did this once more chats were seeded, and every Home surface timed out. Count a lazy image only when it is shown inside the viewport, as `homeSettled()` and `windowSettled()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/home.js` do (`fd841aeb5`).
- **The app scrolls a loaded chat back to the bottom:** after a chat loads, `scrollLoadedChatToBottom()` (`public/script.js`) scrolls to the bottom again on timers (`CHAT_LOAD_SCROLL_SETTLE_DELAYS_MS`, the last at 2400 ms, plus `CHAT_LOAD_BOTTOM_LOCK_EXTRA_MS`) until the user scrolls. A scripted `scrollIntoView()` in that window is undone by a later timer. A `wheel` or touch event on `#chat` clears the lock (`clearChatLoadBottomLock()`), so a test turns the wheel over the chat first, as `scrollToOlderMessagesControl()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/chat-steps.js` does (`3d5f3e028`).
- **The delayed chat autofocus dismisses an open menu:** `getChat()` (`public/script.js`) triggers a click and a focus on `#send_textarea` a moment after `CHAT_LOADED` (`debounce_timeout.short`), unless a visible text input has the focus or Settings is open. A menu that closes on any click outside it closes when it is opened in that window. The persona picker (`public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`) did, for a user as well as for a test; its outside-click handler now ignores a click that is not the user's (`event.isTrusted`, `644fde062`; `tests/persona-picker-dismiss.e2e.js`). A test that opens another such menu waits until the focus is in a visible input or textarea first, but only when it loaded the chat itself: `openPersonaMenu()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/status.js` goes through `openAuditChat()` of `dialog-steps.js` (`66247bfe9`). A chat that the boot restored has had its autofocus already and the persona bubble can hold the focus, and a wait for an input then never ends (about one boot in fifty, a 120 s hang).
- **The delayed chat autofocus takes the focus back from the greeting picture button:** the same callback (`getChat()` and `getTemporaryChat()` in `public/script.js`) ran after the greeting viewer had closed and returned the focus to `.sbGreetingArtOpen`, because a chat that loads more than once ends its autofocus 200 ms after the last load, and a button is not a text input. The focus went to `#send_textarea`, and `dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js` (greeting picture viewer, Escape) failed 2 of 24 runs at load 44. Both callbacks now return while the focus is inside `.sbGreetingArt`; `tests/greeting-art.e2e.js` (`keeps the focus on the picture button when the delayed autofocus of a chat load comes after it`) loads the chat again and puts the focus on the button inside the 200 ms. 80 of 80 runs passed at load 61 after the change.
- **The logit bias list is not stable right after the Sampling page shows:** the app fills the preset select and builds the rows after the page is shown, and applying a Chat Completion preset that holds bias presets sets `bias_preset_selected` from the preset (`public/scripts/openai.js`, where the preset's bias presets are normalised). In audit runs the list was empty in some runs and filled in others, and the page differed by a few pixels in height. `selectLogitBiasPreset()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/steps.js` selects the fixture's preset, waits for its rows, checks again once the page has settled, and selects again when the selection was undone (`c7894912c`). The order of the two events was not traced.
- **A normaliser that copies the live state cuts the drawn rows off from it (F-287):** `normalizeLogitBiasPresets()` (`public/scripts/openai.js`) rebuilt every preset array with copied entries, and Add runs it, so after an Add the rows already on screen held objects that were no longer saved: typing in them was lost. A `keepEntries` mode normalises in place (`normalizeLogitBiasState()` passes it). Test: "Adding a Logit Bias entry keeps the rows…" (`tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js`), red 1 of 1 without the mode. Merge of 14.6c, 2026-11-18.
- **Seeded data changes what a page shows:** the persona list shows a batch of personas (`Personas_PerPage`, 50 when unset) behind a Show More row, and Home hides older chat rows behind "show more", so a step that expects a row on the first page fails once other fixtures add data. Steps page to the row (`openPersonaEditorEntry()` in `tests/helpers/surfaces/panel-steps.js`), and the audit global setup seeds every fixture for every run, so that a run narrowed to one family sees the same data as a full one (`tests/audit/seed-fixtures.global-setup.js`, `de1fe8f5d`).
- **`firstRun: false` in any test file:** `tests/unit/welcome-helper-coverage.test.js` flags a file under `tests/` that contains `firstRun: false` or `firstRun = false` without an import of a Welcome helper, whatever the text means there. An option of that name in a registry file failed it (`070931790`); name such an option differently.
- **State read during a transition:** `tests/character-shell-performance.e2e.js` compared two fills while the toggle's `background-color` transition ran, and now waits for the transition (`bc29198c0`). The foundation sheet-motion test (`tests/foundation/shell-navigation.e2e.js`) polled for the sheet's Web Animation after the click and missed it when tracing slowed the action; it observes from before the click (`638cb6157`).
- **A whole-object assertion on a record that other code normalises:** `tests/welcome-startup.e2e.js` compared the new persona's description with `toEqual({ description: '', position: 0 })`. Welcome writes those two fields (`savePersonaName()`); `getPersonaRecord()` in `public/scripts/personas.js` (and the persona editor's `getPersonaEditorDescriptor()`) add `appendices: []` and `activeAppendices: {}` the first time anything reads the record, so under load the assertion saw one shape or the other. Forcing `getPersonaRecord()` before the read made it red every time (`Received + 2`). Welcome stays as it is, because it writes what the onboarding popup of upstream wrote; the test asserts the fields Welcome owns with `toMatchObject`.
- **A rule that names a gallery stops matching when the section order moves the panel out of it:** "saved source rows retain selection, removal, and mobile keyboard focus" (`tests/sillybunny-settings-consistency.e2e.js`) failed under load (5 of 40 at a load average of 208) with `clientHeight` 38 against 40 on the remove button of a saved source. `{ section: '#bpt-animated-bg-panel' }` in `SB_SETTINGS_SECTION_ORDER` moves the Animated Backgrounds panel from `#Backgrounds` to `#sb-appearance-content`, at a time that depends on load, and `#sb-settings-page #Backgrounds .menu_button { border: 0 }` then no longer reached the remove button: it kept the default 1px border (the box stayed 40px, `clientHeight` excludes the border), unlike the source button beside it. `sillybunny-tabs.css` gives the remove button `border: 0` itself. The first guess, that the read came before the panel was presented, was wrong: waiting for `data-sb-background-presented` changed nothing. What showed it: `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate` at 6 in the test made 12 of 16 runs fail, a dump of the computed style in the passing and failing runs differed in the border, and the panel's ancestors in the failing runs had no `#Backgrounds`. The test now places the panel where the order puts it, so it is red every time without the rule (8 of 8) and green with it (40 of 40). A rule that is scoped to a container the element can leave needs the element's own selector too; the hover and focus rules of this block already named `#bpt-animated-bg-panel`, the base rule did not.
- **A page read after a fixed wait, before it has built its groups:** "every Customize and Backend page, and Characters Import, keeps the one page frame" (`tests/sillybunny-settings-consistency.e2e.js`) read each page 300ms after opening its tab, and twice on 2026-11-18 under load Data & Security had no `.ds-pref-group` yet ("has no group"; passed alone each time). The test now polls the read until a group shows; 12 of 12 at six workers after.
- **A hidden icon that keeps spinning:** `.hidden` only sets `visibility`, so the hidden connection profile spinner ran an endless `fa-spin` animation for the whole session, and a test that waits for running animations saw it. `:is(.fa-spin, .fa-spin-pulse, .fa-pulse).hidden` no longer animates (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`, `362bfdb4d`); `tests/header-icon-button-parity.e2e.js` covers both states of the spinner.
- **Tests that need a connection or saved data:** a throwaway data root has no API connection and no saved tracker. `tests/frontend/ConversationLorebooks.e2e.js` sets a connected status before it sends, and the long-tracker test in `tests/in-chat-agents-editor.e2e.js` creates and removes its own saved tracker through a pass-through that is open only for those two calls (`638cb6157`). `tests/card-script-sandbox.e2e.js` holds Shift over the message to reveal its actions, because the run button is hidden until the row is expanded (`bc29198c0`).
- **A journey that runs close to its timeout fails when the app gets slightly slower:** the mobile "Manager create…" test of `tests/character-folders.e2e.js` is about 100 steps of 0.3 to 1 s against the default 30 s, and took 29.5 s at `7b4ba0a66` (1 run of 3 already failed) and 30.3 s at the head, where the folder rows animate (`createFolderMotion`) and the manager's close takes 0.5 s longer. It is a data-preservation journey and the motion has its own test, so the test runs with `page.emulateMedia({ reducedMotion: 'reduce' })` (17 s on a phone, 13 s on desktop). When a test fails only at the limit, time its steps against an older tree before raising the limit.
- **A `waitForFunction` predicate that throws fails the wait:** `window.SillyTavern.getContext()` throws `ReferenceError: Cannot access 'chat' before initialization` (`public/scripts/st-context.js`) while the app's modules start, so a bare `getContext()?.eventSource…has('app_ready')` fails the test on a page that is slow to start (2 of 60 runs of the "connection profile change notice" test at 16 workers on one server). `waitForAppReady()` (`tests/helpers/app-ready.js`) and `finishBoot()` (`tests/helpers/surfaces/boot.js`) treat a throw as not ready. About 45 other specs and helpers still carry the bare form.
- **A settings page builds its groups after its tab opens:** a read of `.sb-prompting-group:visible` titles right after `openPanel()` missed "Character Names Behavior" on Presets (`tests/sillybunny-backend-settings.e2e.js`). Poll the titles (`expect.poll`) while that page is open; only the open page has visible groups.
- **The startup request count is an exact ratchet:** `STARTUP_RESOURCE_LIMIT` in `tests/frontend-performance.e2e.js` equals the steady count of a repeat start (871), so a new module that loads at startup fails the test until its commit raises the limit on purpose (`bc29198c0`, `55244b488`). The budget for compressed JavaScript there is 13 MiB (`646f13755`). A first start of the day for an admin account adds two requests, `/api/server-admin/install-type` and `/api/settings/save`: `doInstallNoticeCheck()` (`sillybunny-install-notice.js`) asks once a day and stores the day's stamp in the account storage, which saves the settings. Which test of a shared server saw them depended on which start came first, so the count was 861 or 863 (864 with a second save) against a limit of 863. The spec now starts the page once in another browser context, until the stamp is in the stored settings, and measures a start in a fresh context after it; the browser cache of the measured start stays cold. The spec is not a CI job and `e2e-related` does not list it for a new module, so run it for any change that adds a file under `public/scripts/` which a startup module imports: `auto-fix-markdown.js`, `generation-response-length-overrides.js` and `sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.js` merged without the raise and left the count at 864 against 861, and `markdown-engine.js` with `sillybunny-markdown-engine-row.js` make it 866. `280e33bf4` (staging at `d30a65077` plus a docs commit) counted 867 against 866; the toast forms add `sillybunny-toast-form.js`, `sillybunny-toast-placement.js` and `sillybunny-toast-position.js` and make it 870, and `sillybunny-toast-notification.js` makes it 871. The clean-up that followed (`581fe195f`, `2c464d249`) added and removed no module, and the count stays 871: the spec passes at a limit of 871 and fails at 870. `novelai-precise-reference.js` and `novelai-precise-reference-ui.js`, which Image Generation and Quick Image Gen import at startup, make it 873; their stylesheet loads only when Precise Reference is on.

- **A page already has its own `#qr--bar`, and on a phone it sits in the collapsed extension tray:** a test that injects a second `#qr--bar` reads the real, empty one through `querySelector`. `tests/composer-bar-controls.e2e.js` fills the page's own bar and opens the tray with `.sb-bottom-chat-tray-toggle`; the tray opens with a scale animation, so a height is polled (`expect.poll`) and not read after a few frames (42.1px against 44px).

- **The Home card's open action loads the chat twice, and the first greeting is on screen before the second chat replaces it:** `openRoleplayChat` (`sillybunny-home/assistant-chat.js`) runs `selectCharacterById` and then `doNewChat`. A test that waits for `#chat .mes[mesid="0"]` and sends at once can have its generation ended by the second load (`generation_ended`, a new chat, no request to the provider), which is how `tests/request-errors.e2e.js` "chat answered with 401json" failed with no toast at all. `openChatWithToastRecorder` counts `chatLoaded` events from before the click. The fixture stubs chat saves, so the assistant never has a chat file, and the open action then loads the chat once (the selection creates it; see the entry on Home, one chat for a character with none), so the wait is for one. It waited for two until the app stopped making the second chat, and the three 401 cases timed out on that wait while the app was right. Other specs that open a chat through this card and send straight away may have the same race.

### Unit tests that run git

- **Breaks:** the unit suite, started from a process that git spawned, writes to the real repository: on 2026-11-12, `git rebase -x 'cd tests && npm run test:unit'` left `core.bare = true` and a `[user]` section in the shared `.git/config`, two tags (`v0.0.1`, `v9.9.9`) and test commits on the rebase head.
- **Files:** `tests/server-plugin-manager.test.js`, `tests/bootstrap-installer.test.js`, `tests/list-sb-class-changes.test.js`, `tests/bump-agent-template-versions.test.js` and other suites that build temporary repositories with plain `git init`, `git config`, `git commit` and `git tag`.
- **Guards:**
  - git exports `GIT_DIR` and related variables to `git rebase --exec` commands and to hooks, and a child `git` follows `GIT_DIR` instead of its working directory, so `git init --bare` in a temporary folder re-initialises the real repository.
  - Run the suite from a normal shell. To test each commit of a series, `git checkout <sha>` in turn and run it there, or unset `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE` and `GIT_INDEX_FILE` first.
- **Repair:** `git config core.bare false`, remove the `[user]` section, delete the tags, `git rebase --quit`, then `git checkout -f <branch>` and `git clean -fd` in the worktree.

- **Confirm verbs:** an upstream confirm that sets no `okButton` shows "Yes". `confirmVerb(label, { destructive, negative })` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-delete-confirm.js` returns the Popup options for one call site (a spread under a `SillyBunny:` marker), so the destructive rule (`defaultResult: NEGATIVE`, `is-destructive` on OK in `onOpening`) lives once. The helper sits in a file that `script.js` already imports, so it adds no startup module. A `confirmVerb` spread that sets `negative` is a `cancelButton` key, which stops `replaceDefaultNegativeLabel` from renaming the negative to "Cancel"; use it where the negative means "do not do this part" and the flow goes on (seven sites). Third-party confirms keep the frame's default. Tests: `tests/unit/confirm-verbs.test.js` (the approved list per file, a marker within three lines of each use, and a parse of every upstream-origin CONFIRM call that fails on one without a verb), `tests/unit/sillybunny-destructive-confirm.test.js`, `tests/unit/popup-contract.test.js`.
- **Two Cancels and the Cancel slot:** a custom response that resolves to `null` is ordered `-2` in an Alert (`buildAlert` in `popup-frame.js`), ahead of the default Cancel and the negative, so the real cancel is first in a row and last in a stack. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-popup-frame.test.js`.
- **A mock of `popup.js` must export what the helper reads:** `sillybunny-delete-confirm.js` imports `POPUP_RESULT`, `POPUP_TYPE` and `Popup` from `popup.js`, and the call sites import `t` from `i18n.js`; a unit test that mocks `popup.js` for an upstream file needs all of them (`tests/unit/bulk-edit-overlay.test.js`, `tests/quick-reply-settings-ui.test.js`).
- **A confirm without `okButton` can read "OK" in zh-cn and zh-tw:** `popup.js` sets `data-i18n="OK"` on the OK button before the CONFIRM branch writes "Yes", and `i18n.js` translates every added `[data-i18n]` node, so a third-party confirm shows "确定" (checked in a zh-cn context). A label passed as `okButton` sets its own `data-i18n` and is not affected.
- **Legacy `#dialogue_popup_ok`:** it is the suggested action, and `callPopup` marks it `is-destructive` only for the types that delete. The sheet rule needs two ids (`#dialogue_popup_controls > #dialogue_popup_ok`) because `style.css` paints `#dialogue_popup_ok` crimson at one id.

### Dialog surfaces and layering

- **Breaks:** a broad `.sb-dialog` descendant textarea rule changes the in-chat Agents Regex editor height (159.75px instead of 160px).
- **Guard:** scope textarea sizing to the adapter rows that need it, not generic dialog descendants. `89cd805db` keeps the `8lh` minimum on the window-content adapter's scenario and YAML rows; regression: `tests/in-chat-agents-editor.e2e.js` (regex fields at least 160px).
- **The expanded editor's text area is named from the Window title (p6 follow-ups):** the `.editor_maximize` handler in `public/scripts/chats.js` (upstream-origin, `SillyBunny:` marker) builds the Window title from `label[for]`, then the `h4` or `.ds-field-header` around the button, and now a third source, `closest('.ds-pref-group-header')` and its `.ds-pref-group-heading`; the same string goes on the text area as `aria-label`. The persona, agent and World Info entry fields have no `label[for]`, `h4` or `.ds-field-header`, so their expanded Window had no title and the text area no name (six axe `label` records, now removed). Their expanded Window gets a title because of the third source. Test: `tests/window-content-dialogs.e2e.js` (the text area's accessible name equals `.sb-dialog__title`), the `field-expanded` audit states.
- **Floating rows:** an empty entry row can paint its placeholder underneath the floated title. Keep the hint in `aria-description` and suppress the painted placeholder while the title floats. Fixed in `357be55f7`; regression: `tests/sillybunny-editor-rows-layout.e2e.js`.
- **Action-menu sheets live for one opening:** the phone bottom sheet of `openActionMenu` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-action-menu.js`) used to be one module-level sheet that closing only hid. It kept `#sb-action-menu` and `#sb-action-sheet-title`, so the next popover got duplicate ids and its `aria-controls` resolved to the stale menu, and it kept `data-sb-prompt-menu` (written by `sillybunny-prompt-editor.js` and read by `sillybunny-preset-autosave.js`) on every later menu. `createSheet()` now builds a sheet per opening, `releaseSheetIds()` strips the ids from a leaving sheet at once, and the sheet is removed in `createSheetController`'s `onHidden` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-sheet.js`), which runs once both exits finish, immediately when nothing animates. Popovers keep their block size and top inside `bounds`, and keyboard focus scrolls the focused row into view (`getRevealScrollTop()`), because `focus({ preventScroll: true })` had left End and ArrowDown below a capped popover at 844×390. Tests: `tests/action-menu-viewport.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-action-menu.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-sheet.test.js`, `tests/sheet-swipe.e2e.js` (reads the drag state through a handle taken before the sheet is removed).
- **A closing Popup holds its ids for the whole exit:** `Popup#hide()` keeps the `<dialog>` in the document until the 200ms exit ends, so a second Popup whose content has the same ids (a template with fixed ids, a `customInputs` id) put every id in the document twice when it opened in that time: a `#id` lookup then found the closing copy first, and `label[for]` and `aria-labelledby` pointed at it. `Popup#show()` (`public/scripts/popup.js`, `SillyBunny:` marker) now waits for a closing popup that holds one of its ids, for at most 1000ms so a closing popup whose `onClose` awaits the new one cannot hold it for ever; a popup with other ids opens at once. Tests: `tests/popup-closing-ids.e2e.js` (red before: 2 copies). The `openDialog` hosts need no such guard (task 14.7a audit): `dialog.closed` settles after `host.remove()`, and every `openDialog` caller with fixed ids is a singleton (`openOnce`, `activeManager`), has a busy button, or sits behind the modal dialog it opens, which keeps the page behind it inert for the whole exit. The ids of the shared row helpers and dialog titles are generated (`uid()`, `uniqueId()`).
- **Help window levels and selection:** Help (`public/scripts/sillybunny-help/`) has a front page, section pages and topic pages, with a section sidebar. Wide layouts replace the content pane in place, and the trail, More Information and the sidebar go up; narrow layouts push section and topic pages, the trail pops to the page it names, and See Also swaps in place; one `view` state survives resizes across the collapse breakpoint. `validateHelpSections()` rejects a model with repeated ids, an empty section or an unknown `seeAlso` id before the window opens.
