- **A bottom message sits above the bars that are docked at the bottom, and a panel, a floating composer or a dialog is not one:** a panel of the top bar (Backend, Customize, Quick Access: Agents and Extensions, Characters, the character editor, every tab of the shells) is drawn over the composer, and a toast that kept its 24px above `#form_sheld` floated in the middle of the panel (1440×900: 703 from the top instead of 876; 390×844: 659 instead of 820). `sillybunny-toast-placement.js` asks the page instead of knowing the panels. `composerCovered()` hit-tests five points 3px inside the composer's top edge with `document.elementsFromPoint`, skips both message containers, and counts a point as covered when the topmost element left neither is in the composer nor holds it (the chat shell holds it, and answers where a gap of the composer lets the pointer through); more than half of the points that answer have to be covered, so a menu over a corner of the edge moves nothing, and `pointer-events: none` never answers. A modal dialog covers the composer the same way (its dimming answers at every point), so it is not skipped: a bottom message sits at the window edge under a dialog, a toast 24px and a notification 4px from it, and a dialog that is a bottom sheet is a bar for the toasts only (the entry below). `composerDocked()` asks for the composer's bottom to be within 4px of the window's, so a composer that MovingUI floated above the bottom is no bar, and the message sits at the window edge. `sheetsAbove()` finds the other bars generically: from the topmost element at three points (25%, 50%, 75% of the window's width) 14px above the support (the window's bottom, or the composer's top while it is a bar) it asks that element and each element that holds it whether it is `position: fixed`, ends on the support (up to 12px above it, 4px below it), spans half the window or more, and leaves the extent of the tallest toast on screen with its margins (82px with none) under the top bar, as a dialog sheet must, and it is not a dialog, in a dialog, a message, the composer or something that holds it; the next sheet is looked for on the top of the one found, three at most. The side-panel sheet on a phone ends 6px above the composer (its margin), which is why the support has a gap, and a page of the top bar starts at the top bar, which is why it is not a sheet (Backend, Characters and the editors at 390 give the same placement as before). A fixed room of 24px let a sheet 24 to about 80px under the bar be the bar for the toast, which then lay over the top bar (390×500, an action sheet with its top at 100 and the bar ending at 44: the toast at 30 to 76); a sheet with less room than the toasts need is now no bar, and the toast sits at the next bar down, as over a dialog sheet that leaves no room (`tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-placement.test.js`, "under a top bar, with a toast on screen"). The highest of the keyboard, the composer and the sheets is the bar: the keyboard still lifts a message, and the clip edge, being the edge of the strip, goes with it. At Bottom Center the message is centred on the bar that it sits above: the script writes `--sb-toast-bar-shift` (the distance of the bar's middle from the window's middle, from the part of the bar that is in the window) to the strip and the notification container, and the CSS translates the toast or the card by it, clamped so that it stays 12px inside the window, which is the allowance of its width (`calc(100cqw - 24px)`, and `100vw` for a card). At Bottom Left and Bottom Right the message moves by the start or the end of the bar instead (`--sb-toast-bar-start`, `--sb-toast-bar-end`, the end negative, each from the part of the bar that is in the window), so that it keeps against the bar the margin it has against the window, a toast 12px and a card 4px, under the same clamp. At the window edge, in a dialog and at the other positions nothing is written. While a message is on screen a `MutationObserver` (the `class`, `style`, `hidden`, `open`, `inert` and `aria-hidden` attributes below the body, and the children of the body), the end of a transition or an animation outside the messages, and the `finished` promise of each running animation of a surface (a sheet that the Web Animations API springs in fires no event, and it is not on its edge until it has stopped) schedule one `requestAnimationFrame` check per burst, at most once in 100ms (`BAR_CHECK_INTERVAL`: the first change after a quiet time is looked at in the next frame, and a change inside the interval waits for its end with the others that come meanwhile, so the last one is seen; a page that mutates every frame made about 8 hit-tests per frame, 1,432 `elementsFromPoint` calls in 3 seconds, before this), which writes only when the inset or the shift changed; a message that shows is placed at once, outside the watch (`tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-placement.test.js`, "how often the bars are looked at"). The message eases (`glideToPlace` sets an inline `transition` on `bottom` and `margin` for `MOTION_SURFACE` plus 50ms, since a transition that is taken off while it runs ends at once) when the source of its place changes (the window edge, the keyboard, the composer, a sheet), and follows at once when the same bar grows; the shift eases through a CSS transition on `translate`. A message that is placed while a panel is open is not eased: a first placement is a jump. The stack offset is computed from where the toasts are going to be (`overlayTargetBox`), not from their box, which a glide has not reached yet. A probe of the gaps at 1440, 1024 and 390 found the group chat fine (the toast that sat 94px high was stacked above the error toast of a group that the probe faked). Not probed: the Conversation style, which hides `#form_sheld` (`public/css/sillybunny-conversation.css`) and so has no composer for this code to find, and a software keyboard on a real device (the keyboard inset is unit-tested with a stubbed visual viewport only; when `elementsFromPoint` answers at no point, the composer stays the bar). Tests: `tests/toast-composer-cover.e2e.js` (Backend and Customize at both widths, the glide both ways, the first frame, an element that no list knows, a small menu, a narrow panel, `pointer-events: none`, the action menu and the side-panel sheet at 390, the centring at 1024 and its clamp, a modal dialog, MovingUI), `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-placement.test.js` (the covered check, docked, the sheets, the shift, the watch, the glide), `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js` (the clamp and the transition). A spec that reads a message must clear the one that the app shows at startup first (a success with a button sits in `#toast-container` and answers `#toast-container > .sb-form-notification` before the message that the spec made), and must wait for the slide-in before it reads a box.
- **While a modal dialog is open, a toast stays at its place in the window, above the dialog, and a Popup is the one dialog that moves it:** DESIGN.md Toasts › Position. The overlay is a child of the top open modal dialog (`getTopmostDialog()` in `placeToastContainers()`), which puts it in the top layer and keeps it from being inert, and it is `position: fixed` like any strip. No rule places it in the dialog any more: `--sb-toast-frame-*`, `data-sb-toast-in-dialog`, `placeInDialog()` and the bottom centre of the dialog are gone, and `edgeOf()` has no dialog argument. A dialog is the containing block of a fixed child only while it has a transform, a filter, a backdrop filter, `contain: layout` or `paint` or the like. The frames (`dialog.sb-dialog-host` animates its surface and its scrim, not itself), a plain `showModal()` dialog and a Popup at rest have none (a Popup reports `transform: none` and `backdrop-filter: none` even with `--SmartThemeBackdropFilter: blur(10px)`), so a strip in them is at the window from its first frame. A dialog that an extension gives a transform, as one that centres itself with `transform: translate(-50%, -50%)`, a filter, `will-change`, `contain: paint` or a perspective is the containing block at rest too, and the overlay and `#toast-container`, which are `position: fixed` children of it, took its width and sat at its foot, not at the window. `containingBlockInsets()` (`sillybunny-toast-placement.js`) reads the computed style and the box of the parent of each container and, for a dialog that only moves (a matrix whose linear part is the identity, no `rotate`, `scale` or perspective) and lets its overflow show, writes `--sb-toast-cb-left`, `-right`, `-top` and `-bottom`, the distances of the window's edges from the dialog's padding box, which the CSS adds to the four edges of both containers (`placeContainerFrame()`, run on every placement and every check of the bars, so it follows a dialog that moves). Two cases are not compensated and keep the place that the dialog gives the containers: a dialog that is scaled, turned or projected at rest has no insets in px that are right for its inside, and a dialog that clips its overflow, which is the default for a modal dialog (`overflow: auto`), would cut off a container at the window place, which usually lies outside its box, so the container stays inside the dialog, at its foot, where it can be read. The Popup is scaled only while it opens, which `.popup[opening]` hides the strip for. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-placement.test.js` ("a dialog that centres itself with a transform", with stubbed geometry and computed style, not run in an engine), `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js`. A Popup animates itself (`pop-in`: a scale from 0.8 with a spring, and an opacity, over about 550ms at 1440; a slide on a phone) and has `overflow: hidden`, so while it has `[opening]` the strip is measured against the dialog and clipped by it (a bottom toast at 1440: 534 down to 382 from the window bottom against 24 at rest; a top toast: 424 against 776; at 390, 2 up to 207). `.popup[opening] #sb-toast-overlay { visibility: hidden }` hides it then, as `.popup[opening] #toast-container` hides a notification, so no frame is drawn at a wrong place, and a toast raised before or during a Popup's opening is drawn once `[opening]` has gone. When a dialog closes the containers leave for the body at `closing`, at the window place. The dimming covers the composer, so a bottom toast sits 24px from the window edge. `sheetToastInset()` makes a dialog that is a bottom sheet a bar for the toasts: its surface is read at its layout size (`layoutBoxOf()`, from the offsets while it has a transform), it reaches the bottom of the window within 4px and spans half the window, and with room above it (a toast and its margins between the top bar and the sheet, the room being the tallest toast on screen plus its margins, 82px when none shows) the toast sits above its top edge, and without room above the top of its bottom action row (`actionRowTop()`), or above its bottom edge when it has none. The strip's own edge is the clip edge, so the toast comes out of that edge. A notification does not follow the sheet (`placement.note`), and a notification beyond a toast follows the toast's place. A container that moves to another parent has no computed style, so it would jump (10px/ms at a dialog's close and 9px/ms at its open) where it should ease: `placeToastContainers()` resolves `getComputedStyle(container).bottom` before it writes with `glide`. Tests: `tests/toast-over-dialog.e2e.js` (a Popup, an alert, a Dialog, a Window and a raw `showModal()` at 1440 and 390, a sheet with room and tall sheets with and without an action row, a toast raised before and during a dialog's opening, a dialog that closes, Dismiss and the toast's button above the dialog, a toast and a notification together), `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-placement.test.js` (`sheetToastInset`, the dialog describe, the watch). A toast whose button has run goes, so a spec that presses it shows another toast for Dismiss.
- **A `:has()` rule that names `[role="button"]` made every write of a role restyle the document once more:** `welcome-slide-restyles.e2e.js` at 1440×900 failed from `1513f8cd4` with a median of 5 whole-document restyles per slide against a limit of 4 (the median is `sorted[4]` of eight slides, so five slides at 5 fail it; the counts went from `[4, 4, 2, 2, 4, 5, 2, 2]` to `[6, 5, 2, 2, 5, 6, 2, 2]`). The Welcome dialog writes `role`, `tabindex` and `class` on its `.menu_button`s when a page mounts, and the three toast rules `#sb-toast-overlay > .sb-form-toast:has(...)`, `> .toast-message:has(...)` and `.toast-message :where(:has(> ...))` listed `button, [role="button"], input[type="button"], input[type="submit"], input[type="reset"], .menu_button`. A trace of a forward slide showed the extra `UpdateLayoutTree` over the whole document ahead of the frame's animation callbacks, with the same mutations as before. Taking the stylesheet of `1513f8cd4` back, or the three `:has()` rules out, restored the counts, and each rule alone brought the extra restyle back. Narrowing the argument of all three to `button`, to `.menu_button` or to `input[type="button"]` removed it, and narrowing it to `[role="button"]` kept it (`[5, 5, 2, 2, 5, 6, 2, 2]`). The cause inside the browser was not read; the finding is the trace counts. `markButtons()` in `sillybunny-toast-form.js` now marks the toast (`sb-toast--button`) and the element that directly holds the button (`sb-toast-button-holder`) from `BUTTON_SELECTOR`, when a toast is placed, on its measure clone and when a caller changes its content (`watchToastButtons()`), and the CSS reads the markers inside `:where()` so that the specificity stays what it was. An in-app notification is not marked, because it keeps the markup of its caller (`toast-forms.e2e.js` pins it). Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-form.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-toast-queue.test.js` (the toast is marked at once, a card is not), `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js` (no toast rule has an attribute selector inside `:has()`), `tests/welcome-slide-restyles.e2e.js`.
- **Upstream's rules for `#toast-container` still apply to a notification, and one that has a pseudo-class beats a rule of the same class count:** `public/style.css` (`body #toast-container ...`), `public/css/popup.css` (`.popup #toast-container ...`) and toastr's own sheet style the container of the notifications and its cards. They were checked rule by rule with computed styles and boxes at the six positions, at 1440 and 390, with and without a Popup open. Fixed in `sillybunny-theme.css` (`2c464d249`): `body #toast-container .toast-close-button:focus { opacity: 0.7 }` has the specificity (1,2,1) and beat the form's `opacity: 1` rule (1,2,0), so a focused Dismiss was dimmed to 70% in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (the form's `:hover` rule already sets `opacity: 1`; `:focus` had none); and `.toast-message a` gave an anchor with no href the link colour, where the card's `label` rule already took the ink. Both are overridden by selectors keyed on the form (`tests/toast-notification-layout.e2e.js`, "keeps its full strength while it has focus" and "a link with an href is in the link colour"; `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js`). Kept, and checked to be harmless: `body #toast-container { margin-top }` (the top positions have a rule of their own, and at a bottom position a margin on the side that is not anchored moves nothing, so the box is where it was with the rule and without it); `body #toast-container > div { opacity, font-size }` (the card's own values); `.popup[opening] #toast-container { visibility: hidden }` (it keeps the strip from being drawn at a wrong place while a Popup, which is then the containing block, scales and clips it; the overlay has a copy); `.popup #toast-container { text-align: left }` (every card sets `text-align: start`); and the three `.popup #toast-container.toast-top-*` `top` rules (the strip's `[class]` rule has one more type selector, so `top: 0` wins in a Popup; `tests/toast-over-dialog.e2e.js`). Removed from `sillybunny-tabs.css`: `body :is(#toast-container, #sb-toast-overlay) { margin-top: var(--sb-topbar-layout-offset) }`, which the top rule of each container overrode and which moved nothing at a bottom position; the boxes of both containers were the same before and after at every position, with and without a Popup.
- **An error notification covers the Home menu button at a phone width, and stays until it is dismissed:** at 390×844 the card of "This window couldn't open" lies over `.sbHomeMenuButton`, so a click on the button lands on Dismiss. That is the design (an error stays, and a notification at the top covers what is under it); `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` "a window that fails to load logs the error, toasts, and can be opened again" dismisses the card before it presses the menu, as a user does. The same holds over a dialog: at 390×844 a "Settings could not be saved" card covers the Close button of the full-height Pathfinder Window, and `tests/pathfinder-settings.e2e.js` "390px Pathfinder editor keeps its existing Close result in a scrollable Window" dismisses it before it closes the Window. A spec that clicks near the top of the window at a phone width after an error has to dismiss the error first; the side-by-side full run of the branch against `d30a65077` found these two.
- **A toast takes the colours of a notification, and the contrast test holds both forms to WCAG on every bundled theme:** `tests/toast-theme-contrast.test.js` (resolver in `tests/helpers/toast-theme-colours.js` and `tests/helpers/css-colour.js`) computes each text, glyph, link and button pair of the toast and the notification for all 56 bundled themes from the stylesheets and the theme file (4.5:1 for text, 3:1 for a glyph; 1,176 checks). The toast was first drawn on a dark layer with white ink, and that failed 161 of 1,064 checks in 35 themes, because the layer is a mix of the theme's own colours and a mid-tone one (Solarized Light's `#816924`, a green or pink dark theme) gives a mid-tone pill: the fix was a lightness cap of 0.433 on the surface (libadwaita's own `#505053` is 0.4323, so the Libadwaita themes did not change), with the chroma scaled by `min(1, cap / l)` because capping the lightness alone left Sonokai Espresso Light's `#24746f` as a saturated `#07635e`, a status lift to 0.8 that the lifted red sets (3.08:1 on Sonokai Default Light, about 2.8:1 at 0.76), a link lift to 0.85, and white ink, which brought the toast to 0 failures (`3623bc53e`). T1 removed all of it (`daed1f1da`): `--sb-toast-ink`, `--sb-toast-surface`, the cap, the link floor, the lifted link tokens, the status lift (`--sb-toast-status`), their guarded `@supports` blocks and the toast's own focus ring are gone, and the toast is on the layer and in the ink of the notification, so each of its pairs equals the notification's (the test pins that for the label, the three link states, the text with no link and the three glyphs). What fails now is what the notification fails, in the same eight dark themes and for the same cause, so the test lists both in `KNOWN_FAILURES`: button text on the fill of the ink at 10% (GNOME Shell's fill; 4.11 to 4.49:1 in Glacier, Lime, Magenta, Mint, Nord, Slate, Solarized Dark and Sonokai Atlantis) and Solarized Dark's link and visited link (4.44 and 4.41:1, the theme's own accent on its own layer). A fix needs a value that is neither Shell's nor the theme's, which is a deviation, so it is the maintainers' to decide; the test fails when a case stops failing or a new one appears. The counts were 10 of 1,064 (the notification only) before T1 and are 20 of 1,176 after (the toast 10, the notification 10). The tooltips and the press-and-hold label keep `--sb-layer-toast` and `--sb-layer-toast-fg` (the body colour on the dark tone, white on the light tone, libadwaita's OSD text), and the test pins that their rules and their resolved colours did not change; the computed fill, ink, border and arrow of a tooltip were the same before and after T1 in Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2. The guard of every relative colour that is left (the two layers forced opaque, and the hover colour of a link) tests the functions it uses: `@supports (color: oklab(from red l a b))` is true in Safari 16.4, which implements an older draft where the channel keywords keep a percentage-like type (MDN, csswg-drafts #9955), so `calc()` or `min()` on a channel may be invalid there, and a custom property that the engine cannot read turns the background that uses it transparent. `tests/toast-theme-contrast.test.js` fails for a relative colour of either form that sits outside a guard with the same colour function and math, or that is a custom property with no unguarded declaration; it is unverified on a real Safari 16.4 or 17, and `BASELINE_CONDITIONS` in `tests/helpers/css-colour.js` holds the shaped guards as true. The resolver was checked against the real stylesheets in Chromium 156, Firefox 157 and WebKit 27.2 for the nine toast colours of every theme (504 per engine, within 1 channel unit but one: Dracula's button text, whose body colour is translucent, is painted over the pill by the check and over the button fill by the resolver, which is the resolver's reading). Reading it back has traps that gave false differences of up to 55 units: a computed `oklab()` colour drawn through a canvas `fillStyle` is gamut mapped and is not the pixel the element paints (read a screenshot instead); a theme switched in a page with the stylesheets' transitions reads a colour in the middle of the transition (set `transition: none`); a theme's Custom CSS sets `--sb-accent` in Black Orange, so the harness has to apply it; and a translucent colour, as in Dracula, has to be painted over its surface before it is read. Tests: `tests/toast-theme-contrast.test.js`, `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js`, `tests/focus-contrast-css.test.js`.
- **A sheet keeps clear of a notch:** the sheet host pads its sides with `env(safe-area-inset-left, 0px)` and `env(safe-area-inset-right, 0px)`, the action sheet's side padding is `max(8px, env(...))`, and the two JavaScript clamps that kept a floating layer 8px from the edge read `--sb-safe-area-left` and `--sb-safe-area-right` (declared in `:root`), because `env()` cannot be read from script. Playwright cannot fake an inset, so `tests/toast-sheet-css.test.js` pins the rules.
- **The colour picker sheet follows its content:** it had `height: 100%` on the sheet and on the menu, which filled a phone however short the content was. Without the sheet's height the menu's `height: 100%` has no definite parent, so the inner scroller grew instead of scrolling when the window got short; the menu now stretches as a flex item. Tests: `tests/sillybunny-color-picker.e2e.js` ("keeps the focused field visible when the mobile viewport contracts").

### Pixel art and illustrations

- **Pixel art is drawn at a whole number of device pixels per art pixel:** a picture scaled by a fraction draws some art pixels one device pixel wider than their neighbours, which shows as stripes. `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/pixel-art.js` rounds the screen scale to a step of at least 1; a panel keeps that step, and a banner steps down by whole steps. Where no whole step fits, the art is shrunk by a fraction and must be drawn smooth: `data-sb-art-fit='smooth'` switches it to `image-rendering: auto`, because under `pixelated` the shrunk art kept uneven blocks. Tests: `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js`, `tests/art-panels.e2e.js` ("the pair is shrunk to the body and drawn with smooth sampling").
- **Playwright's `deviceScaleFactor` is not a screen scale:** it lays the page out in CSS pixels, so a check of even art pixels sees seams that a real screen does not have. `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` launches Chromium with `--force-device-scale-factor` and a window of the size it wants, with `viewport: null`, at scales 1, 1.1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2 and 3; 1.1 and 1.75 are what browser zoom gives on 1x and 2x screens, and 1.1 is reported as the nearest float.
- **Read a window's real padding:** Compact density changes a window body's padding and row gap, so `art-layout.js` measures them and passes them to the CSS (`--sb-art-inset`, `--sb-art-inset-block`, `--sb-art-row-gap`) instead of assuming the default 32px and 16px. Test: `tests/art-panels.e2e.js` ("a body with the padding of Compact density").
- **The row metrics of an art dialog are load-bearing:** `.sb-dialog:has(.sbArtFigure)` in `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` keeps the 44px rows, the 52px subtitle rows, the 16px inset and the 12px gap. Without it Welcome at a screen scale of 3 is 1437.438 device pixels tall instead of 1437, the centred figure sits at 886.281 instead of 886.5, and `art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` (the layers of the figures of Welcome are even blocks while they move) fails with 41781 uneven device pixels. Snapping the figure onto a whole device pixel (`alignToDevicePixels` for panels outside a transformed ancestor) fails ten more evenness tests, because `LAYER_EDGE_BIAS_CSS_PX` is tuned for a figure that the browser places itself. A way to drop the exception has to change the layer bias with it, and be checked at every scale in Chromium and WebKit.
- **Widening a window can lag a frame:** Help's sidebar follows a new window width in a `requestAnimationFrame` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-editor/list-shell.js`), so the first read after a widening measures too little gain. `planWidening` (`art-layout.js`) probes, corrects by the gain it measured, takes the widening back when the gain is under 5%, and stops after two rounds; the window never grows past `innerWidth`. Tests: `planWidening` in `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-layout.test.js`; Help at 1024x600 in `tests/art-panels.e2e.js`.
- **Size every splash picture by the tallest one:** sizing each picture by its own height made Tewi larger in her own splash than in the pair. `chooseFit()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-splash.js` takes the step from `TALLEST_ROWS`, the height of the tallest picture, and `public/css/loader.css` draws a fractional fit smooth (`.sb-boot-mascot[data-fit='smooth']`). Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` ("is drawn at the step of the tallest picture: Tewi is not enlarged to fill the share").
- **A resolution query fires once:** the splash makes a new `(resolution: …dppx)` query after each change of screen scale, and a query made for a splash that has gone would keep the removed markup alive, so it stops once the preloader is removed. Test: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` ("does not ask for the next change once the preloader has gone").
- **A spec rule and the test that reads the spec change together:** `f4c3fa2c8` made pixel slots of `assets-src/branding/export-specs/shell.json` refuse `css` (`parsePixelAsset`, `scripts/lib/export-art.mjs`), while `test/export-shell.test.mjs` still required `css` on every slot and stayed red through three commits. The test now checks the pixel-slot rules: native size of the framed master against the app's art table (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-sources.js`), `approved/pixel/<id>.png` masters, and only the two portrait rows smooth. When a spec key changes meaning, run the package's tests that read that spec in the same commit. Tests outside the package read it too: pointing the splash slots at their loop masters (`approved/pixel/splash3-<variant>--<frame>.png`) passed `test/export-shell.test.mjs`, but `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` still expected `approved/pixel/splash-<variant>.png`, because the splash code and the spec were changed in separate trees. The app's tests now read `assets-src/art/art-manifest.json` through `tests/helpers/art-manifest.js`, not `shell.json`: `tests/unit/sillybunny-splash.test.js` expects each splash still to repeat (`from`) the frame its loop opens on, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-sources.test.js` checks the Home table against the manifest's grids and motion files, and `tests/unit/sillybunny-art-manifest.test.js` checks the size of every listed picture. When the manifest changes, run those three files and `npm test --prefix scripts/art`, which derives the art again and proves it byte for byte.
- **A motion description that a run left is built into the player's, not copied:** a motion master of `assets-src/art/art-manifest.json` may be the description that a run of the art pipeline left (`format` 1 and a `kind`, `splash-loop` or `motion-frames`). `node scripts/art/cli.js derive` then builds the splash player's `--anim.json` or the breath player's `--breath.json` from it and from the asset's entry (`scripts/art/lib/motion.js`). Each picture of the run is the asset's picture whose master has the run's file name; a run that names another picture or leaves one of the asset's out, a loop that opens on another frame than the still repeats, and a scene layer off its box fail with `motion-mismatch`, so a frame set and its entry cannot drift apart. The two values that no run gives, the `cell` of the description's grid and a splash loop's `layer` id, live in the asset's `motion` block. A player's own description, as the shipped art was adopted with, still ships as it is. Test: `scripts/art/test/motion.test.js` builds the three shipped splash descriptions and the five shipped breath descriptions from the run's form of each, byte for byte.
- **An animated asset drawn denser than it is shown is fitted as whole frames, never as small layers:** a still or splash loop at a ratio above 1 (up to 2) ships each frame as the fit of its whole master, so its layer is the whole grid and `motion.js` only brings the run's master pixels (an effect's `x` and `y`, a breath's waist) onto the placement grid with `cellIndex` (`scripts/art/lib/fit.js`). A scene's figure layers cannot be fitted on their own: a layer picture fitted alone puts its edges on other cell borders than the scene's fit does, so the layers would no longer give the fitted frame. `fitScene` (`scripts/art/lib/scene-fit.js`) composes each frame on the master's canvas, fits and frames it exactly as the still, and ships a layer's picture as what that frame shows beyond the layers drawn before it. The layer's `box` is then measured, so `derive --write` records it (a dry `derive` lists `layerBoxesStale`, and `prove` reports `manifest-stale` for a box that is not the measured one), and a scene at a fit needs the run's description, which places each layer in the master's pixels. `fitScene` lays every combination of one picture per layer over the fitted backdrop and refuses with `composition-mismatch` when one is not the fit of the whole frame, which two layers whose pictures share a cell can cause. Tests: `scripts/art/test/ratio.test.js` (splash loops, a breath still and a scene with two overlapping figures at 3/2, 5/3 and 2).
- **A derived card keeps its text, and its new picture needs a new managed version:** a `card` entry of `assets-src/art/art-manifest.json` names the sprite master, the crop, the gradient and, as the source of its text, the card file itself, so `node scripts/art/cli.js derive --write` redraws the picture and keeps the text that the file holds, an edit saved from the app included (`scripts/art/lib/card.js`, `scripts/art/lib/card-entry.js`). The managed manifest (`default/content/sillybunny-managed.json`) records a fingerprint and a version of each bundled card, and a card that changes without a new version never reaches the installs that hold the earlier one. `node scripts/art/cli.js check` reports such a card as `managed-stale`, and `node scripts/art/cli.js bump --write` refreshes the manifest through `src/sillybunny-managed-content.js`, raises the version, keeps the earlier fingerprint and writes the version into the card's `character_version` (`scripts/art/lib/managed.js`). Run it after `derive --write`: without it `tests/unit/sillybunny-shipped-managed-content.test.js` fails on the new card. Tests: `scripts/art/test/card-entry.test.js`, `scripts/art/test/managed.test.js`.
- **An expression pack ships its sprites at one whole step, and its folder is one managed `sprites` item:** a `pack` entry of `assets-src/art/art-manifest.json` has a `grid` (the sprites' native grid) and, per expression, a native master and one `webp-lossless` file named after the expression, all in one folder directly under `default/content/` and all at the same `scale` (`scripts/art/lib/pack-entry.js`, `derivePack` in `scripts/art/lib/derive.js`). The sprites are placed on the grid before they are delivered, so the derive only draws each art pixel as `scale` by `scale` pixels; a pack is never stored lossy and never resized by a filter, whatever its total size. The app manages the folder as one `sprites` item, and a file of it that changes without a new version never reaches the installs that hold the earlier one: `check` reports `managed-stale`, and `bump --write` raises the item's version once and keeps each file's earlier hash (`scripts/art/lib/managed.js`, which compares each type of item with its own rule). A sprites folder that the art manifest does not ship but that changed makes `bump --write` refuse with `managed-unrelated`. A folder the manifest still pins (`expressions-reisen`, `expressions-tewi`) is not touched. Tests: `scripts/art/test/pack.test.js`, which proves the type on synthetic masters.
- **The 16 and 32 px icons are hand work on top of a draft, and the fix is tied to that draft:** `assets-src/branding/scripts/icons.mjs` draws the 16 and 32 px icons as an area-coverage draft of `approved/logo-plate-nolines.svg` and applies `assets-src/branding/fixes/logo-16.json` and `logo-32.json` on top. The edits are absolute colours, so they only fit the draft they were made against. `applyHandFix` (`scripts/lib/icon-set.mjs`) refuses a fix whose listed pixels are not all changed, or that leaves a pixel inside the outer eighth that is not solid and a colour of the SVG; before it existed, a shifted drawing still built and would have shipped a mix of the new draft and the old fix. After the logo changes, redo the fix against the new draft. Tests: `assets-src/branding/test/icons.test.mjs` ("a pixel-fix that does not fit its draft stops the build", "a stale pixel-fix stops a write run"). Each shipped icon is also a master in `assets-src/art/master/logo/`, so a new icon set goes in as new masters with `node scripts/art/cli.js derive --write`; an icon file written by hand fails `prove` with `output-stale`.
- **Check which file a `srcset` picture shows, not only that it loaded:** the Home and About logos take the hand-fixed PNG at 1x and a larger file at higher screen scales (`public/scripts/templates/sillybunnyHome.html`, `createLogo()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-about.js`). Any entry loads a picture, so `naturalWidth > 0` passes with the wrong one. `expectRealLogo` (`tests/helpers/logo.js`) compares `currentSrc` with the file meant for `devicePixelRatio`, and the scale tests in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` and `tests/home-windows.e2e.js` run it at scales 1, 2 and 3. With About's 2x entry pointed at the PNG, the scale 2 and 3 tests failed and the scale 1 test passed.
- **`@import`ed stylesheets are outside the budget gate:** `scripts/check-frontend-budgets.js` counts the `<link>` stylesheets of `public/index.html`, not the 18 sheets that `public/style.css` imports (about 157 KiB on 2026-11-04), which also block the first render. Moving rules into an imported sheet passes the gate without making the first render cheaper.
- **A class that code only queries is dead, and a literal grep misses a built one:** the dead-rule pass of the blocking sheets treats a class, id or `data-` attribute as dead when no runtime file creates or sets it. A name that only a selector string names does not count: `document.getElementById('sb-chatbar-layer')` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js` and `.matches('.sb-theme-slider-caption')` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-presentation.js` query elements that nothing builds, and the shell's header and tab rail are no longer built (`sillybunny-tabs.js`, "the in-shell header and horizontal tab rail are no longer built"). A name can also be built from parts: `streaming-display-btn` is `` `${CSS_PREFIX}-btn` `` in `public/scripts/streaming-display.js`, and `sb-shell-root-left` is `` `sb-shell-root-${shellKey}` `` in `sillybunny-tabs.js`, so a rule is kept when a prefix or suffix of its name is built. Names that a library creates (`.select2-*`, `.ui-*`, `.toast-*`, `.tooltip-inner`, `data-popper-placement`) never show in the repository's scripts and stay. A rule that no coverage run reaches is not dead on that evidence alone: coverage cannot reach `:hover`, `:focus-visible`, forced colours or an error state.
- **The old character editor's controls are gone from `public/style.css` (task 10.4c):** `#avatar_controls`, the `.char-button-group*` and `.char-button-toolbar` rules, `.sb-character-editor-side-actions`, `.greetings-editor-shell`, `.character-greeting-label-row` and the phone `@media` block that stretched them were left in after the editor rebuild; no markup or script builds any of them (`tests/mobile-character-editor-css.test.js` now asserts their absence in `style.css` and the mobile shell). The same pass removed rules for markup that SillyBunny deleted from upstream's templates: `.nanogpt-credits-popup`, `.editable-slider-notification`, `.swipe_picker_header`, `.select_chat_actions` (`style.css`) and the `.toggleOn`/`.toggleOff` rules (`toggle-dependent.css`). `.alternate_greeting_actions` stays in `style.css`, because `#alternate_greeting_form_template` in `public/index.html` still carries it, and `.alternate_greeting` is also a class of the rebuilt editor's greeting rows. A dead selector in `style.css` is removed only with proof that its markup is gone from this tree; upstream utility classes that nothing here uses (such as `.flex3`) and classes that a library builds (`.hljs-*`) stay.
- **Removing a dead id from a `:not()` changes the cascade (measured, task 10.4b):** `:not(#bg_selection_mode_button)` in the icon-only `.menu_button` rules of `public/css/sillybunny-theme.css` and `public/css/mobile-styles.css` names an id that nothing creates since the Backgrounds gallery rebuild, and it is still load-bearing: the id raises the specificity of every selector of the `.menu_button:not(...)` pair by one id, so those rules beat every one-id rule that sets `width`, `min-width` or `border-radius` on an icon-only button. Served without it (`:not(#bg_selection_mode_button)` removed from all six selectors), 41 elements at 1440x900 and 21 at 768x1024 and 390x844 computed differently on the page at boot (for example `#ui_preset_import_button` went from 38px to 26.9px wide, and `#model_openai_picker_toggle` from a 9px to a 50% radius). A dead id inside a `:not()` therefore stays until the rules it competes with are known; a dead alternative inside an `:is()` is different when the other alternatives keep the rule above every competitor (the `#bg_selection_mode_button:not(:has(...))` arm of the Backgrounds icon-button rule in `sillybunny-tabs.css` went, with 0 differences in 18 captured states, because the rule keeps three ids and no competing rule has more than two). Compare the computed style of every `.menu_button` and Backgrounds element with the sheet served both ways (`page.route` on the stylesheet) before an id leaves a selector.
- **Injected boot scripts are outside the budget gate too:** the splash loop player (`public/scripts/sillybunny-splash-loop.js`) is injected by `sillybunny-splash.js`, so `scripts/check-frontend-budgets.js`, which counts the static tags of `public/index.html`, never sees it, but it and its frames still download during boot. Injected as soon as the still had loaded, fixture loops delayed `sb-app-ready` by a median of 139 ms on desktop; injected when the browser is idle (`requestIdleCallback`, at most `LOOP_IDLE_LIMIT_MS`), the delay was within noise. Measure `sb-app-ready` with `scripts/measure-frontend-performance.js` before listing a loop. `/scripts/` is network-first in `public/sw.js`, so an edited loop script reaches users anyway; the key chain is still: edit the loop, bump `LOOP_SCRIPT_VERSION` in `sillybunny-splash.js`, then bump that script's `?v=` in `index.html`.
- **A splash loop starts on its still, and Home's motion counts leave the splash out:** each splash still is the first frame of its loop (`public/img/sillybunny/splash-*--anim.json`), so the loop takes over without a visible change. `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` compares frame 0 with the still as decoded pixels and as painted at scales 1, 1.5, 2 and 3; a different still fails both checks. The splash frames share `/img/sillybunny/` with Home's motion frames, so `tests/art-motion.e2e.js` and `tests/art-motion-frames.e2e.js`, which count the motion requests to that folder, pass `splash-` files through; before that, the splash's own requests failed both specs.
- **A playing splash loop hides the still:** `play()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-splash-loop.js` sets the still's `visibility` to `hidden` while the loop's layers show the picture, so a wait for `.sb-boot-mascot` to be visible times out when the loop starts before the wait's first check. `splashPicture()` in `tests/splash.e2e.js` waits for the loop's box (`.sb-boot-loop`) once it is in the page, and for the still before that. Tests that need the still itself turn the loop off with reduced motion, as the forced-colours describe in that spec and `tests/splash-loop-frames.e2e.js` do.
- **The hero panel's padding counts against the band:** the Home hero sits in an `.sbArtPanel`, so its whole step is chosen from the band width less the panel's padding, which `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js` reads from the panel's own style. With the day art 384 px wide, a fixed step at scale 1 needs a 466 px container (384 + 2×12 panel + 2×16 band + 2×12 layout + 2 border), which no phone reaches, so phones draw the hero at a smaller whole step (256 CSS px at 3x, 192 at 2x) and 1x screens below that width draw it smooth. Narrower panel and band padding on phones was tried and removed: no padding a panel can keep reaches 466. A test that reads a fitted picture's size must poll (`settledRead`, `tests/helpers/settled-read.js`), because the fit lands a frame or two after the render.
- **Layered motion keeps whole art pixels:** the art player (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-player.js`) lays its frames over the still at the art-pixel size the layout sets (`--sb-art-unit`), and places them again after they decode, because a resize during the decode changes the step. Under a smooth fit (`data-sb-art-fit='smooth'`) art pixels are not whole device pixels, so the player keeps the still and loads nothing, and the layout calls `refresh()` after every fit. When real frames are listed in `PIXEL_MOTION`, bump `ART_VERSION` (`art-sources.js`), because `public/sw.js` serves `/img/`, the scene JSON included, stale-while-revalidate by full URL; `node scripts/art/cli.js bump --write` does it when the frames are in the art manifest. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-player.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-motion.test.js`, `tests/art-motion.e2e.js`. Each layer edge also gets a small bias, `LAYER_EDGE_BIAS_CSS_PX` (5/128 CSS px, `pixel-art.js`): a centred figure often stands on a half device pixel, and the engines cut lengths to 1/64 CSS px units, so without it some layer edges snap one device pixel off their neighbours. The working window is narrow (1/32 to 3/64 pass in Chromium and WebKit; 1/64 and 1/16 fail in WebKit), so re-run the layered `tests/art-pixel-evenness.e2e.js` cases in both engines when it changes. Snapping the figure's origin to a whole device pixel fixed Chromium but not every WebKit scale.
- **Larger text widens the art panels' content floor:** the content column keeps `MIN_CONTENT_WIDTH` (360px) times `--fontScale` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-layout.js`), so at 200% text on a 1024×600 window a one-figure window widens and keeps its panel, while About, with two figures, flips to a banner (`tests/art-panels.e2e.js`).
- **A layout that must follow the text size needs an `em` container condition:** inside an `@container` condition, `em` is the query container's font size (`welcome-home` is 15px times `--fontScale`), so a threshold in `em` moves with the text size and one in `px` does not. The Home tiles stack into one column below `calc(15em + 40px)`, a width no screen reaches at normal text, because at 200% text on a 390px phone three tiles in one row cut their titles and broke words mid-way. Test: the `home-page` surface in `tests/helpers/dialog-accessibility-surfaces.js` (no clipped labels at 200% text).
- **A slot that tests only measure as a box can ship as a placeholder:** the Reisen and Tewi Home cards showed grey boxes that spelled `row-reisen` and `row-tewi`, because `describeAssistant` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-cards.js` gave them `art.avatar: null`, so `createCardArt` never added a picture, and `createArtPlaceholder` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art.js`) wrote its asset id into the box as text. The tests for the portraits read the size and corners of `[data-sb-art-placeholder]` and one of them read `image-rendering` of the box itself, which is `auto` for any `div`, so a box with the right size and no picture passed. Each card now shows the thumbnail of its own library card (`getThumbnailUrl('avatar', avatar)`, as the third slot and the chat avatar do), a card with no file shows the empty tinted box, and the placeholder never writes its name (the name stays in `data-sb-art-placeholder` for styles and specs). Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("show the thumbnail of their own card on every card", "show no art name as text", "shows a tinted box … with no picture and no text"); the `image-rendering` check in `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` reads the picture; `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-art-placeholder.test.js`.
- **Tewi's card picture is zoomed on Home so her head matches Reisen's:** her card draws her head about 0.8 of Reisen's, so the shared `object-fit: cover` crop showed her whole head and much of her body. `cardFraming` (`zoom: 1.22`, `origin: '50% 22%'`) on her entry in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/assistant-registry.js` reaches the `img` as an inline `transform` and `transform-origin` through `describeAssistant` (`assistant-cards.js`) and `createCardArt` (`assistants-view.js`); the art box already clips (`overflow: hidden`). Reisen and the pointer card have no framing. The field is not called `portrait`, because `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-registry.test.js` reserves that name for a card to create. A zoomed picture's `getBoundingClientRect()` is larger than its box, so a test of the layout size reads `offsetWidth` (`tests/home-assistants.e2e.js`, "show the thumbnail of their own card…" failed on the rectangle until it did). Tests: `tests/home-assistants.e2e.js` ("zoom the picture of Tewi in on her face…"), `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-cards.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistants-view.test.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-assistant-registry.test.js`.
- **The Home hero is balanced on its weight, not its box:** both hero pictures have their figures left of the middle and the sky on the right, so a panel centred on the picture showed it leaning left on its lavender (by day 28 art pixels, by night 19). `getHeroCentre()` in `public/scripts/sillybunny-home/art-sources.js` holds the opacity-weighted centre of each still, and `balancingLead()` in `pixel-art.js` gives the panel that much room on the left, only from what the band has to spare, in an even number of device pixels, so a hero fitted to a phone keeps its size. The room is a physical left padding (`--sb-art-lead`), because the picture is not mirrored in a right-to-left layout. Measure the centre again when a hero picture changes. Read the panel's base padding from its smaller side: subtracting the last room from the reported padding drifts by the 1/64px rounding of layout and drops an even step where the room is the limit. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-home-pixel-art.test.js`, `tests/home-hero-art.e2e.js` (the rule written out), `tests/home-layout.e2e.js` (the panel centred, the weight centred where the band has room).

### Connection status and Connect buttons

- **Breaks:** Cancel stays visible and every Connect button stays disabled after the work that started the loading state has ended, so the user cannot connect again. `startStatusLoading()` shows `.api_loading` and adds `disabled` to every `.api_button`, and `.menu_button.disabled` has `pointer-events: none` (`public/style.css`). Only `stopStatusLoading()`, which `resultCheckStatus()` calls, undoes it.
- **Cause:** `cancelStatusCheck()` aborts the status check and sets the status to `no_connection`, but it leaves the loading state. A change of the main API calls `cancelStatusCheck()` alone (`public/script.js`), and only Chat Completion then reconnects (`reconnectOpenAi()` in `public/scripts/openai.js`). A Text Completion type change goes through the same `#main_api` handler and clicks Connect only when the new type needs no URL or has one (`public/scripts/textgen-settings.js`), and deleting or rotating an API key triggers that handler too (`public/scripts/secrets.js`). Work that ends on that abort without `resultCheckStatus()` leaves the buttons disabled. The loading state is one state for all work, so work that ends it also ends it for a status check that still waits for its answer.
- **Files:** `public/script.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/openai.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/textgen-settings.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/secrets.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.js`.
- **Guards:** the Cancel button calls `resultCheckStatus()` after `cancelStatusCheck()` (#836). The KoboldCpp model switch ends its restart wait when the status check aborts, when the KoboldCpp URL changes and when the script that ran `/kcpp-model` stops. Then `switchKoboldCppModel()` calls `stopStatusLoading()` itself (#941). After a cancel or a timeout it ends the loading state only when no status check started during the wait: `startStatusLoading()` counts each start through `countStatusLoadingStart()`, and a status check that started during the wait, such as the Chat Completion reconnect after an API or source change, ends the loading state itself when it answers (`6d380a937`). The switch uses the URL and the selected connection of the mode it started in, Text Completion or Chat Completion (#943). `startStatusLoading()` also sets the pending status flag with `setStatusCheckPending(true)` and `stopStatusLoading()` clears it, so a switch that leaves the loading state to a later status check also leaves the page in the connecting state (`data-sb-connection`) until that check ends.
- **Tests:** `tests/openai-status-reconnect.test.js`, `tests/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.test.js`, `tests/koboldcpp-model-switcher.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** #836 (manual cancel), #941 (KoboldCpp model switch after a connection change or a stopped script), #943 (the same in Chat Completion mode), `6d380a937` (a status check that starts during a model switch, and a deleted API key while KoboldCpp stays selected), `82e5b7338` (`6d380a937` in Chat Completion mode).

### Server state after a URL change

- **Breaks:** after the server URL changes, SillyBunny keeps using what the old server reported. For KoboldCpp this was the restart wait and model list of a model switch, and in Chat Completion mode the loaded model name, which keys token counts in the cache, with its context size and media support.
- **Cause:** the URL fields and `/api-url` change the URL at once, and the state stays until a status check, `/kcpp-model` or a finished switch fetches it again. A URL typed without Connect runs no status check, and `refreshKoboldCppModels()` returns without a change while a model switch waits.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/tokenizers.js` (upstream).
- **Guards:** the restart wait stops when the URL of its connection mode changes, and the list for the new URL loads when the wait ends (#941). Each switcher state records the URL that reported it, and `getKoboldCppChatState()` returns null for another URL, so a token count from the new server is not cached under the old model's name (#943).
- **Limit:** the context cap and media support that a Chat Completion status check applies while a model switch waits can stay until the next status check. A model that KoboldCpp loads without SillyBunny keeps the old name until the next status check.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.test.js`, `tests/koboldcpp-model-switcher.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** #941 (restart wait and list after a URL change), #943 (Chat Completion state after a URL change).

### KoboldCpp model list during a model switch

- **Breaks:** after a model switch ends by a cancel or a timeout, the KoboldCpp model dropdown of either connection mode shows its placeholder until the next Connect, although KoboldCpp answered a status check during the switch, for example the one that Connect starts right after Cancel or the Chat Completion reconnect after a change of the main API.
- **Cause:** `refreshKoboldCppModels()` returns without a change while a model switch waits, because KoboldCpp may still be restarting. A timeout or cancel clears the list, because KoboldCpp may still be loading, and the switch loaded the list again only when the URL had changed.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.js`, `public/scripts/textgen-settings.js` (upstream), `public/scripts/openai.js` (upstream).
- **Guards:** `refreshKoboldCppModels()` records a refresh that it skips during a switch, for each connection mode. When the switch ends without a restart, `switchKoboldCppModel()` loads the list of the selected KoboldCpp connection, Text Completion or Chat Completion, if a status check asked for it during the switch or, in the mode of the switch, the URL changed (`bc32b0baa`, `82e5b7338`).
- **Limit:** after a restart, the switch loads only the list of its own mode, so it drops a refresh that a status check of the other mode asked for during the switch. That needs a change of the selected connection to the other mode during the switch, for example after `/kcpp-model` started the switch while KoboldCpp was not the selected connection.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-koboldcpp-models.test.js`, `tests/koboldcpp-model-switcher.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** `83329cf61` (list after a URL change), `bc32b0baa` (list after a status check during the switch), `82e5b7338` (the same in Chat Completion mode, also after a change of the main API during a Text Completion switch).

### Provider errors and connection feedback

- **Breaks:** a failed request shows only the HTTP reason phrase ("Bad Request", "Unauthorized"), so a bad key, a missing model and a provider outage look alike.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/sillybunny-request-errors.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-provider-message.js`, the failed-response sites in `public/script.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js`, `textgen-settings.js`, `kai-settings.js`, `nai-settings.js`, `horde.js` and `custom-request.js`, and on the server `src/util.js` and `src/endpoints/backends/chat-completions.js`.
- **Guards:** `showRequestError` shows the provider's own message, with a hint for 401/403, 404, 429 and 5xx. The server turns a provider 401 into 400 so the browser keeps its Basic auth, and sends the original status in `X-Upstream-Status`, which `getFailureStatus` reads.
- **Guards:** `extractProviderMessage` gives up only on a top-level body that looks like JSON and does not parse. A nested message that starts with `[` or `{`, such as `[400 Bad Request] API key not valid`, is the provider's own sentence; dropping it left the toast with the bare reason phrase.
- **Guards:** Send with no connection (`online_status` `no_connection`, or not known yet) shows the "Not connected to API!" notice, whose Show Me opens Connections, and leaves the draft in the box (`sendTextareaMessage` and the Send button handler in `public/script.js`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-connection-notices.js`). Before, Enter posted the user's message with no reply and no word why. Slash commands still run offline.
- **Keyboard presses are untrusted clicks:** upstream `public/scripts/keyboard.js` turns Enter on a `<div class="menu_button">` into `target.click()`, which has `isTrusted: false`. Telling a user's Connect from an automatic one by `isTrusted` left keyboard users without the missing-key feedback; `onConnectButtonClick` in `public/scripts/openai.js` checks for `e.originalEvent`, which jQuery's `.trigger('click')` does not set.
- **A toast's own `onHidden` replaces the global one:** toastr's per-toast `onHidden` overrides the global hook that calls `fixToastrForDialogs()` (`public/script.js`), so the connection notices call it themselves.
- **Invalid fields lost their focus colour:** surface focus rules that include an id, such as `#sb-settings-page … input:focus`, beat `[aria-invalid]:focus`, so a focused invalid field looked like any focused field. `:is(.text_pole, select)[aria-invalid='true']` sets `--sb-composer-focus-border` and `--sb-composer-focus-ring`, which those rules read (`public/css/sillybunny-theme.css`); no `!important`.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-request-errors.test.js` (the message matrix), `tests/sillybunny-request-error-sites.test.js` (the Kobold, NovelAI, Horde and custom-request sites), `tests/request-errors.e2e.js`, `tests/sillybunny-connection-notices.test.js`, `tests/connection-notices.e2e.js` (Enter on a focused Connect button included).
- **Fixes:** #870 (request errors; Send and Connect feedback).
- **Offline at boot is not offline:** `online_status` is `no_connection` from the start of every boot until the saved connection's status check ends. `changeMainAPI()` sets it and starts the check, nothing awaits the check, and Home renders at `APP_READY` (`public/script.js` `getSettings()`, `public/scripts/welcome-screen.js`), so Home can render while the check is still in flight. A failed check leaves the status at `no_connection`, so `ONLINE_STATUS_CHANGED` never fires for it. A surface that shows something for "offline" therefore reads `data-sb-connection` on `<html>` (`connecting`, `offline` or `online`; absent until the first hook runs) and shows it with CSS only, so no panel adds a listener per mount. `public/scripts/sillybunny-connection-state.js` writes it from `setOnlineStatus` (on every call), `startStatusLoading`, `stopStatusLoading`, and `changeMainAPI`, which clears the state when the API changes and sets it for the Horde check (`public/script.js`). The Connect a Model button on the assistant cards (`.sbHomeCardConnect`) is the first user. Cancel (`.api_loading`) ends the state through `resultCheckStatus()`.
  - **A pending state can stick** where a check starts and nothing ends it, which hides the button and keeps the spinner on. A Chat Completion check that a newer check or a change of `#main_api` supersedes returns from `getStatusOpen` without `resultCheckStatus()` (`public/scripts/openai.js`), so it never ends its own state. The newer check ends it; a change of `#main_api` has no newer check, so `changeMainAPI()` clears the state itself right after it sets `no_connection`, and a check that is still in flight is already cancelled by then (a held request answered after the change leaves the state at `offline`; `tests/home-offline-link.e2e.js`). The Chat Completion status checks that run without `startStatusLoading()` (the OpenRouter, Chutes and Electron Hub model sort changes and the bypass switch in `openai.js`) set no pending state and, apart from the bypass and no-validate cases that set an online status at once, no status before the response, so the state stays as it was and only a failed check moves it, to `offline`.
  - **Known limit:** a Text Completion, Kobold or Novel check that a newer check supersedes catches its abort, sets `no_connection` and calls `resultCheckStatus()` (`getStatusTextgen` in `public/scripts/textgen-settings.js`, `getStatusKobold`, `getStatusNovel`), which clears the pending state while the newer check is still in flight. The state then reads `offline`, so the button can appear and vanish. It takes a change of type or URL, or a second Connect, during an open check; it never happens at boot. It is not fixed.
  - **Tests:** `tests/unit/sillybunny-connection-state.test.js` (the rule, the attribute writes, no listener), `tests/home-offline-link.e2e.js` (a held status request: no frame shows the button while the check is open, it shows after a failed check, never when the check connects, and a user with no key sees it from the first frame that has it; the Horde start; Cancel; a check superseded by a change of `#main_api`; the 44 px touch target).

### Connection profiles and Custom endpoint profiles

- **Breaks:** a connection profile or a Custom endpoint profile selects the wrong endpoint, key or Additional Parameters.
- **Files:** `public/scripts/openai.js` (`setCustomEndpointPreset`, `loadCustomEndpointPresets`, `syncCustomEndpointPresetSelectionBySecretId`, `bindCustomEndpointParameterFields`, `runCustomEndpointPresetCallback`), `public/scripts/openai-preset-utils.js` (`fillMissingCustomEndpointParameters`, `getCustomEndpointProfileRequestOverrides`), `public/scripts/extensions/connection-manager/index.js`, `public/scripts/extensions/shared.js` (`getChatCompletionProfileRequestOverrides`), `public/script.js` (`getSettings`, and the `custom_endpoint_presets` spread in the `saveSettings` payload, which persists every key a profile holds).
- **Guards:**
  - Connection profiles record the endpoint profile by name, because secret ids are not unique across endpoint profiles: saving one without typing the key again binds it to the active secret. `CC_COMMANDS` applies `custom-endpoint-profile` after `secret-id` and before `api-url`, `model` and the three Additional Parameters commands, so the connection profile's own values win.
  - An endpoint profile stores the Additional Parameters (`custom_include_body`, `custom_exclude_body`, `custom_include_headers`) under the live settings' keys. The live values belong to the selected profile, so `loadCustomEndpointPresets()` gives only the selected profile, and never None, the live value of each one it lacks (`fillMissingCustomEndpointParameters()`) and saves the settings. It never fills the other profiles: the live headers can hold the token of one provider, and a copy would send it to another provider when that profile is selected. A profile that lacks them leaves the current values when it is selected, as before the profiles stored them. The fill reads `oai_settings`, so `getSettings()` must run `loadOpenAISettings()` before `loadCustomEndpointPresets()`. Selecting None clears them, as it clears the URL, key and model, because `normalizeCustomEndpointPreset()` gives None empty values. `setCustomEndpointPreset()` changes them only when the caller passes `parameters`. The load-time apply in `loadCustomEndpointPresets()` passes no `parameters`: the saved settings already hold the values in effect, and a connection profile can set them after its endpoint profile.
  - An edit in the Additional Parameters dialog is written to `oai_settings` and to the selected profile, except None (`bindCustomEndpointParameterFields()`), so the next settings save stores it and selecting the profile again brings it back. Written to `oai_settings` alone, the edit was replaced by the stored values at the next selection. The dialog's fields are not written from `setCustomEndpointPreset()`. When the selection changes while the dialog is open, the next edit shows the live values again and is dropped, because the text in the fields belongs to the previous profile.
  - `syncCustomEndpointPresetSelectionBySecretId()` also selects an endpoint profile while a connection profile without an endpoint profile name, or with a deleted one, is applied. A change of selection there writes the stored values of the new profile to `oai_settings`, empty ones for None, and a profile that stores none leaves them; the connection profile's own values come later and win. Only that application calls it, so no load or background rotation changes the live values.
  - A request through a connection profile (`ConnectionManagerRequestService.sendRequest`) runs none of its commands, so it never selects the endpoint profile. `getChatCompletionProfileRequestOverrides()` takes each Additional Parameter that the connection profile does not record from the endpoint profile it names (`getCustomEndpointProfileRequestOverrides()`), only for a profile of the Custom source, because a stale endpoint profile name on another source would otherwise send Custom values; without that, the request used whatever endpoint profile was selected last, while applying the same connection profile used its own endpoint profile's values.
- **Tests:** `tests/openai-custom-endpoint-credentials.test.js` ("loading profiles that store no Additional Parameters", "editing in the Additional Parameters dialog", "applying a connection profile that selects an endpoint profile by its secret", and Delete falling back to None), `tests/openai-preset-utils.test.js`, `tests/openai-proxy-preset-wiring.test.js` (the live settings load before the endpoint profiles), `tests/connection-manager-profile-save.test.js`, `tests/connection-profile-request-fields.test.js` ("Additional Parameters from the Custom endpoint profile", including a connection profile of another source).
- **Fixes:** #560 (bind endpoint profiles to their saved secrets), #621 (persist the endpoint profile secret), #765 (select endpoint profiles by name and record the Additional Parameters in connection profiles), #1006 (store the Additional Parameters with each endpoint profile).

### Upstream compatibility and sync

- **Breaks:** the version map reports the wrong SillyTavern base, so extensions that need a newer SillyTavern fail.
- **Breaks:** a merged SillyTavern condition on a reasoning effort changes meaning, because the two projects map efforts differently. On GPT-6 Sol and Luna, SillyTavern sends Min as `'none'`. SillyBunny sends Min as `'minimal'` and None as `'none'`.
- **Files:** `docs/upstream-sync.md`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-version-map.js`, `public/scripts/openai.js`, `public/scripts/tool-calling.js`, `src/endpoints/backends/`.
- **Guards:** the version map clamps a SillyBunny version with no mapping to the highest synced SillyTavern version for its major.
- **Guards:** `isToolCallingSupported` opens Chat Completions tools on GPT-6 Sol and Luna for None, where SillyTavern/SillyTavern#6070 checks Min. Translate a SillyTavern effort condition when a merge brings one in.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-version-map.test.js`, `tests/upstream-provider-parity.test.js`, `tests/reasoning-effort-none.test.js`, `tests/locale-validators.test.js`.
- **Third-party settings drawers lost their page when settings became a page list:** extensions that predate the Extensions panel append their `.inline-drawer` to `#user-settings-block-content`, and the settings split leaves that column unpainted (`buildShell`, `public/scripts/sillybunny-tabs.js`), so such a drawer measured 0×0 and showed on no settings page; the old four-tab `data-active-tab` filter it had relied on no longer existed, and `tests/topbar-extension-adoption.e2e.js:265` failed on the base `f6c1b217b` too. `public/scripts/sillybunny-settings-stray-drawers.js` moves (never clones) each direct-child drawer of that column into `#extensions_settings2`, now and through a `MutationObserver`, so the Extensions page lists it as a user-installed row; authored drawers deeper in `#UI-Customization` stay. A moved drawer keeps its open state like any drawer there, and global settings search finds its settings under Extensions. Tests: `tests/topbar-extension-adoption.e2e.js`, `tests/unit/sillybunny-settings-stray-drawers.test.js`.
- **Native controls and upstream keyboard handling:** upstream `public/scripts/keyboard.js` turns Enter on any `.interactable` into a synthetic `click()`, because upstream's `.menu_button` controls are `<div>` elements. A native `<button>` with that class clicks on Enter by itself, so one press ran its handler twice: Change Password opened two popups, and the Clear cookies alert opened and cancelled at once. A marked early return now skips native buttons, links, `summary` and button-like inputs. When you turn an upstream `<div>` control into a native element, check upstream handlers that synthesise its activation. Tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-keyboard-enter.test.js`, `tests/dialogs-accessibility.e2e.js`.
- **Fixes:** #410 (SillyBunny 1.6.4 still mapped to SillyTavern 1.16.4). The same bug came back on the next minor version; #497 fixed it with the clamp. #843 and #845 (provider parity with upstream).
- **Partial adoption:** `7b6db61d1` took SillyTavern's `src/electron/` package files (`Start.bat`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`) but not `index.js`, which `package.json` names as `main` for `electron .`. `Start.bat` also installs with `--ignore-scripts`, and the electron package downloads its binary only in its `postinstall` script, so the launcher could not start. npm audit still reported 30 advisories against its lockfile (electron 41.1.1 and extract-zip 2.0.1). SillyBunny removed `src/electron/`. SillyTavern still ships it, so a sync leaves `src/electron/**` out (`docs/upstream-touch-ledger.md`).
- **Open:** issue #713, where the Quick Image Gen manual sync fails in Docker (no `bash`, no real `node`). No fix PR found.

### Dependencies and lockfiles

- **Breaks:** `package-lock.json` and `bun.lock` drift apart, or Bun cannot read `bun.lock` and resolves again from the registry without the release-age gate.
- **Files:** `package-lock.json`, `bun.lock`, `tests/package-lock.json`, `.npmrc`, `Dockerfile`, `start.sh`, `Start.bat`, `Start-Node.bat`, `scripts/dependency-state.js`.
- **Guards:**
  - Both lockfiles ship. The Bun launchers install from `bun.lock` with `--frozen-lockfile` (`start.sh:250`, `Start.bat:141-145`), and so does the Docker image (`Dockerfile:24`). `Start-Node.bat` and CI's `npm ci` use `package-lock.json`. The launchers restore the tracked lockfiles after an install (`start.sh:306-313`, `Start.bat:168-174`).
  - When every change in the checkout is to `bun.lock`, `package-lock.json` or `package.json`, `getRepositoryStatus()` in `src/endpoints/server-admin.js` restores them with `git restore --staged --worktree`. `POST /api/server-admin/status` and `POST /api/server-admin/update` call it, so either request discards uncommitted dependency edits in the checkout that the server runs from. Commit those edits before you start a server there.
  - When a frozen Bun install fails, the launchers retry without `--frozen-lockfile` (`start.sh:275-288`, `Start.bat:162-165`). The retry resolves from the registry and does not use the committed `bun.lock`.
  - `npm audit` reads only `package-lock.json`. Check `bun.lock` with `bun audit`.
  - `min-release-age=7` in `.npmrc` applies to npm only. Bun 1.4.4 ignores it and chose an `electron-to-chromium` release from the day before. Bun reads `--minimum-release-age=<seconds>` or `minimumReleaseAge` in `bunfig.toml`. To keep the gate, update `package-lock.json` with npm, move `bun.lock` aside and run `bun pm migrate`. Without a `bun.lock`, `bun install` also migrates from `package-lock.json`.
  - Bun 1.4 writes `"lockfileVersion": 2` for a new or migrated lockfile (oven-sh/bun#31539). Bun 1.3.14, which `engines.bun` still allows, stops with `Unknown lockfile version`. Version 2 adds only parse-time checks, and Bun 1.4 keeps an existing version 1 lockfile at version 1 (oven-sh/bun#31602). After a migration, set the version back to 1 and check that frozen installs on Bun 1.4.4 and 1.3.14 leave `bun.lock` byte-identical.
  - After a change to `overrides`, Bun 1.4.4 fails a frozen install with `overrides in package.json changed since bun.lock was saved`, and the launcher retry then resolves without the gate. Regenerate both lockfiles after an `overrides` edit.
  - Keep `overrides` top-level. With a nested override such as `"vectra": { "uuid": "^11.1.1" }`, Bun 1.4.4 writes `"lockfileVersion": 3` instead of 2 (checked with `bun install --lockfile-only`), and the reset to version 1 above does not cover it. Upstream SillyTavern nests its `openai` and `whatwg-url` overrides; SillyBunny lists them at the top level.
  - `overrides` moves four packages past ranges whose releases npm audit flags and that have no fixed release: `basic-ftp` for get-uri 6 (through proxy-agent 6), `lodash-es` for chevrotain 11, `protobufjs` for onnx-proto 4 (through onnxruntime-web 1.14 in sillytavern-transformers) and `uuid` for vectra 0.2. Nothing else in the tree depends on them. Remove an override when its dependent accepts a fixed release; `npm ls <name>` shows the dependent.
  - The server never loads the installed `protobufjs`. The Node.js entry of onnxruntime-web, `dist/ort-web.node.js`, requires only Node.js built-ins and `onnxruntime-common`. Only `onnxruntime-web/lib/onnxjs/` requires `onnx-proto`, and nothing imports it.
  - The browser bundle never uses the installed `lodash-es`. `webpack.config.js` aliases `chevrotain` to its prebuilt `lib/chevrotain.mjs`, which carries its own copy of the lodash functions it uses. That copy has neither `_.template` (GHSA-r5fr-rjxr-66jc) nor `_.unset` and `_.omit` (GHSA-f23m-r3pf-42rh). Outside `tests/`, only `public/lib.js` imports chevrotain.
  - `npm audit fix` also moves packages within their ranges when that clears no advisory; on 2026-11-01 it moved zod and three ESLint packages. `npm update <names> --package-lock-only` changes only the named packages.
  - A refresh inside the `package.json` ranges can still change behaviour. For each package whose version moves, diff its code and test the app's call sites under Node.js and Bun; installs, lint and the unit suite passed with all of the breaks below.
  - `bing-translate-api` is pinned to exactly 4.1.0. From 4.2.0 it sends every request with `http2: true` through got 11 and http2-wrapper, and under Bun 1.4.4 those requests never finish, so `POST /api/translate/bing` (`src/endpoints/translate.js`) waited with no end. Under Node.js 24, 4.3.0 works. Remove the pin only after a translate call under Bun finishes with the newer release.
  - @adobe/css-tools 4.5 parses `@property`, `@scope` and other at-rules as their own node types, and nested rules inside declarations. `decodeStyleTags()` in `public/scripts/chats.js` scopes only rule selectors, so it calls `removeUnscopableCss()` (`public/scripts/sillybunny-style-tag-scope.js`), which removes node types outside the 4.4 set and rejects nested CSS. When css-tools adds a node type, the type is removed until it is added to that set with a way to scope it.
  - url-join 5.2 rewrites `&` in a path part to `?`. `src/byaf.js` builds ZIP entry names with `path.posix.join()`. The other `urlJoin()` calls join a base URL with a fixed path or an encoded value.
  - Accepted in #1016: fuse.js 7.5 returns nothing for an extended search that mixes a term and an inverse term over several keys (`king !queen`), returns every item without a score for a query of one space, and uses a new score scale; and mime-db 1.54 marks `application/octet-stream` compressible, so the `compression` middleware gzips such responses. Without `tests/.npmrc`, `npm config get` in `tests/` reported `min-release-age=null`, `ignore-scripts=false` and `allow-git=all`. `tests/.npmrc` repeats the root `.npmrc`, and `tests/npm-settings.test.js` fails when the two files differ.
  - Both lockfiles hoist `mime` 3.0.0 for `@jimp/core` and nest `mime` 1.6.0 under `send` (which calls `mime.lookup()`). A half-updated `node_modules` missing `send/node_modules/mime` crashes static file serving with `TypeError: mime.lookup is not a function`. `scripts/dependency-state.js` checks that `express` resolves `send` (and any distinct `serve-static` `send`) with a `mime` major matching `send`'s declared dependency before trusting `.sillybunny-dependencies.json`, so launchers reinstall automatically. Test: `tests/dependency-state.test.js`.
- **Fixes:** the v1.2.8 release commit `221de633c` replaced `"version": "1.2.7"` with `"1.2.8"` throughout `package-lock.json`, which also changed `@types/range-parser` and `is-callable`. Their `resolved` and `integrity` still pointed at 1.2.7 and no 1.2.8 exists, so `npm ci` installed 1.2.7, but `bun pm migrate` built 1.2.8 tarball URLs that returned 404. `scripts/bump-version.js` now edits only `version` and `packages[""].version`. #1016 pinned `bing-translate-api`, added `removeUnscopableCss()` and replaced url-join in `src/byaf.js`; tests: `tests/unit/sillybunny-style-tag-scope.test.js`, `tests/message-formatting-inline-style.e2e.js`, `tests/byaf-import.test.js`.
- **Limit:** a name@version comparison of the two lockfiles always lists the npm aliases `string-width-cjs`, `strip-ansi-cjs` and `wrap-ansi-cjs`, and `@jimp/plugin-blur`. npm satisfies the `@jimp/plugin-blur` peer of `@jimp/plugin-shadow@0.22.12` with a top-level 1.6.x copy, and Bun reuses 0.22.12.
- **Open:** npm audit flags file-type 16.5.4, which `@jimp/core` 0.22.12 uses through jimp 0.22 in sillytavern-transformers, for GHSA-5v7r-6r5c-r473. The first fixed release is file-type 21.3.1, which is ESM-only.

### Macro whitespace

- **Breaks:** a trim in the macro engine removes whitespace that a template asks for with `{{newline}}`, `{{space}}` or `{{noop}}` (SillyTavern/SillyTavern#5673 and SillyTavern/SillyTavern#5674).
- **Cause:** the engine trims text after it resolves the macros in it. `{{if}}` trims its resolved branch, scoped macros trim their content, and variable shorthand values (`{{.x = ...}}`) are trimmed. The legacy `{{trim}}` post-processor runs after `{{noop}}` is already an empty string, so `{{noop}}` does not stop it as it did in the legacy engine (`public/scripts/macros.js`).
- **Files:** `public/scripts/macros/engine/MacroCstWalker.js`, `public/scripts/macros/engine/MacroEngine.js`, `public/scripts/macros/definitions/core-macros.js`.
- **Guards:** `public/scripts/macros/sillybunny-whitespace-markers.js`. The walker wraps `{{newline}}` and `{{space}}` output in U+FDD0 and `{{noop}}` output in U+FDD1, and removes them after each trim. `MacroEngine.evaluate()` removes them before it returns, except for the `{{if}}` branch, which `resolve(text, { keepWhitespaceMarkers: true })` returns with markers for the trim in the `{{if}}` handler. Only the `core:` post-processors see the markers.
- **Tests:** `tests/sillybunny-macro-whitespace.test.js` (runs the real engine in Jest), `tests/frontend/sillybunny-macro-whitespace.e2e.js`.
- **Limit:** `{{trim}}` still removes `{{newline}}` output next to it, as the legacy engine did. Put `{{noop}}` between them to keep the newline.
- **Limit:** prompt text that already contains U+FDD0 or U+FDD1 loses those characters.

### Client addresses behind proxies

- **Breaks:** the whitelist, a rate limit or `requireHttps` decides on a forwarded value that no trusted proxy set.
- **Cause:** a reverse proxy adds the address it received the request from at the right end of `X-Forwarded-For`, and it can pass the other forwarded headers on from the client unchanged. The server cannot tell which headers its proxy set.
- **Files:** `src/express-common.js`, `src/middleware/whitelist.js`, `src/middleware/requireHttps.js`, `src/middleware/basicAuth.js`, `src/middleware/hostWhitelist.js`.
- **Guards:**
